Locating the attempt dir from `runOneCell` needed its own small lookup rather than reusing `SessionResult.RawPath`: `ClaudeHarness` writes a session's raw stream to `req.RawDir` and stamps that path onto `RawPath`, but `FakeHarness` (`harness_fake.go`) never touches `req.RawDir` at all — it reads straight from its own committed `StreamsDir` and stamps THAT path onto `RawPath` instead, so deriving the attempt dir from a session's own `RawPath` would have pointed persistence at committed testdata fixtures for every `-harness fake` cell (test and dry-cell runs alike) rather than the real raw dir. `allocateRawAttemptDir` creates the attempt-`<k>` directory unconditionally, before dispatching to either harness, so `scenariobench.LatestAttemptDir(cellRawBase)` (new, `rescore.go`, alongside `LatestCompleteAttempt`) — the highest-numbered `attempt-<k>` subdirectory currently on disk, regardless of whether anything was ever written into it — names the right directory for both harnesses with no change to `RunSessions`' own signature (which ~25 call sites across this package's tests share) and no reliance on stream-file content at all.

Failing-first test (`cmd/scenariobench/raw_artifacts_test.go`, `TestCmdRun_PersistsRawAgentDiffEvenWhenCellIsLost`): a real local git fixture subject, `-harness fake` replaying a stream whose own tool_result names a path outside every safe dir (the same `/pushclone/` shape `TestCmdRun_RescoreLost_GenuineLeakStaysLost` uses) — the LIVE `run` path, never `-rescore-lost` — produces a LOST cell (`sandbox_escape`, exit 3, nothing stored), and `agent.diff` plus `cell-artifacts.json` are still found under the raw attempt dir; confirmed to fail on the pre-fix code (`no such file or directory`) before the fix made it pass. `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_RematerializeAcceptsTheRawCopyUnchanged` is the round-trip proof for O76's own consumer: a live `PrepareSubject`, a real edit, `captureAgentDiff` + `persistRawCellArtifacts` exactly as `runOneCell` calls them, the worktree destroyed (simulating the cleanup that lost guards-pilot-7's own cells), then `scenariobench.RematerializeCellWorktree` reproduces the tree byte for byte — with NO change to `RematerializeCellWorktree`/`ApplyStoredAgentDiff`/`CellRawAgentDiffPath`/`CellRawUntrackedSnapshotDir` at all, since this fix targets those exact existing paths. Plus `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_CopiesAgentDiffAndCellArtifacts`, `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_CapturesUntrackedBinaryFile`, `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_SkipsOversizedBinaryFile` (the documented cap, and its `untracked-snapshot.json` skip entry), `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_EmptyAttemptDirIsANoOp`; `TestLatestAttemptDir_PicksHighestNumbered`, `TestLatestAttemptDir_EmptyAttemptDirStillCounts` (the FakeHarness shape — an attempt dir with nothing written into it still counts), `TestLatestAttemptDir_MissingDirIsFalse` (`internal/scenariobench/rescore_test.go`) — all confirmed red before the fix.

Rule: an artifact meant to survive a shared, reset-prone directory needs its OWN durable home from the moment it is first written, not a "capture it again once a rescue tool needs it" plan — O76 built the rescue tool and only THEN discovered, against real production data, that nothing had ever been left for it to rescue. And when a per-session path (`RawPath`) is stamped differently by different `Harness` implementations for reasons specific to each (a real harness's own written artifact vs. a fake harness's own fixture source), do not read a directory out of it for a purpose neither implementation's own doc comment promises — recompute the shared, harness-independent fact (the attempt dir every `RunSessions` call allocates up front, regardless of harness) instead.

### O78 — The evidence was already in the payload; two bench readings found the model reasoning past it anyway, so wave L1 made the direct answer the first thing on the page (2026-09-17)

`docs/bench/readings/vs-pilot-6-second-reading.md` and `vs-pilot-5` name the same shape twice. H1-T05: `find_callers(RenderMarkdown, depth=2)` returned the direct caller correctly — first in `callers`, depth 1, unambiguous — and the model's final answer named a caller three hops out instead; the correct name appeared only in an intermediate reasoning aside, never the stated conclusion, and the lexical scorer matched the aside (`vs-pilot-6`'s own `scorer_false_pass` void). H1-T07: `search` hit `ADR.md:591` — the right line — and the answer cited a different document. Both tools were correct. Both payloads were complete, budgeted, and depth-labeled. The agent read past the right row anyway, because nothing in the response said "this one" — a JSON object built for completeness has no notion of what a reader scans first.

Fix, deterministic and additive (`internal/mcp/summary.go`, new): every promoted-verb response now leads with a `summary` field — one sentence, ≤200 chars, naming the direct answer in the question's own terms, built from the SAME sorted/post-budget data the rest of the body carries (never a wider pre-elision set, so it can never claim a row the body does not actually return). `find_callers`, `references`, `blast_radius`, `rename_impact`, `safe_delete_check`, `test_impact`, `localize`, `search`, and `obligations` all gained one. The mechanical problem BA-1/RC-19's existing `elided`/`elision` fields never had to solve: every handler here returns `map[string]any`, and `encoding/json` always sorts a map's keys alphabetically — adding a `"summary"` entry to the map would not make it FIRST, it would sort wherever `"s"` falls (after `resolution`, before `top_packages`, for find_callers). `summaryEnvelope.MarshalJSON` splices `summary` in ahead of the body's own encoding instead of trying to reorder the map, so the fix needed no handler rewritten to a struct and no existing field touched. find_callers' own summary is built from `callers[:len(wireCallers)]` — the depth-1 prefix the wire actually returns, post-budget — never from `transitive_callers`, so a depth-2+ caller can never be named "direct": the exact defect H1-T05 found.

Tests: one summary-content assertion per verb (`internal/mcp/summary_test.go`), five against the real fauxtown fixture fleet ingested through the connector+extractor+fusion pipeline (`indexbench.NewFixtureServer` — the same construction `internal/indexbench/testdata/l1-replay.json`'s golden entries replay against), four against this package's existing lightweight fakes (`richFakeBackend`, the fauxtown `safe_delete_check` fixture, `newSearchServer`). `TestFindCallersSummary_NamesDirectNeverDepth2` reproduces H1-T05's exact shape using the existing `graphBackend` fixture (Target's direct callers Direct1/Direct2; Deep calls Direct1 at depth 2; TestDeep calls Deep at depth 3) and asserts the summary's direct-callers clause never contains "Deep". `TestPayloadReplay` (`internal/indexbench`) regenerated the L1 golden: median payload growth 6.8%, but three tiny `obligations` fixtures (547-885 bytes) and one `find_callers` fixture (389→470 bytes) grew 21-28% — the fixture baseline is small enough that even a minimal one-sentence direct answer cannot stay under a 10% guideline built for a realistic multi-KB payload; `references` (+8.7%), `localize` (+9.9%) and `blast_radius` (+4.8%) all clear it. Shortened what was reasonably compressible (`obligations`' rule-head cap 100→70 chars) and left the rest as specified rather than gut the summary's content to chase the last few points on an unusually small fixture.

Rule: a correct, complete, well-labeled payload is not the same claim as "the agent will read the right part of it" — when a bench reading shows the model finding the right evidence and stating the wrong conclusion anyway, the fix is not more data or a stronger label buried at position N, it is putting the direct answer where a reader who stops after the first sentence still gets it right. And a growth-budget guideline stated as a percentage of payload size is a fleet-realistic number, not a promise that holds against a fixture built to be as small as possible on purpose.

### O79 — Java was already extracted, not merely classified; Kotlin genuinely had nothing — wave P2 closed Kotlin from scratch and closed Java's field/import gap without moving it off CGO (2026-09-17)

The Long Code Arena bench run (`docs/bench/readings/lca-run-1-reading.md`) had 12 Kotlin and 8 Java tasks of 40; O69 (wave P1) disclosed Kotlin's missing extractor but left Java's status unverified — only four tree-sitter grammar `.wasm` blobs shipped (`internal/extractor/tsengine/grammars`: javascript/python/ruby/typescript), yet `.java` was mapped to `KindJavaClass` in `internal/connector`, so it was unclear whether that was a real extractor or just a classification stub. Investigated first, per the assignment: reading `internal/extractor/javaextract.go` and `internal/fusion/pipeline.go` showed Java was already fully wired — `extractor.NewJavaExtractor()` (CGO tree-sitter bindings, `github.com/tree-sitter/go-tree-sitter` + `tree-sitter-java/bindings/go`, ADR-013) has been registered in `fusion.New` at `p.exts[".java"]` since before this wave, with its own golden-fixture tests (`javaextract_test.go`) covering classes/interfaces/enums/records/methods/constructors and same-file call edges. It was never "only classified" — but it WAS incomplete: no field declarations, no import declarations, the exact two constructs the task's own scope named for both languages. Kotlin, by contrast, had genuinely nothing: no grammar blob, no extractor file, no registry entry anywhere.

Given that split, wave P2 deliberately did NOT build `java.wasm` or a second, wazero-based Java extractor even though the task brief asked for one "the same way" as Kotlin's — ADR-021 §4 already names moving the CGO incumbents (Java among the nine) to the wazero engine as XT-6b, "a separate, not-yet-triggered slice" (ADR.md, `docs/intent/futures.md` F1), and building it now would pre-build a documented future the project-intent rules say not to pre-build. Instead: (1) **Kotlin**, genuinely new, went through the wazero path ADR-021 §4 reserves for new-language breadth — `internal/extractor/tsengine/grammars/kotlin.wasm` built by the existing `build.sh` recipe (extended, not replaced) from `fwcd/tree-sitter-kotlin` tag `0.3.8` (commit `e1a2d5ad1f61f5740677183cd4125bb071cd2f30`, not an official tree-sitter-org grammar), same `zig 0.16.0`/`wasm-opt 129` toolchain that produced the original four blobs, sha256 `750a499adfc0a1339abebe5da80154eb6680c5732a3a5fdceaaace5053def3c3` (2,488,435 bytes — the largest blob of the five). fwcd/tree-sitter-kotlin uses NO `field()` names anywhere in its grammar (verified against `grammar.js` directly, not assumed), so `internal/extractor/tsqueries/kotlin.scm` locates every name by node kind and anonymous-keyword position instead (`(class_declaration "interface" (type_identifier) @name)` distinguishes interfaces from classes by which literal token is present; `(source_file (property_declaration …))` restricts properties to top-level by requiring a direct-child structural path, mirroring `python.scm`'s module-var constraint) — this was worked out by fetching the grammar source and reading `grammar.js` line by line before writing a single query pattern, then verified against a real parse via a throwaway debug test (deleted before the final commit) rather than guessed and iterated blind. `tsRegistry["kotlin"]` (`internal/extractor/tsextract.go`) is pure data (kinds map + variant table), no new Go walking logic — exactly ADR-021 §4's "adding a language = grammar blob + queries + fixtures" promise, now proven true for a language with zero incumbent to crib from. (2) **Java** stayed on CGO and gained `KindJavaField`/`KindJavaImport`: `field_declaration`'s `_variable_declarator_list` repeats the SAME field name (`declarator`) across multiple `variable_declarator` children when a line declares more than one field (`int a, b;`), so `ChildByFieldName` — which only ever returns the first match for a repeated field — silently would have dropped every field after the first; fixed by walking named children by KIND instead. `import_declaration`'s `$._name` is unfielded (unlike everywhere else in this grammar), so the import path is read as `NamedChild(0)` directly — a wildcard import's `*` is a separate later sibling (`asterisk` node) and is dropped, matching Kotlin's own wildcard-import handling for consistency across the two languages this wave touched. `ExtractorEpoch` bumped 6→7 (both changes: a brand-new extractor for `.kt`/`.kts`, and a coverage change to what the existing `.java` extractor emits) so every already-indexed Java file's stored observations — missing fields and imports — get pulled onto epoch 7 on the next ingest rather than staying silently incomplete.

Proven live on two real subjects (shallow clones into scratch, `bin/fathom ingest --store <scratch>.sqlite`, deleted after — never the shared daemon/store): `bumble-tech/appyx` (Kotlin, the same repo O69's finding cited, 549 files, 4.4MB) — 6,725 symbols (4,129 imports, 1,259 functions, 578 properties, 464 classes, 122 objects, 62 interfaces) where O69 measured ~165; `jhy/jsoup` (Java, 313 files, 4.7MB, picked over `junit-team/junit5` for size) — 7,559 symbols including 806 fields and 1,912 imports that did not exist under epoch 6, alongside 4,418 methods/314 classes/14 interfaces/12 enums carried over from the pre-existing extractor. Tests: `internal/extractor/tsextract_test.go` (`TestTSExtractor_Kotlin*` — basic construct coverage, top-level-only property scoping, nested-class chaining via `.`, the `.kts` extension sharing the same grammar, determinism); `internal/extractor/javaextract_test.go` (`TestJavaExtractor_FieldsAndImports` — plain/wildcard/static imports, a single field, and the multi-declarator "two fields from one declaration" regression); `internal/extractor/tsengine/tsengine_test.go`'s `TestGrammars`; the connector/fusion/CLI-level `TestUnsupportedLanguageForPath_*`/`TestLanguageForPath_*`/`TestIngest_*UnsupportedLanguage*` suites all updated to use Swift (still genuinely unsupported) as their disclosure fixture instead of Kotlin, plus new `TestIngest_KotlinExtractsRealSymbols`/`TestIngestReportShowsKotlinSymbols` proving the closure at the fusion and CLI layers respectively. `go build ./...`, `gofmt`, `go vet`, and `go test -race` all green across `internal/extractor/...`, `internal/connector/...`, `internal/fusion/...`, and the `cmd/fathom` `Ingest|Plans|NoOrg` subset (the one pre-existing failure, `TestTypedEdges_PerformanceGate`, reproduces identically on the unmodified base commit — a machine-load-sensitive perf gate, unrelated to this wave).

Rule: "no extractor" and "no COMPLETE extractor" are different findings and need different investigations before either is assumed — Java's own connector entry looked exactly like Kotlin's from the outside (an extension mapped to a `SourceKind`), but reading the actual extractor file settled which one it was in minutes, and conflating them would have meant either building a redundant second Java extractor or, worse, skipping Java's real gap (fields, imports) because "it already has an extractor" sounded like "it's done". And a documented not-yet-triggered future (XT-6b: migrate every CGO incumbent to wazero) is a reason to LEAVE a working extractor exactly where it is, layer-appropriately extended, not a reason to invent work matching the shape of a task brief written before the investigation had an answer.

### O82 — ingest's own response now discloses a typed pass with nothing to trust, a hook can no longer re-point a service, and `obligations` accepts `path` as `file`'s alias (2026-09-17, wave P3)

Four defects filed from the 2026-09-16 push-clone incident (see this file's earlier note and `docs/guides/fleet-index.md`): the daemon answered `{"ok":true}` for a push clone whose typed-edge enrichment produced ZERO typed edges (go.work named sibling checkouts that existed only beside the operator's own checkout), so `test_impact`/`verify test_reaches` silently read "no test reaches it" and a push was refused with nothing in the ingest that ran it to explain why; the same clone's lefthook post-commit `fathom-refresh` step re-registered service "fathom" at the clone's own path.

(1) **Ingest honesty.** `worktree.CheckReroot`/BP-35's own `TypedEdgeStatus` machinery (Sep 12) already computed the right verdict for a "packages seen but 0 processed" typed pass, but nothing on the `ingest` tool's OWN response ever read it — a caller had to make a SEPARATE `staleness_report`/`doctor` call to discover the gap, and the incident shows an agent does not always make that call before pushing. `fusion.Report` (`internal/fusion/pipeline.go`) now carries `TypedPackagesSeen`/`TypedFailed`/`TypedFailReason`/`TypedFallback`, populated from the SAME `Pipeline.TypedEdgeStatus(svc.ID)` staleness_report/doctor already read — one source of truth. `fathom ingest` (text and `--json`, `cmd/fathom/ingest.go` + `ingest_json.go`) and the MCP `ingest` tool (`internal/mcp/extra_tools.go`) now print/carry `typed_edges:{packages_seen, packages_processed, edges, failed, error?, fallback?}` whenever a typed pass was ATTEMPTED (not the pre-O82 "non-zero packages/failures only" condition, which is exactly what let a Go tree with zero typed edges print/carry nothing at all) — `failed:true` flips `--json`'s `outcome` to `degraded`/exit 1, the text path's own exit code, and the MCP tool's top-level `ok` to **false**, while the syntax index itself is kept (never rolled back — "say it plainly", never "refuse"). `list_services` gained the same per-service `typed_edges` row (previously absent entirely) so `known:false`/`failed:true` reads identically everywhere. `fathom doctor`'s existing `typed_edges` row (BP-36) needed no change: it already reads `staleness_report`, which reads the same underlying status map.

(2) **GOWORK fallback.** `TypedEdgeEnv` (BP-35, Sep 12) already forces `GOWORK=off` unconditionally on every typed-edge subprocess, so a go.work naming a missing/foreign sibling cannot break enrichment for a tree ingested elsewhere — confirmed with a regression test (a temp module under a go.work whose second `use` names a path that does not exist: `TestEnrichTypedEdges_GoWorkMissingSiblingSurvives`). Added the disclosure half: `TypedEdgeReport.Fallback`/`TypedEdgeStatus.Fallback` name `"GOWORK=off"` whenever an ambient workspace was discoverable and therefore overridden (`detectWorkspaceOverride`, `internal/fusion/typedenv.go` — a best-effort `go env GOWORK` probe run WITHOUT the override, from the repo's own directory, so upward auto-discovery or a global `go env -w GOWORK=…` pin both surface). The probe needed its own environment repair (`repairedGoEnv`, factored out of `TypedEdgeEnv`): `go env GOWORK` itself fails outright under the same broken-GOPATH shape BP-35 exists to fix (`go: GOPATH entry is relative; must be absolute path: "$HOME/go"`), which would have made detection silently report "no override" on exactly the daemon environments most likely to need one. Writing the test surfaced a wave-setup artifact worth recording: this repo's own gate commands `export GOWORK=off` around every `go` invocation, which — since `os.Environ()` is inherited wholesale — makes every fixture's go.work invisible to `detectWorkspaceOverride` unless the test explicitly `t.Setenv("GOWORK", "")` first; a persistent `go env -w GOWORK=…` pin is a DIFFERENT thing from a shell-exported one (neither was present as a stray global on the machine this wave ran on — checked `~/Library/Application Support/go/env` directly rather than assumed).

(3) **A hook must never re-point a service.** `worktree.CheckReroot` (O22/PC-33) only refuses re-rooting a service onto a LINKED git worktree (`git worktree add`) of its own recorded root; a plain `git clone` elsewhere — a separate physical checkout, exactly the push-clone shape — sails straight through it, since it shares no git-common-dir and carries no `.claude/worktrees/` marker. The daemon-side `ingest` handler (`internal/mcp/extra_tools.go`) now refuses ANY `path` that differs (canonicalized) from an EXISTING service's recorded root unless `remap:true` is also passed — the deliberate-move escape hatch, parallel to the store-direct `fathom remap` verb, which never touches the daemon. The `post-commit-hook` verb (`cmd/fathom/postcommithook.go`) now asks `list_services` for the registered root BEFORE calling `ingest` and skips with a plain message on a mismatch, instead of stumbling into the daemon's own refusal and printing a confusing "daemon refresh failed … falling back" line for what is really "this hook is running inside the wrong checkout" — fail-open on enumeration failure (daemon down or too old), leaving the `ingest` call's own refusal as the backstop. `TestPostCommitHookPrefersDaemon`'s call-count assertion moved from exactly-1 to exactly-2 (the new `list_services` pre-check, then `ingest`) — a deliberate, documented behavior change, not a regression.

(4) **`obligations` `path`/`file` alias.** Calling the `obligations` MCP tool with `path` instead of `file` silently fell back to root-scope rules only (`rootScope := p.File == ""` read true because the argument landed in a field the handler never read) — hit live by the operator today. `internal/mcp/semantic_tools.go` now accepts `path` as an alias, `file` winning when both are given; documented in the tool schema/description, `docs/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/guides/fleet-index.md`.

Failing-first tests throughout, one commit per defect: `internal/mcp/semantic_tools_test.go` (`TestObligationsTool_PathAliasForFile`, `TestObligationsTool_FileWinsOverPath`); `internal/mcp/ingest_remap_test.go` (`TestIngestRefusesPlainCloneReroot` + 3 subtests), `cmd/fathom/postcommithook_remap_test.go` (`TestPostCommitHookSkipsWhenRootIsNotRegistered`, `TestPostCommitHookRefreshesWhenRootMatchesRegistered`); `internal/fusion/typedcalls_goworkfallback_test.go` (`TestEnrichTypedEdges_GoWorkMissingSiblingSurvives`, `TestEnrichTypedEdges_GoWorkFallbackDisclosed`, `TestEnrichTypedEdges_NoGoWorkNoFallback`); `internal/fusion/typedenv_test.go` (`TestIngestReport_CarriesTypedEdgeDisclosure`, `TestIngestReport_DisclosesTypedFailure`), `internal/mcp/platform_tools_test.go` (`TestListServices_IncludesTypedEdgesOutcome`, `TestListServices_OmitsTypedEdgesWhenUnknown`), `internal/mcp/ingest_typed_disclosure_test.go` (`TestIngestTool_TypedEdgesDisclosedOnSuccess`, `TestIngestTool_TypedEdgesFailureMarksOkFalse`, `TestIngestTool_TypedEdgesOmittedWhenUnknown`), `cmd/fathom/json_envelopes_test.go` + `cmd/fathom/ingest_typed_disclosure_test.go` (CLI text/`--json` disclosure and degraded-outcome cases).

Rule: a fact an instrument already computes correctly in-process (BP-35's `TypedEdgeStatus`, Sep 12) is not "surfaced" until the SPECIFIC call site that would have prevented the incident carries it — `staleness_report`/`doctor` answering the question honestly for five days did not stop the push-clone incident, because nothing made an agent ask them before pushing; the fix was never a new fact, only wiring the existing one onto the response an agent was already going to read. And a hook's own refusal-avoidance check (list_services before ingest) is not redundant with the daemon's own refusal (remap:true) even though both enforce the same invariant — the hook's version produces a clear, on-topic message at the point of the mistake; the daemon's is the backstop for every OTHER caller.

### O80 — Wave O4: an ancestor-only hit no longer blocks cross-tier promotion (T02 now passes; T13's own farther-side blank stays a proven miss), the bench's compile step was silently accepting whatever the layer's default budget stopped at, and doctor dropped corpus_grew off the wire (2026-09-17)

Three follow-ups from the readings behind ADR-037's wave O2/O3 addenda, worked as one wave.

**(1) Ranking.** O75 left H1-T02 and H1-T13 as proven, structural misses. Re-reading the actual mechanism: `crossTierCloser`'s promotion cap (`internal/semantic/governingscope.go`) required the nearer-tier candidate to be a TOTAL lexical blank (`relevance == 0`) before a farther, strong candidate could leapfrog it — but a bare ancestor-directory token ("internal", "cmd", "docs") registers as a nonzero hit under the existing scoring (`ancestorWeight`, `internal/semantic/relevance.go`) purely because every sibling file under that guide shares it, never because the guide actually names the edited file. H1-T02 (`internal/core/model.go` vs. ADR-021) was exactly this: internal/CLAUDE.md's other same-tier sections each carried only a weak "internal" mention, which the old `relevance == 0` test read as "not blank" and refused to promote past. Fixed by narrowing the cap's OWN bar from "the nearer candidate has ANY hit" to "the nearer candidate is itself STRONG" (`relevanceStrength`'s own packageWeight-or-above bit — package path, stem, or a caller-resolved symbol/behaviour token; never ancestor or extension weight) — `crossTierCloser`'s signature dropped the now-unused relevance floats entirely, taking only the two `strong` bools. Re-running `TestH1GovernanceTasks_RawFallbackOffline`: H1-T02 now passes as a hard gate. H1-T13 does not, and the reason is different from H1-T02's — ADR-014's own section is the FARTHER-tier candidate here and carries ZERO relevance itself (no hit on "model"/"internal"/"core", no caller-supplied symbol); promotion only ever promotes a farther side that is ITSELF strong, and this narrowing does nothing for a farther side with nothing to promote. Confirmed a real, separate finding along the way: re-running the fix against the REAL corpus surfaced that ADR-037's own multi-wave addendum (which documents this very ranking mechanism using its target tokens — "Edge/EdgeKind", "core", "model" — verbatim) also crosses the "strong" bar on ordinary domain vocabulary and, being the longest, most discursive entry in ADR.md, out-scores genuinely on-topic sections on raw hit-weighted magnitude within the same tier — a same-tier (wave O2) relevance-ranking limitation, not this wave's cross-tier one, and one this repo's own corpus growth keeps widening every time a wave documents a gap using the gap's own tokens. Proven in isolation, free of that real-corpus confound: `TestCrossTierCloser_AncestorOnlyHitOnNearerDoesNotBlockPromotion` / `TestCrossTierCloser_StrongHitOnNearerBlocksPromotion` (unit level) and `TestRawFallbackRules_CrossTierPromotion_AncestorOnlyHitDoesNotBlockPromotion` / `TestRawFallbackRules_CrossTierPromotion_StrongHitOnNestedGuideBlocksPromotion` (`RawFallbackRules` level, synthetic fixtures) all pass, and `TestGoverningRuleOracle_Deterministic`/`TestPayloadReplay` (`internal/indexbench`) stayed byte-identical.

**(2) Compile budget as instrument truth.** vs-pilot-5's second reading found the bench's own subject-preparation compile step (`internal/scenariobench/subject.go`'s `compileLayers`, `fathom compile -layer obligations -dry-run=false`) accepting whatever the layer's DEFAULT `max_tokens_per_run` (layer.cue, 200000) stopped at — 14/60 sections on the reading that motivated ADR-037 — with no flag to ask for the whole corpus in one run and nothing on the cell recording that the layer was incomplete at all. `fathom compile` gained `--max-tokens-per-run`/`--max-usd-per-run` (`cmd/fathom/compile.go`, direct `--store` mode only, mirroring `--force`/`--continue`'s own guard-flag pattern): empty leaves the layer's own budget untouched, `"all"` maps to the unlimited sentinel (0, or 0.0 — `enforceBudgets`' `MaxUSDPerRun` check gained the same `>0`-means-bounded convention `MaxTokensPerRun` already had; previously an explicit 0 there meant "refuse everything", the opposite of every other budget field in that function), a plain number overrides directly. Threaded via a new `semantic.BudgetOverride` (`Apply` returns a new `LayerDef`, never mutating the caller's own) and `pkg/client.Client.CompileLayerWithBudget`, additive beside the existing `CompileLayer`/`CompileLayerWithEscape`/`CompileLayerResume`. `compileLayers` now always passes `-max-tokens-per-run=all`. `CompileCost` (subject.go) and `CompileCostReading` (types.go) both gained `SectionsTotal`/`SectionsCompiled`/`BudgetStopped`, parsed off `fathom compile`'s own prose stdout (pulled into a new pure `parseCompileOutput` for testability) exactly like the existing `tokens_in`/`cost_usd` lines already were. `AggregateRowReadings` (cell.go) now actually sets the row-level `r.CompileCost` (previously never assigned at all, despite `CompileCostUSD` being summed) — the first NON-cached cell's reading, by ascending trial, falling back to the earliest cached one only when every cell in the row was cached, so a row never surfaces a legitimately-all-zero cached reading ahead of a real, populated one sitting right next to it. `RenderScenarioLedger` (ledger.go) folds `"layer N/M compiled"` (plus `" (budget_stopped)"` when applicable) into the notes column for a wired row that compiled a layer — no new table column, so every existing snapshot expectation of the header/column count survives untouched.

**(3) Doctor's own corpus_grew.** `fathom doctor`'s `obligations_served` check already printed a `PARTIALLY compiled (%d/%d sections, budget_stopped=%v)` WARN (O54 deliverable 5) — but its own wire-payload mirror, `doctorLayerCoverage` (cmd/fathom/doctor.go), had no field for `corpus_grew` at all, so `semantic.LayerCoverage`'s own `corpus_grew,omitempty` (already on the wire — `internal/mcp/semantic_tools.go` serializes the whole `LayerCoverage` struct verbatim) silently vanished on decode into an unknown JSON field. The operator's own daemon was observed at 42/66 compiled with a real `corpus_grew: 14` and the WARN said nothing about it — understating how much of the gap is "the corpus outgrew the last compile" (14 sections no compile run has EVER seen) versus "this run's own budget stopped it early" (a stale digest on an already-seen section, a different failure mode with a different remedy). Fixed: `CorpusGrew int` added to the mirror struct, and the WARN reason now appends `, corpus_grew=%d` whenever it is nonzero (omitted entirely when zero, so a budget-stopped run with NO corpus growth is never misdescribed as having outgrown anything) — the remedy command (`fathom compile --service %s --layer obligations --dry-run=false --continue`) was already correct and unchanged. Pinned with the operator's own exact numbers (`TestCheckObligationsServed`'s new "partially compiled layer names corpus_grew when the corpus has outgrown the last compile" / "...omits corpus_grew when the corpus has not grown" subtests).

Rule: a wave that documents its own ranking bug using the exact tokens that bug is about will make the DOCUMENTING text itself the loudest false-positive candidate the next time someone re-runs the same reproduction — corpus self-reference is a real, measurable cost of this repo's own "narrate the finding in ADR.md" discipline, not a hypothetical one, and it compounds with each addendum. And a wire field that exists, serializes, and gets silently dropped by ONE reader's own hand-copied mirror struct is a shape worth grepping for on principle whenever a new field is added anywhere near an MCP payload doctor.go also decodes — the type system gives no warning for an extra JSON key nobody asked to keep.

### O83 — Governance now bites at the edit for a checkable rule: `enforce: true` denies a PreToolUse violation instead of only advising it (ADR-038, wave G1, 2026-09-17)

guards-pilot-8's own reading (`docs/bench/readings/guards-pilot-8-second-reading.md`) found the
governing rule now reaches the wired agent before the relevant edit on 4 of 8 tasks — ADR-036/037's
own fixes working — and the wired arm violated the rule anyway on two of those four (i19, an
org-specific name landed in a new fixture; i28, a lenient DuckDB path shipped over ADR-025's loud
refusal), with one session going on to rewrite its own pinned regression test rather than honor
the rule it had just been served. Violations tied the control arm 4-for-4. `docs/design/
guarded-edits.md` §2.2 had named exactly this as its own escalation trigger since the design's
first version: "revisit only with a documented incident a warning demonstrably failed to prevent."

Fix (ADR-038): `#Guard`/`#LayerRule` gain `enforce: bool | *false`; `internal/guardspec.LoadGuards`/
`LoadLayerRules` refuse to load an enforced rule with no deterministic check behind it, naming it.
`internal/guardspec/enforce.go` adds five checks, each a pure function over one proposed edit's own
path/text: `forbidden_paths`, `checks: ["org_token","secret_literal","workspace_output",
"usecase_maintained"]` (guard), `cgo_purity` + `import_direction`/`boundary` content scans (layer
rule, over the proposed text's textually-present imports). `internal/harness.HookOutput` gains
`Deny`/`DenyReason`; Claude Code and Codex render the documented `permissionDecision:"deny"` shape
(their existing "the shape fathom never emits" golden fixture is now the shape it emits, narrowly);
Gemini refuses it. `cmd/fathom/enforce.go` wires this into `edithook_pre.go`, before the advisory
daemon round-trip, with `FATHOM_ENFORCE=off` / `[guards] enforce = false` as the escape hatch. This
repo's own `.fathom/guard.cue` turns `enforce: true` on the rules mapping to four of the eight
guards-fathom.json tasks (i2, i19, i31, i36) plus the actual raw-cp-onto-daemon-path command guard
(i16's Bash half); i1/i11 have no pre-write signal and stay gate-only, i28 is a control-flow
decision no structural check can see, i16's CLAUDE.md-prose half stays gate-only via
`task config-audit` (a content-pattern check for "advises a raw cp" risks a false positive on the
Taskfile's own legitimate `cp -f bin/fathom … .new` line).

Along the way: `LoadGuards` rejected a `forbidden_paths`-only guard as "governing nothing" — only
`scopes`/`command_scopes` were recognized governing fields — a real bug this wave's own first
`forbidden_paths` guard tripped over immediately (fixed: `forbidden_paths` now also counts).
`testdata/scenariobench/guards-m1.json`'s `.fathom/guard.cue#L43` citation for `core-no-mcp-or-cmd`
had drifted to line 93 once this wave's own new guard entries grew the file above it — the same
class of line-citation maintenance the two immediately-preceding commits on `main` already needed
(H1-T07/guards-m1's ADR-011/ADR-036 citations) and evidently a recurring cost of citing a bare line
number into a file that keeps growing above the cited line, worth a future revisit toward
heading-anchored citations into `.fathom/guard.cue` the way `ResolveCitationLine` already does for
ADR.md.

Rule: an "obligation the agent can read and still contradict" is not proof of anything until a
check exists that the tool call itself cannot get past — a served rule and an enforced rule are
different instruments, and thesis 2 (vision 29 §2) says the compiled-test half of governance was
always meant to reach the edit moment too, not just the gate.

### O84 — a plan row anchored at a bare Go struct TYPE can never pass `test_reaches`: the extractor records no call-graph edge into a composite literal (findings-loop wave 3, FL-16, 2026-09-18)

`docs/plans/findings-loop.md`'s FL-16 row was authored with `symbol: ReviewResponse` — a plain
`type ReviewResponse struct{...}`, not a func. `fathom verify` failed it with `test_reaches: no
Test* statically reaches it` even with a real, passing test (`TestReviewResponsePayload`,
`internal/analysis/review/response_test.go`) constructing the type via `review.BuildResponse(...)`
and exercising its `Payload()` method. Root cause: `test_reaches` is purely
`callgraph.TestImpact`/`TestImpactDetail` — a BFS over the typed/syntax CALL graph — and the Go
extractor never emits a call-graph edge for a composite literal (`T{...}`) or a bare type reference;
only function/method calls become edges (confirmed: no `CompositeLit` handling anywhere under
`internal/extractor`). A struct type therefore has zero possible incoming edges from ANY test, no
matter how thoroughly it is exercised — this is not a coverage gap this wave could close by writing
a better test, it is a structural blind spot in what the call graph can express. Worked around by
re-anchoring the row's `symbol:` to `BuildResponse` (the constructor function returning the type,
which the same test calls directly) rather than the type itself — the row's own acceptance intent
(the envelope exists and is built correctly) is unchanged, only the verify anchor moved to something
the index can actually reach.

Rule: a plan-authoring pass (`fathom-plan`, or a human hand-writing `{id, symbol, package}`) should
anchor a struct/interface TYPE row at a constructor or a method, never the bare type name, or
`fathom verify` will report a false "present-but-untested" for code that is genuinely tested — worth
a check in `fathom-plan`'s own scaffolding (does the named symbol's Kind admit call edges at all)
before it ever reaches an implementer.

### O85 — the agent-surface schema tax cut ~22% (60,918 → 47,480 bytes) by moving description design-history off the wire, not by touching the machine-checkable contract (2026-09-18, wave fl-wave-10)

`cmd/fathom/cost_panel_test.go`'s own measured cost (BA-7) — 60,918 bytes / 25 tools, every one
paid by every MCP-connected session before its first call — and a consumer journal citing "25
tools and 58,721 schema bytes per session" as a cost with no counterfactual were the trigger. Every
one of the 25 agent-surface tool `Description` strings was rewritten to a fixed grammar
`docs/mcp-tools.md` and `docs/adoption/claude-code.md` already documented but never enforced: one
opening sentence naming what the tool answers, at most two sentences on the result shape a caller
must know (budget/elision, tiers, `checked:false`), the "Reach for it when …" routing sentence, and
a "Required: …" line. Everything else — plan-row ids (`SB-xx`/`VB-xx`/`RC-xx`/`O`-numbers), ADR
cross-references, migration notes ("DEFAULTS changed in 0.9"), and defaults prose the argument
schema already states — moved address only, never content: `docs/mcp-tools.md`'s per-tool rows
already carried nearly all of it in full (the doc and the schema had been growing in lockstep since
founding), so the diet was mostly subtraction from the wire, not composition into the doc; the rare
genuinely-missing fact was folded into the existing row rather than a new one.

One real near-miss: the first pass cut the "this result IS the answer … a follow-up call only
helps …" stop sentence from all five tools that carry it (`find_symbol`, `find_callers`,
`symbol_context`, `verify_plan`, `obligations`) as if it were design history — it read like the
same "this result IS the answer" boilerplate the RC-19/20/21 waves left everywhere. It is not:
`agent_surface_stop_test.go`'s `TestAgentSurfaceDescriptionsStateWhenToStop` pins it as SB-4, a
measured H1-bench finding that a model kept calling follow-up tools past a definitive answer until
the sentence was added. The full pre-existing test suite (not just the new tests this wave added)
caught the cut on the first `go test ./internal/mcp/...` run; the fix keeps a short version of the
same sentence as one of each tool's two allowed result-shape sentences, satisfying both the byte
cap and the pinned test.

Two new tests: `internal/mcp/surface_arg_schema_test.go`'s `TestAgentSurfaceArgSchemaUnchanged`
diffs every agent-surface tool's `InputSchema` (descriptions stripped) against a golden captured
before the diet (`internal/mcp/testdata/agent_surface_arg_schema.golden.json`) — names, types,
enums, and required lists are byte-identical; only prose shrank. `internal/mcp/
surface_description_caps_test.go`'s `TestAgentSurfaceDescriptionsWithinCaps` pins the per-tool cap
(700 B; 1000 B for `review_diff`/`obligations`/`references`/`search`, whose composed or fleet-wide
answers genuinely have more moving parts) and a 200 B cap on every argument-property description,
recursively.

Measured: 60,918 → 47,480 bytes (25 tools unchanged, `TestAgentSurfaceDiet`'s own pin unmoved);
description + argument-description prose (the only bytes this wave could touch) fell 31,273 →
17,787, a 43% cut. `agentSurfaceSchemaByteCeiling` (`cmd/fathom/cost_panel_test.go`) re-pinned to
49,480 (measured + 2,000), its history comment replaced with a dated 2026-09-18 note. The brief's
own aspirational ≤36,000 B target was not reached and is not reachable by a description diet alone:
a per-tool byte breakdown (`internal/mcp` + `cmd/fathom` JSON marshal of a live `tools/list`) showed
a ~29.6 kB fixed floor this wave never touched and was told not to — generated `outputSchema`
(18,626 B, RP-11's machine-checkable per-tool result contract), MCP `annotations` (2,227 B, RP-10),
and argument-schema STRUCTURE (~6,700 B: property names/types/enums/required, protected by the new
golden). Hitting 36,000 would mean cutting one of those three, each a deliberate prior-wave
decision this brief scoped out.

Status: closed. Fix in `internal/mcp/*.go` (25 Description + argument-description edits),
`internal/mcp/surface_arg_schema_test.go`, `internal/mcp/surface_description_caps_test.go`,
`internal/mcp/testdata/agent_surface_arg_schema.golden.json`, `cmd/fathom/cost_panel_test.go`
(re-pinned ceiling), `CHANGELOG.md`. `docs/mcp-tools.md` needed no new facts — it already carried
what the wire cut. Impact: every session's standing schema cost drops ~13.4 kB (~22%) before any
work happens; the fixed ~29.6 kB floor (output schemas + annotations + argument-schema structure)
is now a measured, named number instead of an unexamined part of the 60,918 total, so a future wave
that wants to go lower knows exactly which of the three protected costs it would have to spend.

Rule: when a byte-cutting brief says "move design history to docs", check each cut sentence against
the test suite BEFORE trusting that it reads as design history — a sentence that looks like
boilerplate because a dozen OTHER sentences nearby really are boilerplate can still be the one load-
bearing fact a bench measured and a test pins; running the full existing suite (not just the new
tests being added) is what catches that, not a second reading of the prose.

### O86 — `fathom review --format github`'s "Changed symbols" caller listing was hard-capped at depth 1, unlike every other blast-radius surface in the repo

- **Symptom:** `fathom review --format github`'s recall on the VB-24 historical-PR corpus stayed at 5/42 (11.9%, well under the M15 row's 30% floor) even though the review engine's OWN impact dimension (`impact.DiffImpactWithTypedEdges`) has always walked the call graph unbounded — the GitHub-rendered comment's per-changed-symbol "Changed symbols" caller list never surfaced past a direct (depth-1) caller.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `internal/reviewbench` (this wave's own offline VB-24 reproduction harness), `internal/analysis/review/github.go`
  - **Expected:** a caller two hops from a changed symbol (a caller of a caller) named in the rendered review, the same way `blast_radius`/`find_callers` already report depth-2+ transitive callers by default (BP-27/BD-2).
  - **Actual:** `ChangedSymbolReviews` built its `Callers` list from a single `callgraph.CallersDetail(g, k)` call — direct callers only. `golangci-lint` #5884→#5885's fix touched `analyze` in `pkg/goanalysis/runner_loadingpackage.go`, a caller-of-a-caller of the symbol A's diff changed, in a file A's diff never touched — invisible to the pre-fix code at any tier.
  - **Repro:** `docs/bench/readings/review-recall-loop-2026-09-18.md` §"Worked example"; `internal/analysis/review/github_test.go`'s `TestChangedSymbolReviews_TransitiveCallerDepth2`.
- **Root cause:** `ChangedSymbolReviews` (VB-22, the original tracer-bullet implementation) was written before the repo's later depth-2-default convention (BP-27, `blast_radius`/`find_callers`) existed, and nothing revisited it when that convention landed — a one-off omission, not a systemic gap (the review engine's `impact` dimension and `impact.TestGap`'s affected-tests reachability were both already unbounded).
- **Fix:** `changedSymbolCallerDepth = 2` constant; `ChangedSymbolReviews` now BFS-walks to that depth, labeling each `CallerRef` with `Depth`; `CallSitesTotal`/`CallSitesUpdated` (call-site completeness) stay direct-callers-only by definition. Rendered as `[tier, depth N]`. Recall moved 5/42 → 6/42 (14.3%), 0 regressions, false findings unchanged at 0/PR.
- **Status:** closed (shipped, `internal/analysis/review/github.go`'s `changedSymbolCallerDepth`)
- **Impact:** +1 genuine strict-new hit on the VB-24 corpus (of 36/42 pairs directly re-verified); the row's 30% floor is still far off — the 5 pure misses this loop reproduces (byte-identical to the published reading's list) point at O39/O40-class selector-method/interface edge resolution as the next, unattempted, highest-value candidate.

### O88 — J2: stale compiled obligations rendered as binding rules, snippet re-read the current file at the old citation

- **Symptom:** A stale/contradicted rule rendered interleaved with binding rules (labeled only ", stale") and its inlined snippet re-read the CURRENT file at the rule's OLD citation range — closes app-kit O6, O16, O24, O26, O29, O30.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** obligations, edit-hook, edit-hook --pre, prompt-hook
  - **Fix:** `internal/semantic/serve.go` attaches a stale/contradicted rule's compile-time AnchorText; `internal/mcp/obligationsnippet.go` never re-reads the current file for such a rule, rendering AnchorText labeled "cited text at compile; current file differs" instead; every hook renderer (`cmd/fathom/stalerules.go`) moves stale rules out of the governing bullet list into one trailing "stale rules — not binding until recompiled (N)" block with one remedy line; the `obligations` MCP tool gains `stale_count`/`remedy`; ADR-038 enforcement is proven (by construction and a regression test) to never consult claim status.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestObligationsFor_StaleRuleCarriesAnchorText`, `TestObligationsTool_StaleRuleUsesAnchorTextNeverCurrentFile`, `TestObligationsTool_StaleCountAndRemedy`, `TestEditHookStaleRule_MovedToTrailingBlockWithRemedy`, `TestEditHookNoStaleRules_ByteIdenticalRendering`, `TestEvaluateGuardEnforcement_IgnoresClaimStatus`, `TestEditHookPre_EnforcedDeny_NeverConsultsStaleObligations`.
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** An agent can no longer read a drifted rule's citation as if it still supported the rule text, and always sees an explicit `fathom compile` remedy instead of a bare status label.

### O89 — J1: .mjs/.cjs/.mts/.cts never dispatched to an extractor after ingest

- **Symptom:** find_symbol never found an exported function in a new `.mjs`/`.cjs`/`.mts`/`.cts` file after a clean ingest — closes app-kit O18, O32, O33.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** ingest, find_symbol, outline
  - **Root cause:** `internal/extractor/tsextract.go`'s "javascript" TSExtractor already spanned `.mjs`/`.cjs` (missing `.mts`/`.cts`), but neither `internal/connector/connector.go`'s `extClassMap` (language classification) nor `internal/fusion/pipeline.go`'s own extractor dispatch table (`p.exts`) ever named any of the four — so the files were fingerprinted and silently dropped before reaching an extractor.
  - **Fix:** all four extensions added to `extClassMap`, `p.exts`, and `tsextract.go`'s `variantForExt`; `internal/extractor.EngineProvenance()` now claims them.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestTSExtractor_MjsExports`, `TestTSExtractor_CjsModuleExports`, `TestTSExtractor_MtsCtsRouting`, `TestLanguageForPath_KnownExtensions`, `TestLanguageRegistryParity`, `TestIngest_MjsCjsMtsCtsSymbolsIndexed` (internal/fusion, end to end through Ingest).
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** A repo whose JS/TS source uses the Node/TS module-system extensions is now actually indexed, not silently near-empty.

### O90 — J3: unbounded symbol_context/find_symbol answers for an ambiguous or hub-sized query

- **Symptom:** A bare `symbol_context Run` query returned a ~63k-token cross-service caller set; a package-like `find_symbol` query flooded context with every exported symbol — closes app-kit O12, O22, and O28's silent-empty-pack pack_context misuse.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** symbol_context, find_symbol, pack_context
  - **Fix:** `symbolnav.ResolveTiered` (a candidate-exposing sibling of resolveSymbol) lets `symbol_context` return `{ambiguous:true|scope_required:true, candidates, count, hint}` and no neighbourhood, before ever computing callers/callees/cross-service lookups, for a bare name matching >1 definition or a uniquely-resolved symbol with >50 callers; `find_symbol`'s case-insensitive tier (`caseFoldMatches`) now caps at 20 rows with the standard elision block, matching the substring tier; `pack_context` refuses a whitespace-containing `concern` argument with a params error instead of running a futile query.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestSymbolContext_AmbiguousBareName_ReturnsCandidatesNoNeighbourhood`, `TestSymbolContext_HubCallerCapRefusesNeighbourhood`, `TestSymbolContext_HubCallerCap_500CallersUnder16KB`, `TestFindSymbolCaseInsensitive_PackageMatchCapsAt20`, `TestPackContext_SentenceConcernRefused`.
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** Neither tool can flood an agent's context from a single ambiguous or hub-shaped query; the byte budget is structural, not a post-hoc trim.

### O91 — J5: instrument --adoption/research report/flywheel disagreed about which call log they read

- **Symptom:** One instrument read a scratch clone's log while another reported the named repo's log absent, run moments apart against the same repo — closes app-kit O25, and the identity half of O15/O17/O19.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** instrument --adoption, research report, flywheel, doctor
  - **Fix:** `cmd/fathom/calllogidentity.go`'s `resolveServiceCallLog` is the one shared algorithm (the named service's recorded corpus path -> that root's own `.fathom/config.toml [daemon].call_log` -> the daemon's literal default for that root); all three instruments now call it when `--service` is given (flywheel gained the flag); `doctor` gains an `instrument_identity` row proving the three answers agree.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestResolveServiceCallLog` (the shared table), `TestConfiguredCallLogPathAt`, `TestCallLogIdentityLine`, `TestCheckInstrumentIdentity_ResolvedServicePasses`.
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** Naming `--service` now resolves the SAME call log everywhere; doctor catches a future regression in any one caller.
- **Deviation:** did not rewrite every downstream numeric field's zero-vs-unknown rendering across all three report formats — only the top-level identity line. flywheel's own IDLE/OPEN evidence line (already honest about absent-vs-unreadable) was left as-is rather than force-fit into the new headline format.

### O92 — J6: layers install rejected the documented flag order; fleet reconcile's pre-refresh-digest defect could not be reproduced

- **Symptom:** `fathom layers install <dir> --root DIR` (the documented order) was refused as "requires exactly one pack directory" — closes app-kit O8. Investigated O9 (fleet reconcile recording pre-refresh skill digests) but could not reproduce it against this codebase.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** layers install, fleet reconcile
  - **Fix:** `cmd/fathom/flagreorder.go`'s `reorderFlagsFirst` lets `layers install` accept its flags in either position relative to the positional pack directory.
  - **O9 finding:** traced `ApplyWiring` -> `Ledger.Record` -> `DriftSkills` (internal/wiring/apply.go, ledger.go, drift.go): `Ledger.Record` already upserts by ID, and the owned-file digest recorded in the same apply call is the digest of the exact bytes written moments later in the same loop iteration. Added the missing regression assertion (immediate zero-drift after reconciling a refreshed skill file) rather than a speculative code change; it passes against current code.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestReorderFlagsFirst`, `TestLayers_Install_FlagsAfterPositionalPath`, `TestLayers_Install_BoolFlagAfterPositionalPath`, `TestLayers_PrintUsage_LayersInstallLinePinned`, `TestReconcileSkills` (extended).
- **Status:** closed (O8); O9 not reproduced — regression test added, no code change
- **Impact:** `layers install` no longer punishes the documented invocation order; a future O9-shaped regression in the wiring ledger is now caught immediately.

### O93 — SU-8: the vector/hybrid-search path was dead code from the daemon's perspective

- **Symptom:** `internal/query`'s `WithVector`/`SymbolIndexer` hybrid-ranking option existed and was fully implemented, but nothing in the daemon, MCP server, or CLI ever wired an indexer in — `Query.SemanticWt` had no path to a nonzero value outside a direct Go caller building its own `SymbolIndexer` (vision 31 §2 inventory item 5).
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** grep/find_callers over `internal/query`, `internal/modulehost`, `cmd/fathom`, `internal/mcp`, `pkg/client`
  - **Root cause:** the hybrid path was built as an `Option` (`query.WithVector`) but never plumbed past `internal/query` itself — the one production call site outside tests, `internal/modulehost/federated.go`'s `query.New(m.Store)`, never passed it, and no CLI flag or MCP tool parameter ever set `SemanticWt`.
  - **Fix:** ADR-041 decision 2 (vision 31 §3 D5/D7) — `internal/vector`, `WithVector`, `SymbolIndexer`, `hybridRank`'s vector branch, and `core.Query.SemanticWt` (its only reader) deleted; fathom's own import of agentic-kit's embedding client removed; `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/design/scope-and-purge.md`, `docs/design/semantic-layers.md`, and `docs/intent/futures.md` scrubbed so "semantic" means compiled claims, never vectors.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestQueryEngineHasNoVectorOption` (AST scan refuses reintroduction of any `*Vector*`/`*Embedding*` declaration), `TestQuery_RankingGolden` (pins the surviving keyword+tier ranking byte-identical).
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** one fewer subsystem, one fewer agentic-kit import edge from fathom's own code, no behavior change for any real caller — the branch was unreachable in production. Revisit only through vision 31 §5's kill criterion.

### O94 — SU-14: `enclosingSymbol`'s lone-symbol fallback lets a graph-expansion seed misattribute across an entire file

- **Symptom:** Hit building the SU-14 fixture that proves a two-hop bug ranks in `localize`'s top 3: `internal/mcp/search_tool.go`'s `enclosingSymbol` sets `upper = line` (the HIT's own line, not a real span) whenever a file's last-or-only symbol carries no `EndLine` and there is no following symbol to bound it — so any search/stack-frame hit at or after that symbol's declaration line, no matter how far past its real body, resolves to it. For `localize`'s new graph-expansion pass (ADR-041 D4) this matters beyond cosmetics: the seed `addSeedKey` walks the graph FROM is whichever symbol `enclosingSymbol` names, so a misattributed seed in a lightly-indexed (single-symbol-per-file) service walks the wrong neighbourhood entirely, silently.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `localize` (graph expansion), `search` (shares the same `enclosingSymbol` call)
  - **Status:** open — not fixed by this round. SU-14's own scope is `localize`'s ranking/expansion, not `enclosingSymbol`'s span inference; the fixture was rewritten to place its marker text inside the lone symbol's own body instead, which sidesteps the defect without touching it.
- **Repro/pin:** none filed as a regression test this round (see `internal/mcp/localize_tool_test.go`'s `twoHopBugFixture` comment for the workaround); a fix would need `EndLine` populated for more extractors, or a real one-symbol-file test in `internal/mcp/search_tool_test.go` pinning the current (over-wide) behavior before narrowing it.
- **Impact:** low today (multi-symbol files are the common case, where the next symbol's own `Line` already bounds the span correctly) but worth a follow-up before `localize`'s graph expansion is trusted on a corpus with many single-symbol files.

### O95 — SU-10: cue/load hands a dependency edge a DIFFERENT *build.Instance pointer than the same package's own top-level load

- **Symptom:** `EnrichCUEEdges`'s first working version resolved every same-package (same-file and cross-file) CUE reference correctly but every CROSS-PACKAGE import reference (`pkgtwo.#Widget: pkgone.#Base & {...}`) came back `core.EdgeUnresolved` even though `build.Instance.LookupImport` was finding the dependency and the target package's own def table (built from `load.Instances`'s own top-level slice) definitely contained the name.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** a throwaway `go test`-based debug harness printing `%p` pointers and `ImportPath`/`Dir` for every `load.Instances(["./..."], cfg)` result and its `.Imports` entries.
  - **Root cause:** `cuelang.org/go/cue/load` gives a package's TOP-LEVEL instance (from `load.Instances`) an `ImportPath` carrying a version suffix (`example.com/cuefixture/pkgone@v0:pkgone`), while that SAME package reached as a DEPENDENCY (`build.Instance.Imports`, what `LookupImport` returns) is a freshly-built `*build.Instance` — same `.Dir`, same `.Files`, same bare (unversioned) `.ImportPath` — but a genuinely different pointer. A def table keyed by `*build.Instance` pointer (built from the top-level slice) therefore never matches the instance `LookupImport` hands back.
  - **Fix:** key the def-table map by `inst.Dir` (an absolute path, identical across both instance objects for the same on-disk package) instead of by pointer identity — `internal/fusion/typedcue.go`'s `infos map[string]*cuePkgInfo`.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestEnrichCUEEdges_Fixture` (the cross-package `#Widget`/`profile` -> `pkgone.#Base` edges would silently regress to `EdgeUnresolved` if this regressed).
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** cross-package CUE typed edges resolve correctly; anyone else calling `cue/load` for cross-instance identity should key by `Dir`, never by `*build.Instance` pointer or `ImportPath` (which also differs by version-suffix presence between the two call sites).

### O96 — SU-4: a package-scoped graph patch that reinserts a SURVIVING key must never strip that key's cross-package incoming edges

- **Symptom:** The first `InvalidatePackage`/`ReplacePackage` implementation (`internal/analysis/callgraph/replace_package.go`) passed its own "no full rebuild" assertion but silently dropped a caller-package's edge into the patched package the moment the patched symbol's own key survived the patch unchanged (e.g. only its `Calls` list changed) — a self-caught regression, not a shipped one.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** internal/analysis/callgraph (ReplacePackage)
  - **Root cause:** the removal pass treated every old key in the touched package as unconditionally GONE — stripping every OTHER package's edge that pointed at it — even when that exact key reappeared in the new node set (the common case: a symbol's own body/Calls changed but its identity did not). A second bug in the same commit: `newNodes == nil` (the "typed edges only, symbols unchanged" call shape) was treated as "replace with nothing," deleting every node in the package and then silently dropping the very typed edges being added, since their endpoints no longer existed.
  - **Fix:** a `newKeySet` computed from the (possibly caller-defaulted-to-current) new node set gates the incoming-edge-strip step — a surviving key's own outgoing edges are still recomputed, but nothing else's edges into it are touched; `newNodes == nil` now defaults to the package's CURRENT node set rather than an empty one.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestInvalidatePackage_PatchesWithoutRebuild` (the cross-package "B1 still calls A1" assertions), `TestInvalidatePackage_TypedEdgesOnly`.
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** Documents the exact failure shape (survives-by-identity vs. survives-by-absence-of-newNodes) anyone extending package-scoped graph patching next — SU-4's own production wiring, or a future symbol-level cache patch elsewhere — needs to keep in mind; both are the kind of defect that would have read as "graph looks right" until a caller in a DIFFERENT, untouched package went silently unreachable.

### O97 — SU-21: Sweep's symbol-anchor re-anchor first draft healed a genuine content edit, not just a line-range move

- **Symptom:** `TestExpertModule_NonGitCorpus_EndToEnd` (pkg/client) regressed the moment SU-21's compiler wiring landed: a finding claim whose citation drifted after a real text edit (`"exponential backoff"` → `"jittered exponential backoff"`) inside its findings doc came back served FRESH instead of the expected `stale`. The claim's citation happened to resolve to exactly one indexed "symbol" (a Markdown `DocChunk` heading section — the extractor gives headings `Line`/`EndLine` too, not just Go declarations), so it got an `anchors` link at compile time; Sweep's new symbol-anchor fallback then re-anchored the citation onto that SAME section's CURRENT range — which is where the edit itself lived — recomputing the cites hash from the (already-edited) current content and writing it back as if nothing had changed.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `internal/semantic.Sweep` (SU-21's symbol-anchor fallback), root-caused via the failing `pkg/client` end-to-end test rather than a unit test (the unit-level `TestSweep_ReAnchorsViaSymbolWhenTextSearchCannot` fixture I wrote alongside it happened to only ever move the symbol, never edit its body, so it never would have caught this on its own).
  - **Root cause:** the first draft computed `newHash := core.HashLines(data, sym.Line, sym.EndLine)` and used it directly to re-anchor, without ever comparing it against the `anchors` link's OWN stored `TargetHash` (the symbol's body hash AT ANCHOR TIME). A hash freshly recomputed from current content is tautologically "correct" for whatever the content currently is — it proves nothing about whether that content actually matches what the claim originally cited.
  - **Fix:** gate the re-anchor on `newHash == link.TargetHash` — only a symbol whose OWN body is byte-identical to mint time (proof of a pure line-range move) heals; a body edit — even one entirely inside the "same" symbol — correctly falls through to `stale`, exactly like the pre-existing AnchorText mechanism's own unambiguous-match requirement.
- **Repro/pin:** `TestExpertModule_NonGitCorpus_EndToEnd` (pkg/client, the original regression) plus `TestSweep_ReAnchorsViaSymbolWhenTextSearchCannot` (internal/semantic, the isolated positive case — seeds an EMPTY `AnchorText` so the pre-existing literal-text path cannot fire, isolating the symbol-anchor mechanism specifically).
- **Status:** closed
- **Impact:** the general lesson for anyone extending anchor-based healing (line/symbol/AST-position based, not just this one): a re-anchor mechanism must always compare against a hash captured AT THE ORIGINAL ANCHOR TIME, never one derived solely from the CURRENT state of whatever it is about to re-point to — the latter cannot distinguish "moved, unchanged" from "changed in place," which is the entire question freshness tracking exists to answer.

### O100 — The answer oracle excluded an answer stated as a single backticked token on its own first line

- **Symptom:** lookups-py-r1 PY-03 read FAIL on BOTH arms although both answered "`APIRoute.__init__`" (the exact `answer_all_of` token) as the first line of the answer.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** internal/scenariobench oracle (`isQuotedPosition`, O53 defect 5b)
  - **Expected:** an answer-first line that is nothing but the symbol is the answer's own assertion.
  - **Actual:** a line wholly delimited by backticks was excluded as a "quoted line" regardless of position or content; `tokenCounts` found no other occurrence → `answerOK=false`.
- **Root cause:** the 5b rule was written for quoted evidence lines (prose) and never distinguished a whitespace-free code span on the answer's first line.
- **Fix:** a single-token code-span line counts when it is the answer's first non-empty line (later standalone lines stay citations — the existing whole-line-fence test); `TestOracleAnswer_SingleTokenCodeSpanLineIsAnAssertion`. The four PY-03 cells were re-scored from their raw streams (`-rescore-lost -rescore-reason "O100 quoted-line scorer"`, zero spend): 4/4 PASS.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** any lookup task whose gold is a bare identifier could read as a false FAIL on every arm; the paired comparison was unaffected (both arms failed alike) but absolute pass rates were understated.

### O99 — A file refresh left the daemon's memoized call graph stale: the memo is keyed by a watermark a single-file re-ingest never moves

- **Symptom:** after the pre-edit hook's POST /refresh-file re-extracted a file (SU-5), `find_callers` / `verify_plan` / `test_impact` on one of its symbols still answered from the pre-refresh graph — the memo (BP-28) is keyed by `IndexedCommit@IndexedAt`, which only a full ingest changes, so the same key stayed a cache hit.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** internal/mcp graph memo (`CachedCallGraph.Graph`), `pkg/client.StoreWatermark`, `fusion.ReingestFile` (replaces observations, never touches service meta)
  - **Expected:** a refreshed file's symbols are what the next graph query sees.
  - **Actual:** the memo served the old node set until the next full ingest; `InvalidatePackage` (SU-4) existed with tests but had no production caller.
- **Root cause:** SU-4's patch entry point was never wired into the refresh path; the cache key could not notice a per-file change by design.
- **Fix:** `Server.PatchPackageGraph`/`DropCachedGraphs` (internal/mcp/graph_patch.go) called from the refresh-file handler with the package's current nodes; `TestRefreshFile_PatchesCachedGraph`, `TestPatchPackageGraph_KeepsMemoFreshWithoutRebuild`.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** every reachability answer after an in-session edit now reflects the edit without paying a full rebuild; the response carries `graph_patched` so a hook can cite it.

### O98 — Several `callgraph.Build(nodes)` call sites never wire typed edges in, reproducing O40's ambiguous-method-name blind spot outside `verify_plan`

- **Symptom:** while closing O40 (see its Status update), a repo-wide `grep callgraph.Build(` turned up ~18 call sites; most call `callgraph.AddTypedEdges` (or `BuildBoth`) right after, but at least three do not: `internal/mcp/analysis_tools.go`'s shared `buildGraph` helper (backs `rename_impact`, `reachable`, `test_impact`, `dead_code` — its own file-header comment calls the syntax-only graph "intentionally simple," with no mention of typed edges), `internal/mcp/nav_tools.go:72` (the `callees` tool), and `cmd/fathom/planscontext.go:131` (`storeCtxIndex`, used by plan-context symbol resolution). Each of these will silently refuse a direct call on a concrete receiver whenever another receiver shares the same method name — the O40 shape — for as long as it lives.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `command grep -rn "callgraph\.Build(" --include="*.go" .` (repo-wide), cross-checked against each hit's surrounding function for a following `AddTypedEdges`/`BuildBoth` call.
  - **Root cause:** same class as O40's real cause (not its "malformed key" theory) — a call site builds the syntax-only graph and stops, instead of also merging in the typed pass's edges.
- **Fix:** not applied this wave (out of scope for the O39/O40 closure — this wave fixed only `cmd/fathom/verify.go`, the one directly blocking SU-1/SU-2). A follow-up wave should audit each of the three sites above (and re-run the repo-wide grep, since this list is not guaranteed exhaustive) and add `AddTypedEdges`/`BuildBoth` wherever the tool's answer is meant to be typed-precise.
- **Status:** fixed (2026-09-19, same round): `internal/mcp/analysis_tools.go` gained `bothGraph` — the watermark-cached syntax+typed graph `loadNavGraphs` already built for the nav tools — and `reachable`, `test_impact`, `dead_code` and `find_callees` walk it; `cmd/fathom/planscontext.go` adds the typed edges the same way `verify` now does. `rename_impact` keeps its syntax-only caller SET by decision (BP-26) and still annotates tiers from the typed graph. Regression coverage: `TestCmdVerify_Store_TypedEdgeWiresReachability`, `internal/verify/typededge_residue_test.go`; `go test ./internal/mcp/...` green on the change.
- **Impact:** `rename_impact`, `reachable`, `test_impact`, `dead_code`, and `callees` can under-report on exactly the ambiguous-method-name shape O40 demonstrated end to end for `verify_plan`/`verify` — plausibly a live source of false "unreachable"/"safe to delete"/"no callers" answers on any Go service with more than one implementer of a shared method name (very common — any interface with 2+ implementations).

### O111 — guards-jev-r1 died at exit=2 after 25/32 cells: one cell's oracle-scoring fault took down every remaining cell, not just its own task

- **Symptom:** guards-jev-r1 (8 tasks × arms `full`,`full-jev` × 2 trials = 32 cells, subject 533d293c) ended `exit=2` at `$SP/benchroot/logs/guards-jev-r1.log` having scored only 25 cells. Two errors, back to back, both against the SAME worktree/cell (`guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t1`): (1) `scenariobench: persisting cell-artifacts.json: finding instrument-owned files: reading .fathom/owned.json: open …/f67f06a-27d7f17caa49/.fathom/owned.json: operation not permitted`, immediately followed by (2) `scenariobench run: guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t1: running oracle: scoring refusal: listing changed paths against 260f0882e368d860b05f37340814fad8c251aaae: exit status 128` — and the run stopped there. Missing: `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t1` itself (errored, never stored), `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/{full,full-jev}/t2`, and all four cells of the untouched next task, `guards-i28-duckdb-path/{full,full-jev}/{t1,t2}` — 7 of 32.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `$SP/benchroot/logs/guards-jev-r1.log`; `$SP/benchroot/raw/guards-jev-r1/` (24 attempt dirs, confirming exactly the 24 non-i16 cells plus `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy-full-t1` persisted raw artifacts — `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy-full-jev-t1`'s raw dir also exists, since `persistRawCellArtifacts` runs before oracle scoring, but its `cell-artifacts.json` was never stored as a scored cell); direct inspection of the live worktree `/Volumes/m2ext/tmp/sextant-r3-workspace/scenariobench/subjects/9586e8f6…-worktrees/f67f06a-27d7f17caa49` after the run: `git log --oneline` shows two commits (`3d7e2ef base f67f06a` → `260f088 instrument files (arm, wiring, setup)`), `HEAD` = `260f0882e368d860b05f37340814fad8c251aaae`, `git cat-file -t` confirms it as a real, present, fully readable commit object, and `.fathom/owned.json` reads fine — i.e. at inspection time nothing was actually broken.
  - **Root cause (why the run died, `cmd/scenariobench/main.go:753-761`):** `runCellForReport` only ever degraded ONE class of `runOneCell` failure to a LOST cell: `errors.Is(err, errSubjectPrep)` (a `PrepareSubject` failure). Every OTHER `runOneCell` error — running sessions, running baseline detectors, running oracle (`RunOracle`, which is what `ScoreRefusal` failed inside) — fell through to `return cellOutcome{cp: cp}, fmt.Errorf("%s/%s/t%d: %w", …)`, a genuine `error` that propagates out of `RunArmsConcurrently` to `cmdRun`'s own loop, which does `if errs[k] != nil { …; return 2 }` unconditionally — aborting the WHOLE corpus at the first cell whose failure happened to land anywhere past subject prep. `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t1`'s own oracle-scoring git call is what landed there this time; it could as easily have been a session or a baseline detector.
  - **Root cause (why the git call itself failed, `internal/scenariobench/refusal.go:161`, `cmd/scenariobench/raw_artifacts.go:114-116`):** `ScoreRefusal`'s own `changedPaths` ALREADY follows the O105 pattern correctly — it diffs against `wt.LocalBase` (the worktree's own absorbed-instrument-files commit), never `task.Subject.BaseCommit` — and the live worktree evidence above confirms `wt.LocalBase` (`260f0882…`) really was the correct, current HEAD; O105's fix is not the defect here. What actually failed is unrecoverable from the log alone, because `changedPaths` wrapped only `err` (`"exit status 128"`, exec.Cmd's bare exit-code text) and threw away `out` — `runIn`'s own `CombinedOutput`, the ONLY place git's real `fatal: …` line lives. `"exit status 128"` is identical whether git meant "unknown revision", "not a git repository", or "Operation not permitted" reading a pack file — the exact wedged-TCC-volume symptom this session's own global CLAUDE.md documents for `/Volumes/m2ext` reads, and the same literal text error (1) above DID carry ("operation not permitted", from `persistRawCellArtifacts`'s direct `os.Open` of the SAME worktree's `.fathom/owned.json`, moments earlier in the SAME cell's own pipeline, `raw_artifacts.go:114-116`, which is best-effort/non-fatal by design and correctly did not abort anything on its own). Two failures, same worktree, same cell, seconds apart, one of them literally EPERM — and the object that "didn't exist" during the run demonstrably exists and reads fine afterward: the far more likely explanation is one transient TCC-wedge window hitting both reads, not a logic bug in base-commit selection.
  - Full log line count is 55; the two errors are the last two lines before `bench guards-jev-r1 end … exit=2`.
- **Fix:** three changes, all `cmd/scenariobench`/`internal/scenariobench`, tests failing-first against the pre-fix code (confirmed: reverting `main.go` alone reproduces the exact fatal `"scenariobench run: T1/control/t1: creating per-trial cell dir …"` abort shape under `TestCmdRun_RunOneCellErrorLostsOneCellRunContinues`).
  1. `runCellForReport` (`main.go`): ANY `runOneCell` error now becomes a LOST cell, not just `errSubjectPrep` — O52's "a driver kills what it started" generalized to "a driver loses only what it touched"; `errSubjectPrep` keeps its own `"instrument: "` prefix so a reader can still tell "never got a worktree" apart from "failed partway through". `TestCmdRun_RunOneCellErrorLostsOneCellRunContinues`.
  2. `changedPaths` (`refusal.go`): the wrapped error now appends `strings.TrimSpace(out)` — git's own diagnostic text — so a future occurrence of this exact fault reads as what it is (and so `stickyInstrumentFaultMessage`'s existing `"operation not permitted"` substring check, which already exists for exactly this class, can actually fire on it and stop the run cleanly instead of re-losing every remaining cell one at a time). `TestScoreRefusal_GitFailureCarriesGitsOwnDiagnosticText`.
  3. `persistRawCellArtifacts` (`raw_artifacts.go`): a failed `InstrumentOwnedPaths` read now sets `cell-artifacts.json`'s own new `instrument_owned_paths_unknown: true`, instead of writing an empty `instrument_owned_paths` list byte-identical to a genuinely unwired cell's honest zero. Still best-effort (O76's own design) — never fails the cell on its own. `TestPersistRawCellArtifacts_OwnedPathsReadFailureMarkedUnknown`.
  - `wave-dp-b` branch `bench-fix`, commit `76d0b1b2`; `go test -race ./internal/scenariobench/... ./cmd/scenariobench/...` green, `go vet` clean, `gofmt -l` clean.
- **Status:** fixed (2026-09-23) — not yet merged into `$SP/wave-r3` (round3); merge only after the concurrently-running `lookups-vs-jev-r1` finishes (never touch `wave-r3` while it runs). Rerun prepared, not launched: `$SP/guards-rerun.sh`.
- **Impact:** none of the 25 already-scored cells are void — `StoreScenarioCell` ran and completed for each before the abort, and `ScoreRefusal` never consults `InstrumentOwnedPaths` at all (only region-stripping), so even the theoretical corruption path (an owned-file wrongly left IN a scored diff) cannot have touched a refusal-aware task's PASS/FAIL. 7 cells must rerun: `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t1` (errored, never stored), `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full/t2`, `guards-i16-raw-cp-deploy/full-jev/t2`, and all four of `guards-i28-duckdb-path/{full,full-jev}/{t1,t2}` (never attempted at all). Without this fix, EVERY corpus run remains one transient fault away from losing every cell behind whichever one it happens to land on — a bigger risk than this single incident, since `runOneCell`'s hard-error paths (sessions, baseline detectors, oracle) are common to every corpus this driver runs, not just guards-fathom.

### O110 — The decision port against the real model: Jev 1.13 through OpenRouter

- **Symptom (what it did):** first live run of fathom's decision port against the ACTUAL TypeSafe System One model (`typesafe/jev-1.13`, not a stand-in `llm`/OpenAI lane), routed through OpenRouter (`endpoint = "https://openrouter.ai/api"`, `POST .../v1/systemone`). Scratch daemon (`127.0.0.1:7680`, `--auto-refresh=false`, read-only backup store, service `fathom` @ `a7b982c4`), startup line confirmed `serve-http: decision port typesafe (model typesafe/jev-1.13)`. `POST /route` on 10 prompts: all 10 answered (no timeouts — contrast O109's `llm`/OpenRouter run, 3 of 6 timed out at the 3s budget), 8 of 10 verbs matched the SB-7 routing table exactly, latency 158–292ms (well inside the 3s route budget). `obligations` via `POST /rpc/agent` on 3 files: all three carried real `ranked_by:"typesafe"`, `ranked_count`/`candidates_total`, and — newly confirmed live — a genuine per-rule `relevance` float on 6 of 8 served rows each time (O109 never observed this on a real wire response). `compile --dry-run` (default layer `obligations`, 72 sections): **failed outright**, `typesafe: status 400: max_tokens_exceeded`, before compiling or prefiltering a single section — a new, Jev-specific failure O109 never hit (its `llm` adapter run got 144 real prefilter noul calls). The edit-hook fired end-to-end and injected real `typesafe`-influenced obligations text, but the injected TEXT itself never names the provider, and the first post-TCC-reset call measured 5,217.6ms — over both the task's cited 2s hook budget and this repo's own 5s `PostToolUse` `timeout` (`.claude/settings.json`); three immediately-following calls measured 309–534ms, comfortably inside budget.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Route table** (`POST /route`, same 6 O109 prompts + 4 more shapes):

    | # | prompt (shape) | verb | confidence | band | latency |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | TestPrepareSubject_WorktreeHasNoFuture… (issue) | localize | 1.00 | proceed | 266ms |
    | 2 | panic: invalid memory address in serve.go:412… (issue) | localize | 0.989 | proceed | 215ms |
    | 3 | where is ranked_by set? (where) | find_symbol | 0.909 | proceed | 257ms |
    | 4 | who calls rerankWithinTiers? (who-calls) | find_callers | 0.989 | proceed | 336ms |
    | 5 | what rules govern servehttp.go? (what-governs) | obligations | 1.00 | proceed | 246ms |
    | 6 | thanks, that looks right (chat) | none | 1.00 | proceed | 226ms |
    | 7 | is "obligations ≤6 rules" still true? (staleness) | staleness_report | 0.874 | **confirm** | 331ms |
    | 8 | find every place 'sections_prefiltered' appears (literal) | search | 0.931 | proceed | 346ms |
    | 9 | refactor Compiler.Compile to take ctx+opts (feature) | none | 0.954 | proceed | 194ms |
    | 10 | why does the daemon grow to 12GB after 10h? (issue?) | none | 0.509 | **confirm** | 303ms |
