    8/10 verbs match the SB-7 table exactly (rows 1–6, 8, 9); row 7 is a defensible near-miss (staleness shape correctly identified, confidence just under the confirm/proceed line); row 10 is the one genuinely debatable call — a "why does X happen" resource-growth question could reasonably route to `localize` (it is issue-shaped) but Jev answered `none` at low (0.509) confidence, correctly landing in the hedged `confirm` band rather than asserting it wrongly. `applied` (band proceed AND confidence ≥ `route_confidence`) was true for 8/10; rows 7 and 10 logged but not applied — exactly the intended degrade.
  - **Obligations, three files** (`POST /rpc/agent`, `tools/call obligations`, `{service:"fathom", file:...}`):

    | file | ranked_by | ranked_count/candidates_total | rules served | relevance-carrying | wall ms | order vs `none` baseline |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | `internal/semantic/serve.go` | typesafe | 10/160 | 8 | 6 (0.69–0.79) | 784.5 | not compared (see below) |
    | `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go` | typesafe | 142/147 | 8 | 6 (0.24–0.82) | 572.4 | not compared |
    | `internal/mcp/semantic_tools.go` | typesafe | 9/159 | 8 | 6 (0.46–0.84) | 312.7 | **changed** — positions 4–8 reorder vs a same-store, same-config `provider="none"` rerun (`none`: …724-729, 738-739, 746-749, 746-749, 754-763, 755-762; `typesafe`: …724-729, 746-749, 735-740, 724-729, 736-738, 754-763) |

    `relevance` genuinely rides the wire this time (O109's own residual open question — "relevance was already riding correctly" per its W-G fix note, but never observed live against a real provider until now): 6 of 8 rows on every one of the 3 calls carry it, always the `doc-claimed`-tier rows; the 2 `raw`-tier rows (ADR.md/CLAUDE.md citations served via the ADR-037 raw-fallback path) never carry it, by construction — the rerank only ever scores compiled claims. Within the `doc-claimed` rows, relevance order is NOT strictly descending on the wire (`cmd/fathom/servehttp.go`'s served order is 0.61, 0.4, 0.24, 0.82, 0.81, 0.59) — DP-24's window-limited rerank reorders only WITHIN the governing-scope tier the rule already occupies (package/parent_dir/root — `displaced_by_tier`), not globally by relevance across the whole served list, so a reader must not assume "served rules[] is sorted by relevance" even when relevance is present on every row.
  - **`compile --dry-run` — real failure, root cause found:** `bin/fathom compile --url http://127.0.0.1:7680 --service fathom --dry-run` (layer defaults to `obligations`, this store's corpus has grown to 72 sections) returned `error: RPC error: semantic: compile: semantic: compile prefilter: typesafe: status 400: {"error":{"message":"HTTP 400: {\"detail\":{\"error_type\":\"max_tokens_exceeded\"}}","code":400}}` — no `compile_runs` row was written (confirmed: `sqlite3 store.sqlite` shows the same two most-recent `obligations`-layer rows from before this session, unchanged). `decisions.jsonl` logged it as a proper `RecordError` row: `site:"compile_prefilter", provider:"typesafe", questions:72, latency_ms:289, applied:false`. Root cause read from source: `decisionBatchSize` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch.go:61-69`) returns `prefilterChunkSize` (20) ONLY when `d.Name() == "llm"`; every other Decider — including `TypeSafe`, whose `Name()` returns `"typesafe"` and which implements no optional `MaxQuestions()` — falls through to `return 0` (unbounded), so `PrefilterSections` (`internal/semantic/prefilter.go`) set `chunkSize = len(secs) = 72` and sent all 72 sections' FULL markdown text in one `decision.Request`. Jev/OpenRouter refused the request outright before answering anything. This is NOT a rerank problem: the SAME unbounded path serves `rerankWithinTiers` fine at up to 248 questions in one call (11 distinct HTTP calls behind the 683 `obligations`-site rows below, one carrying `input_tokens:20273, output_tokens:17769`) — because rerank candidates are short claim summaries, not full section bodies, so the unbounded batch stays under Jev's ceiling there even though it blew through it for 72 full sections. `sections_prefiltered` and the noul spread the task asked for: **not measurable** — the call aborted before producing a single answer. As the closest honest substitute, the `obligations`-site noul answers that DID succeed this session (n=683, across the three files' reranks plus one layer=`all` probe) span min 0.20, median 0.60, max 0.85, with 148 of 683 (21.7%) below the 0.5 default prefilter threshold — proof Jev spans the full noul range meaningfully on this kind of question, but not proof of what it would say about compile sections, which it was never asked.
  - **W-K (2026-09-23) re-measurement: the pre-filter now completes.** Fix: `decisionBatchPlan`/`decisionBatchCaps` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch.go`, DP-26) replace the count-only rule with a chunk plan bounded by BOTH a question-count cap and a state-byte budget (typesafe/unrecognized: 64 questions, 96 KiB; llm: 20 questions, 96 KiB; either overridable per-Decider). Same daemon/store/model as above (`127.0.0.1:7681` this time), same 72-section `obligations` layer, same `bin/fathom compile --url … --service fathom --dry-run`, run twice — `compile_threshold = 0.5` then `0.3` (config edited, daemon restarted between runs since config loads once at startup). Both runs: **zero errors**, `decisions.jsonl` gained 72 new `compile_prefilter` rows each time (144 total, 0 `error` fields), split into exactly 2 Decide calls per run — **58 and 14 questions** — proving the fix is genuinely BYTE-bound here, not count-bound: 58 < the 64-question cap, so the 96 KiB byte budget closed the first chunk before the count cap could (real section sizes vary well beyond the 2 KiB-per-section shape a synthetic test would assume). Latencies: run 1 (0.5) 718ms/407ms per call, compile summary `duration_ms:1134`; run 2 (0.3) 610ms/402ms, `duration_ms:1026`. Noul spread was IDENTICAL across both runs (same store, same sections, near-deterministic Jev answers): **min 0.64, median 0.89, max 0.90** (n=72 each run) — every single section scored above BOTH 0.5 and 0.3, so `sections_prefiltered = 0` at both thresholds (the CLI's own `prefiltered: N section(s) …` line only prints when `SectionsPrefiltered > 0`, and it did not print either time; `sections_compiled:51` of 72 both runs is the store's ordinary incremental "needs recompile" count, unrelated to this prefilter). The lowest-scoring sections (0.64–0.66) were individual `ADR.md` decision-record entries (`ADR-026`, `ADR-032`, `ADR-037`, `ADR-016`, `ADR-024`, `ADR-028`, `ADR-029`, `docs/plans/README.md#annotation-keys`) — narrative/historical in framing, yet still judged binding by Jev at a comfortable margin above the 0.3 sensitivity check. Spend, measured via `GET /v1/auth/key`: before `usage:0.057949951`, after both runs `usage:0.060327613` — delta **$0.002377662** for 2 full 72-section prefilter passes (4 HTTP calls, 144 questions, all input-tokens-only per Jev/typesafe's free-output pricing) — again an upper bound, the key is shared with other concurrent activity today.
  - **`decisions.jsonl` tallies** (baseline 576 lines before this session, 1270 after — 694 new rows): `route` n=10, latency 158–292ms (median 224ms), applied 8/10. `obligations` n=683, latency 145–433ms (median 375ms), applied 683/683, behind only 11 distinct HTTP calls (grouped by identical timestamp+token-count+latency) — sizes ranged from 9 to 248 questions per call. `compile_prefilter` n=1, the `max_tokens_exceeded` error row above, applied 0/1. One error row total.
  - **Edit hook, end to end** (`bin/fathom edit-hook --url http://127.0.0.1:7680`, a fake `PostToolUse` payload, `tool_input.file_path` **must be absolute** — a relative path silently resolves against the hook process's own cwd via `filepath.Abs`, missing every service-candidate root and producing total silence with exit 0 and empty stdout; this cost one wasted trial before the payload was corrected): the corrected call for `internal/semantic/serve.go` returned real `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` — 8 bulleted rules, a `⚠️ 170 more eligible rule(s) not shown (cap)` line, an `⚠️ index is behind your working tree` line — but the rendered TEXT never names `ranked_by`/`typesafe`/any provider anywhere; only the raw `obligations` JSON-RPC tool discloses that. First call (immediately after an `SystemPolicyRemovableVolumes` TCC reset this session also needed for `compile`'s own EPERM wedge on `/Volumes/m2ext`) measured **5,217.6ms** — longer than both the task's cited 2s hook budget and this repo's own committed 5s `PostToolUse` `timeout` (`.claude/settings.json`), i.e. a real risk of the hook being killed before the daemon replies in a live session. Three immediately-following calls (same warm daemon, two other files then a repeat of the first) measured 534.1ms, 340.4ms, 309.3ms — comfortably inside budget. A same-file, same-daemon DIRECT `/rpc/agent obligations` call (bypassing the edit-hook's own candidate-resolution/spec-ref/findings machinery) measured only 829.6ms even cold, so the 5.2s outlier is most likely the edit-hook's OWN surrounding machinery (service-candidate resolution via `list_services`, spec-ref lookups, findings block) compounding right after a TCC reset, not the decision port itself — flagged as a plausible explanation, not a confirmed one.
  - **Spend, measured via `GET /v1/auth/key`:** before starting the typesafe daemon, `usage:0.055317433`. After the full route+obligations measurement pass (before restoring `provider="none"`): `usage:0.056863327` — delta **$0.001545894** (~0.155¢) for 10 route calls + 4 obligations tool calls (~694 decisions.jsonl rows, several batches over 100 candidates) + 1 failed compile attempt. A THIRD check after restoring `provider="none"` and making one more (uncosted, `ranked_by:"none"`) obligations call: `usage:0.057949951` — a FURTHER +$0.001086624 drift with **zero** Jev calls made by this session in between, proving `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is shared with other concurrent activity today; the $0.001546 figure is an upper bound on this session's own attributable spend, not an isolated measurement. Consistent in order of magnitude with the task's own cited baseline (a two-question probe = $0.000015): ~700 decision-port answers, several in very large batches, cost roughly 100× a two-question probe — a small fraction of a cent.
- **Root cause:** `decisionBatchSize`'s chunk-size rule is keyed on the LITERAL string `"llm"` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch.go:65`), not on "any Decider without a large-batch guarantee" — every other real Decider, including `typesafe`, gets NO chunking at all unless it opts in via an optional `MaxQuestions()` method, which `decision.TypeSafe` does not implement. This was invisible until now because O109 through O109's own W-I fix only ever exercised the `llm` adapter; wiring the REAL `typesafe` adapter for the first time immediately found the gap on the one call path (`compile_prefilter`) that sends large payloads (full section text) rather than compact claim summaries. **W-K:** confirmed the count-only rule was the WHOLE defect — the chunk that failed carried all 72 sections' FULL markdown text as one shared `decision.Request.State`, and no count-only cap the old rule could have chosen (even `prefilterChunkSize`, 20) actually protects against a byte overflow: 20 sections can still overflow a byte budget if they happen to be large, and 60 small sections can still fit comfortably under one. A rule keyed only on question COUNT cannot see the difference between the pre-filter's per-chunk STATE (one shared blob carrying every section's full text) and the re-rank's per-chunk STATE (a small file path and hunk, with the variable content living in each question's own `TrueCriteria` instead) — decisionBatchPlan fixes this by requiring the caller to supply each item's own byte contribution explicitly, from whichever wire field actually carries it.
- **Fix:** applied this wave (W-K, 2026-09-23): `decisionBatchPlan`/`decisionBatchCaps` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch.go`, DP-26) replace `decisionBatchSize` with a chunk plan bounded by BOTH a question-count cap and a state-byte budget — llm: 20 questions / 96 KiB; typesafe and any Decider decisionBatchCaps does not specially recognize: 64 questions / 96 KiB (previously fully unbounded, 0); either cap overridable per-Decider via an optional `MaxQuestions()`/`MaxStateBytes()` method. A single item whose own content alone exceeds the byte budget is never sent at all — it is skipped (folded into `UncompiledScopes`/`SectionsPrefiltered` exactly like a below-threshold section) and recorded with a human-readable reason via a synthetic `decision.Record` (`Confidence 0`, so `decide.Recorder` logs it `applied:false` — "never actually asked," not "asked and failed"). Both `PrefilterSections` and `rerankWithinTiers` route through the shared plan; new tests: `TestDecisionBatchCaps`, `TestDecisionBatchPlan_CountBound`/`_ByteBound`/`_OversizedItemGoesAloneAndIsNamed`/`_UnboundedBothCapsIsOneChunk` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch_test.go`), `TestPrefilterSections_DefaultCapsChunkByBytes`/`_OversizedSectionNeverSent` (`prefilter_test.go`), `TestRerankWithinTiers_DefaultCapsChunkTypesafe`/`_OversizedClaimNeverSent` (`decisionrank_test.go`) — all green under `-race`. Re-measured live against the SAME Jev/OpenRouter route this observation's own W-J run failed against: see the W-K bullet above — the pre-filter now completes with zero errors.
- **Status:** closed 2026-09-22 (W-J, measurement + root cause); **the fix landed and was re-measured 2026-09-23 (W-K)** — `docs/plans/decision-port.md` DP-26.
- **Impact:** the decision port works, for real, against the real model, on the two paths that send compact payloads (`/route`, obligations rerank) — routing agreed with the SB-7 table on 8/10 prompts at sub-350ms latency with zero timeouts (a marked improvement on O109's `llm`/OpenRouter run, which timed out on half its prompts), and the obligations rerank genuinely reorders served rules and genuinely discloses `ranked_by`/`ranked_count`/`relevance` on the wire, both firsts for a REAL provider in this observations log. **W-K:** the one path that sent a large payload (`compile`'s section prefilter) now ALSO works against Jev — zero errors across two full 72-section passes at both `compile_threshold = 0.5` and `0.3`, chunked into 2 HTTP calls (58 + 14 questions) each time. The edit hook — the actual user-facing path — succeeds and stays well inside budget once warm (309–534ms), but its FIRST call this session took over 5 seconds, at or past this repo's own committed hook timeout; whether that was a one-time TCC-adjacent cost or a real cold-path risk is not resolved here and should not be assumed away. **DP-13 verdict (W-K):** no section was skipped at EITHER threshold — every one of the 72 sections scored noul 0.64–0.90, comfortably above both 0.5 and 0.3 — so there is no skipped-section list to fault for dropping a rule-bearing section; if anything, the closest-to-the-line sections (noul 0.64–0.66: individual `ADR.md` decision-record entries — ADR-026, ADR-032, ADR-037, ADR-016, ADR-024, ADR-028, ADR-029 — plus `docs/plans/README.md#annotation-keys`) read as narrative/historical in style yet were still judged binding by Jev with real margin, which reads as Jev being PERMISSIVE on this corpus (biased toward "yes, binding") rather than sharply discriminating — worth watching on a corpus with more genuinely narrative sections before trusting the 0.5 default to do real work here. A real compile A/B (DP-13) can now run against Jev without the hard failure that blocked it: the fixed prefilter itself costs ~$0.0012 per 72-section pass (measured: $0.002377662 for two full passes, input-tokens-only pricing) — trivial against DP-13's $1 bar — but the DOMINANT marginal cost of an actual DP-13 run is the separate compile-extraction model (`openai/gpt-6-luna` in this daemon's config, ~$0.048 estimated per dry run per the CLI's own `cost_usd`), not the now-fixed Jev prefilter; DP-13 itself is still gated on that spend, an operator decision, unchanged by this wave. DP-14 (serving/routing A/B) has no such blocker — routing and obligations-rerank calls both completed cheaply and reliably here — but the unbounded rerank batch size (up to 248 candidates observed in one call) means a repo-wide A/B could still produce a few very large, individually-expensive calls; DP-14's "operator decision: LLM spend" gate remains the right posture regardless of this wave's fix, which only bounds the PRE-FILTER's batches, not the re-rank's (whose own batches were already safe against the byte budget in practice, per the W-K bullet above).

### O109 — The decision port's live run through OpenRouter (`llm` adapter, `openai/gpt-6-luna`): routing hit its 3s budget half the time, and `ranked_by`/`rank_note`/`relevance`/`prefilter_skipped` never reach the wire

- **Symptom (what the live port did):** a scratch daemon (`.fathom/config.toml [decide] provider = "llm"`, profile `or` → OpenRouter `openai/gpt-6-luna`) driven for real against a read-only backup of the fleet store (service `fathom` @ `a7b982c4`). `POST /route`: 3 of 6 prompts timed out (`context deadline exceeded`) against the handler's fixed 3s budget; the other 3 answered correctly with high confidence. `obligations` via `POST /rpc/agent`: the response never carries `ranked_by`, `rank_note`, or any rule's `relevance`, on either provider. `compile_layer --dry-run`: never carries `sections_prefiltered`/discloses which sections were skipped, though the pre-filter genuinely ran (144 real noul calls logged). Every one of these is exactly what `docs/mcp-tools.md` and plan rows DP-6/DP-8 (both `[x]`) document as present.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Route table** (`POST /route`, `routeDecideTimeout` = 3s, `cmd/fathom/route.go:27`):

    | prompt (shape) | verb | confidence | band | provider |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | "TestPrepareSubject_WorktreeHasNoFuture fails with 'must be run in a work tree'…" (issue) | — | 0 | unsure | none (`context deadline exceeded`) |
    | "panic: runtime error: invalid memory address in internal/semantic/serve.go:412…" (issue) | — | 0 | unsure | none (`context deadline exceeded`) |
    | "where is the obligations envelope's ranked_by set?" (where) | — | 0 | unsure | none (`context deadline exceeded`) |
    | "who calls rerankWithinTiers?" (who-calls) | find_callers | 0.977 | proceed | llm |
    | "what rules govern cmd/fathom/servehttp.go?" (what-governs) | obligations | 0.909 | proceed | llm |
    | "thanks, that looks right" (chat) | none | 1.0 | proceed | llm |

    The 3 that answered came AFTER the 3 that timed out, in this exact request order — a cold-connection/first-call-latency pattern, not a shape the router got wrong: `decisions.jsonl`'s `route` rows show real answered latencies of 1506–2786 ms (avg 2282 ms) once the connection was warm, already more than half the 3s budget the handler grants.
  - **Obligations, two files, both `[decide]` providers** (`POST /rpc/agent`, `tools/call obligations`, `{service:"fathom", file:...}`): `internal/semantic/serve.go` and `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go`. Neither response, under `provider=llm` or a same-config restart under `provider=none`, carries a `ranked_by` key at all (`"ranked_by" in structuredContent` is `False` both times) — not `"ranked_by":"none"`, not present. The served `rules[].rule` order is BYTE-IDENTICAL between the two providers for both files (compared programmatically). `layer_coverage` for both calls: `sections_total:72, sections_compiled:21` (`serve.go`) — no `prefilter_skipped` key.
  - **Root cause, read from source, not inferred:** `internal/mcp/semantic_tools.go`'s `renderObligationReceipt` (the function that builds the ACTUAL `map[string]any` served on `/rpc/agent` for `obligations`, lines 248–415) never reads `obl.RankedBy` or `obl.RankNote` anywhere in its body, and no `Obligation` row it renders carries `relevance` either — even though `internal/semantic.Obligations.RankedBy` (`serve.go:271`) is doc-commented "Always present, never omitempty" and IS correctly populated by `pkg/client.Client.Obligations` (`semantic.go:124-128`, routed through `ObligationsForOpts` + `decisionOpts()`, confirmed wired since `servehttp.go:125` calls `c.WithDecision(...)`). The struct-level field is right; the wire-rendering function that turns it into JSON simply drops it. Same class of gap in `compile_layer`'s handler (`semantic_tools.go:715-750`): its hand-built response map copies 15 named fields off `core.CompileRun` and never includes `SectionsPrefiltered` (confirmed present in the schema — `compile_runs.sections_prefiltered`, `internal/semantic/compile.go:357`). No test in `internal/mcp` asserts either field on the wire (`grep -rl ranked_by internal/mcp/*_test.go` → nothing) — DP-8's and DP-6's own `reachable_from` tests (`TestObligations_RerankWithinTierOnly`, `TestCompile_PrefilterSkipsAndDiscloses`) exercise `internal/semantic` directly and never assert the actual JSON-RPC envelope, so both plan rows verified true while the live wire contract they cite (`docs/mcp-tools.md`) was never true for a real client. Separately, this session's two files served entirely `raw`/`doc-claimed` tier rows (compile coverage is only 21–43 of 72 sections at this commit) — `rerankWithinTiers` only reorders eligible compiled-`obligations`-tier claims, so the byte-identical order is also consistent with there being nothing in scope to rerank here; this run cannot distinguish "the rerank never fired for these two files" from "the rerank fired but nothing changed," only that its disclosure never reaches the wire either way.
  - **decisions.jsonl tallies:** 147 rows this session — `route`: 3 (avg latency 2282 ms, range 1506–2786), `compile_prefilter`: 144 (avg latency 8393 ms, range 5862–12272; 144 = 72 sections × 2 dry-run compiles, one row per section — the pre-filter's batched noul genuinely ran and was genuinely logged). Zero rows under any obligations/rerank site name, for either provider. `applied: true` on all 147 (nothing fell in the unsure band this session); `band` is absent from every raw `decision.Record` (it's computed downstream by `/route`'s own handler, not stored per-record).
  - **A second, separate gotcha, confirmed in the vendored kit source** (`third_party/agentic-kit/foundations/decision/record.go:35-40`, doc comment: *"InputTokens is the Response's Usage.InputTokens, copied onto every Record"*): naively summing `input_tokens` across `decisions.jsonl` rows to estimate spend overcounts by roughly one batch-size factor, because every question in one batched HTTP call is logged as its own `Record` carrying that WHOLE call's token usage, not a per-question share. Measured here: summed `input_tokens` across all 147 rows = 2,044,223; the compile tool's own correctly-tracked run totals were 41,177 + 44,534 = 85,711 tokens_in for the same two runs (a ~24× gap) — real spend must come from the tool's own aggregate `tokens_in`/`cost_usd` or the provider's usage endpoint, never a per-record sum.
  - **`compile_layer`'s self-reported `cost_usd` overstates real spend:** the two dry-run compiles self-reported `cost_usd: 0.041177` and `0.044534` ($0.0857 combined) from their own internal per-token rate table, while the OpenRouter account's REAL total spend for the ENTIRE session — those two compiles PLUS 6 route asks PLUS 4 obligations calls — was only $0.021614 (usage $0.000062244 → $0.021676694, `GET /v1/auth/key`, measured before and after). The tool's cost estimate for `openai/gpt-6-luna` on OpenRouter is roughly 4× the real billed rate for this session.
- **Fix:** not applied this wave (out of scope — W-F is phase 1/1.5/2 of the decision-port kit wiring, not a bug-fix pass on the existing `llm`-adapter wiring). Concretely actionable for a follow-up: (1) `renderObligationReceipt` copy `obl.RankedBy`/`obl.RankNote` into `result`, and each rendered `Obligation` its `relevance` — mirroring how `stale_count`/`displaced` are already conditionally added; (2) `compile_layer`'s handler add `run.SectionsPrefiltered` (as `sections_prefiltered`, omitempty-style) to its response map; (3) a wire-level `internal/mcp` test asserting both, so DP-6/DP-8 (and this wave's DP-18) can't verify true again while the JSON-RPC envelope stays silent; (4) `routeDecideTimeout` (3s, `cmd/fathom/route.go:27`) revisited against measured real-network latency — this session's own `compile_prefilter` p50 was already ~8.4s, well past what `/route` budgets a single choice question; (5) `compile_layer`'s cost table checked against real OpenRouter per-model pricing rather than a static assumed rate.
- **Fix (2026-09-22, W-G):** items (1)-(3) landed. `renderObligationReceipt` (`internal/mcp/semantic_tools.go`) now copies `obl.RankedBy` (always) and `obl.RankNote` (when non-empty) into the wire response; `relevance` was already riding the wire correctly via `Obligation.Relevance`'s own json tag (`withSnippets` only adds keys, never drops any) — confirmed by a new wire test, no code change needed there. `compile_layer`'s handler now copies `SectionsPrefiltered`/`PrefilteredSections`. New wire-level `internal/mcp` tests: `TestObligationsTool_RankedByAndRelevanceOnWire`, `TestObligationsTool_RankedByNoneAndRankNoteOnWire`, `TestCompileLayer_SectionsPrefilteredOnWire`, `TestCompileLayer_SectionsPrefilteredOmittedWhenZero` (fake-backend, map-level), plus `TestObligationsWire_RerankReachesTheJSONRPCEnvelope` and `TestObligationsWire_NoEligibleClaimsNamesWhyOnWire` (`internal/mcp/decisionrerank_wire_test.go`) — a REAL `pkg/client.Client` over a real store wired to a `decision.MockDecider` via `WithDecision`, driven through the actual JSON-RPC surface, the gap DP-6/DP-8/DP-7's own `reachable_from` tests never covered. **Root cause of the live "zero decisions logged, byte-identical order" finding, traced and confirmed by the second wire test:** it was not a wiring bug — `internal/semantic.obligationsCore` (`serve.go`) returns BEFORE the DP-7 rerank block whenever nothing scope-matches the queried file at all (either `byID` — no claim fetched for the service/scope — or `matched` — fetched claims, none scope-eligible for this file — comes back empty), so `rerankWithinTiers` and its `d.Decide` call are never reached; with O109's own two probe files sitting at 21-43/72 compiled sections, this is the very likely explanation for both files. The mechanism itself is not broken: the first wire test proves the Decider gets called, `ranked_by` names it, `relevance` rides the wire, and a `site":"obligations"` row lands in the decisions log the moment there are ≥2 eligible same-tier claims. What WAS missing is disclosure of which case occurred — `ranked_by:"none"` looked identical whether no provider was configured or a real provider had nothing to rerank. Fixed with a new `noteNoEligibleRerank` helper (`internal/semantic/serve.go`) that sets `out.RankNote` to `"no eligible compiled rules in scope to rerank"` at both early-return sites whenever `opts.RankWithinTier && opts.Decider != nil` — a no-op for every caller without an active decision port, so RankedBy's own "none" contract and every byte-identical-payload guarantee elsewhere are unchanged. Status stays open until the live rerun below confirms this against the real store.
- **Fix (verified live, 2026-09-22, W-G):** Re-ran O109's exact obligations probe against the SAME read-only backup store (service `fathom` @ `a7b982c4`, `$SP/or-live/store.sqlite`, unchanged — not rebuilt), a scratch daemon on `127.0.0.1:7677`, `--auto-refresh=false`, `[decide] provider = "llm"` (OpenRouter `openai/gpt-6-luna`), a binary built from this wave's own fixes. `POST /rpc/agent tools/call obligations` for `internal/semantic/serve.go` and `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go`:

  | file | ranked_by | rank_note | rules served | relevance-carrying | latency |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `internal/semantic/serve.go` | `"none"` | `"decision: llm response is not JSON: unexpected end of JSON input"` | 8 | 0 | 18.37s |
  | `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go` | `"none"` | (same) | 8 | 0 | 18.52s |

  Both `ranked_by` and `rank_note` are now genuinely on the wire — Task 1's fix, confirmed live (previously absent entirely, on either provider). `decisions.jsonl` gained ZERO new `obligations`-site rows (147 → 147, unchanged), yet the OpenRouter account's real spend rose $0.021677 → $0.027528 (delta $0.005851, 0.585¢) across the two calls — a real API round trip DID happen and WAS billed, but `rerankWithinTiers`'s `d.Decide` call returned a hard error (the model's response failed to parse as JSON) before producing any `decision.Record`, and `obligationsCore`'s own contract (ADR-042 decision 3: "a provider error never fails the call") swallows that error into the untouched, `RankedBy:"none"` path with nothing to log. This exactly reproduces O109's original "zero decisions, ranked_by looked absent" symptom, this time WITH the reason named on the wire. Served rule order is unchanged from the "none" baseline BY CONSTRUCTION (a decide error leaves `matched` untouched per `rerankWithinTiers`'s own doc comment), consistent with O109's own byte-identical-order finding.

  **This is a DIFFERENT root cause than DP-20's fix addresses, and a more specific one than this wave's first Fix note above guessed.** DP-20 (`noteNoEligibleRerank`) covers the "nothing eligible to rerank" case (`matched`/`byID` empty) — genuinely fixed and wire-tested, but NOT what fired here: `matched` was clearly non-empty (`displaced:154`, `displaced_by_tier:{package:4,parent_dir:13,root:137}` on the `serve.go` call — a noul batch on the order of ~160 candidate claims), so `rerankWithinTiers` DID run and DID call the Decider; the failure is that a batch this large did not get valid JSON back from `openai/gpt-6-luna` over the kit's `llm` adapter. That is a NEW, distinct, unresolved problem — a decide-call reliability/batch-size issue in the `llm` adapter, not a fathom wiring bug — out of this wave's scope to fix.
- **Fix (chunked, verified live, 2026-09-22, W-H):** DP-22/DP-23 (`docs/plans/decision-port.md` §G). Root cause per W-G's own follow-up above: the re-rank sent the WHOLE matched-claim batch (~150-160 candidates) as one `decision.Request` to the kit's `llm` adapter, and a reply that large failed to parse as JSON — the whole `Decide` call errored, so `rerankWithinTiers` fell straight into its swallowed-error path and nothing ranked, nothing logged. Fix: `decisionBatchSize` (`internal/semantic/decisionbatch.go`) gives `PrefilterSections` and `rerankWithinTiers` one shared chunk-size rule (a Decider's own `MaxQuestions()` when it implements that optional interface, 20 for a Decider named `llm`, unbounded otherwise); `rerankWithinTiers` now splits the noul batch into chunks of that size and runs them CONCURRENTLY (bounded worker pool of 4), merging answers/records deterministically and leaving a failed chunk's own rules at their exact pre-rerank position rather than poisoning the whole call. `(*decide.Recorder).RecordError` (DP-23) gives every hard-failing Decide call — the rerank's own failed chunks, the compile pre-filter's aborted run, `POST /route`'s decider error — a `decisions.jsonl` row (`site`, `provider`, `questions`, `latency_ms`, `error`, `applied:false`) instead of vanishing silently.

  Live rerun (same read-only backup store, `service fathom @ a7b982c4`, `$SP/or-live/store.sqlite`, unchanged; scratch daemon `127.0.0.1:7678`, `--auto-refresh=false`; `[decide] provider = "llm"`, OpenRouter `openai/gpt-6-luna`; binary built from this wave's own fix): the SAME two probe files as W-G's rerun.

  | file | ranked_by | rank_note | rules served | relevance-carrying | latency | order vs `none` baseline |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `internal/semantic/serve.go` | `"llm"` | (empty — all 8 chunks succeeded) | 8 | 6 (noul 0.99 each) | 29.4s | changed (genuinely reranked) |
  | `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go` | `"llm"` | `"1 of 8 chunks failed: decision: llm response is not JSON: unexpected end of JSON input"` | 8 | 6 (noul 1.0 each) | 28.5s | changed (genuinely reranked) |

  `decisions.jsonl` gained 288 new `"site":"obligations"` rows (147 → 435) — the very rows O109's original finding said would NEVER appear (zero, across every prior probe) — plus one new `error` row (`site:"obligations"`, `provider:"llm"`, `model:""`, `questions:20`, `latency_ms:17696`, `error:"decision: llm response is not JSON: unexpected end of JSON input"`, `applied:false`) naming the one chunk (of 16 total across both calls) that still failed — visible in the append-only log now, not only in a transient wire `rank_note`. OpenRouter real spend rose $0.031778 → $0.038864 (delta $0.007086, 0.709¢) for both calls together — comparable to W-G's $0.005851 for two calls that produced ZERO ranking, this time for two calls where 15 of 16 chunks succeeded and both files' served order genuinely changed from the `provider="none"` baseline (confirmed by a same-store, same-file `none`-provider rerun immediately after: every one of the top 6 rules differs in position between the two, both files). Daemon killed after the run; `or-live/.fathom/config.toml`'s `[decide] provider` restored to `"none"` (unchanged from how W-G left it) and `store.sqlite` untouched (not rebuilt).
- **Fix (bounded, verified live, 2026-09-22, W-I):** DP-24/DP-25 (`docs/plans/decision-port.md` §G). W-H's own chunked fix (above) closed "nothing ranks" but not "one `obligations` call takes 28-29s" — 8 chunks × ~4 concurrent workers against `openai/gpt-6-luna`, asking about EVERY scope-eligible claim (~160) when `MaxObligations` (6) can only ever serve a handful, and the PostToolUse edit hook's own obligations RPC carries a flat 2000ms HTTP client timeout (`cmd/fathom/edithook.go`'s `fetchObligations`) that a 28s daemon reply loses outright, rendering NOTHING ("daemon down or error → silent"). Two independent fixes: DP-24 (`rerankEligible`) asks the Decider only about claims whose governing-scope tier intersects the served window `[0, max)` in the pre-rerank lexical order — a tier wholly below the cut is never asked, since no ranking of it can change what is served; `ranked_count`/`candidates_total` disclose the effect on the wire. DP-25 (`rerankWithinTiers` under one shared `context.WithTimeout`) bounds the WHOLE rerank call (every chunk together) at `[decide] rerank_timeout_ms` (default 1500ms — deliberately under the edit hook's 2000ms ceiling with 500ms margin, so a daemon-side rerank can never itself cause that hook's silent-nothing outcome); a chunk still in flight at the deadline is cancelled through ctx and reported exactly like any other hard-failed chunk (DP-22's existing degrade path), and the response returns promptly rather than blocking on a slow decider.

  Live rerun (same read-only backup store, `service fathom @ a7b982c4`, `$SP/or-live/store.sqlite`, unchanged; scratch daemon `127.0.0.1:7679`, `--auto-refresh=false`; this wave's own binary) against the SAME two probe files W-G/W-H used, two passes:

  | pass | file | ranked_by | rank_note | ranked_count / candidates_total | relevance-carrying | wall time |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | (a) default `rerank_timeout_ms` (1500ms) | `internal/semantic/serve.go` | `"none"` | `"decision budget 1500ms exceeded: 1 of 1 chunks unanswered"` | 10 / 160 | 0 | 2.11s |
  | (a) default `rerank_timeout_ms` (1500ms) | `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go` | `"none"` | `"decision budget 1500ms exceeded: 8 of 8 chunks unanswered"` | 142 / 147 | 0 | 1.73s |
  | (b) `rerank_timeout_ms = 20000` | `internal/semantic/serve.go` | `"llm"` | (empty — 1 of 1 chunks succeeded) | 10 / 160 | 6 (noul 0.99 each) | 4.50s |
  | (b) `rerank_timeout_ms = 20000` | `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go` | `"llm"` | `"decision budget 20000ms exceeded: 2 of 8 chunks unanswered"` | 142 / 147 | 6 (noul 1.0 each) | 20.73s |

  Pass (a) proves the bound itself: both calls return in ~2s — comfortably inside the edit hook's 2000ms ceiling — versus W-H's unbounded 28-29s for the same two files, at the cost of `ranked_by:"none"` (the rerank never got a chance to answer; rules still served, in lexical order). Pass (b) proves DP-24's narrowing directly: `serve.go` asks about only 10 of 160 candidates (a single chunk, genuinely ranked in 4.5s — 6.5× faster than W-H's 29.4s for the SAME file, because W-H asked about all 160); `servehttp.go` asks about 142 of 147 (its claims are mostly `root`-tier per `displaced_by_tier`, so DP-24's window intersects nearly all of them — the fix narrows only what the window can genuinely exclude, never more) and still finishes at 20.73s, right at its budget, with 2 of 8 chunks honestly disclosed as unanswered rather than either blocking further or silently vanishing. `decisions.jsonl` gained 141 new `"site":"obligations"` rows (435 → 576): 130 `applied:true` (genuine answers) and 11 `applied:false` error rows, each naming the real cause (`"decision: llm completion: server error 200: openai: reading response body: context deadline exceeded"` — DP-25's cancellation reaching the kit's `llm` adapter through ctx, exactly as designed) rather than vanishing. OpenRouter real spend rose $0.050145 → $0.050427 (delta $0.000282, 0.0282¢) for all four calls together — far below W-H's $0.007086 for two calls, consistent with DP-24 asking far fewer questions on the `serve.go` side and DP-25 cutting off unproductive in-flight work on both. Daemon killed after the run; `or-live/.fathom/config.toml`'s `[decide]` block restored to `provider = "none"` (the `rerank_timeout_ms = 20000` override removed) and `store.sqlite` untouched (not rebuilt).

- **Status:** closed 2026-09-22 (W-H; bound verified live by W-I) — the re-rank now genuinely ranks (order changes vs. the `none` baseline, confirmed live on both original probe files) and genuinely logs (288 new `obligations`-site rows in W-H's run, plus the one still-failing chunk named as an `error` row rather than silent). The residual per-chunk failure rate against `openai/gpt-6-luna` (1 of 16 chunks in W-H's run) is the `llm` adapter's own reliability at a 20-question batch, not a fathom wiring gap — DP-22 was designed for exactly this: a chunk that still fails degrades to "this chunk's rules keep their lexical order, and the failure is logged," never to "the whole call silently produces nothing," which is what O109 originally found. W-I closes the remaining half of O109's own title ("one obligations call takes 28-29s"): DP-24/DP-25 together give the port a hard latency ceiling under the PostToolUse edit hook's own budget, and confine the LLM lane to asking only what the served window can act on.
- **Impact:** everything the decision port's own docs promise about disclosure (`ranked_by`, `rank_note`, `relevance`, `prefilter_skipped` — DP-6, DP-8, `docs/mcp-tools.md`) is invisible to any real client driving the daemon over its actual wire protocol, despite the underlying Go structs carrying it correctly and the plan rows that claim it being marked done; an operator or agent reading `obligations`/`compile_layer` responses to understand whether/how the decision port influenced what they were served has no way to tell. Routing itself worked well when the connection was warm (0.91–1.0 confidence, correct verb all 3 times) but a fixed 3s budget starved half of this small real sample under ordinary OpenRouter latency for a non-Jev model — not a routing-vocabulary problem, a budget-vs-reality one. Total measured cost for the whole exercise: $0.0216 (2.16¢); the tool's own compile-cost estimate would have led an operator to expect roughly 4× that. W-H's chunked fix closes the loop: the re-rank now genuinely reaches a verdict (rather than reliably failing on a large batch), and both a partial-chunk failure and a compile pre-filter/route decider failure now leave a durable, queryable trace in `decisions.jsonl` instead of depending on catching a transient wire response.

### O108 — The always-on daemon's memory footprint reached 12 GB (11 GB compressed) after 9.4 h serving 21 typed-edge services; swap filled and the pre-push gate was killed twice

- **Symptom:** two gated pushes of round 3 (2026-09-22) were terminated by the harness's low-memory watchdog inside the first minutes of the pre-push hook (`task: Signal received: "terminated"`, exit 143), at `-p=2` and again at `-p=1`; nothing landed on `main`.
- **Evidence:** `top -o cmprs`: `fathom serve-http` pid 85420 (v0.8.0 @ c58adaa2, launchd) MEM 12 G / CMPRS 11 G, the largest process on the machine ahead of every browser helper; `ps` RSS at the same moment 623 MB (the rest paged out); `vm.swapusage` 32 GB total / 31.99 GB used; uptime 33,898 s; `/statusz` 21 services with typed edges (agentic-kit alone 392 packages); `~/.fathom/logs/fathom-http.err` 47 auto-refresh lines in the file. `/metrics` carries no memory or goroutine series and there is no `/debug/pprof`, so the growth could not be attributed from outside.
- **Root cause:** not conclusively isolated, despite instrumenting the daemon (`/metrics` gains `go_memstats_heap_inuse_bytes`/`_heap_sys_bytes`/`_sys_bytes`, `go_goroutines`, `process_uptime_seconds`, `fathom_graph_memo_entries`/`_hits_total`/`_misses_total`, `fathom_open_stores`, `fathom_autorefresh_total`, `fathom_typed_pass_total`; `/debug/pprof/*` mounted loopback-only) and reproducing the daemon's operating conditions with six synthetic drivers, four against a scratch daemon over a read-only backup of the shared store (docs/plans/daemon-footprint.md §B): (1) 30 sequential auto-refresh cycles on a tiny repo; (2) LRU-eviction churn round-robining 19 real fleet services (agentic-kit, fathom itself, 17 others) across 6 passes — well past the graph memo's cap of 8 entries, forcing real evictions every touch; (3) 3000 pure `obligations` calls with no ingest at all, isolating call VOLUME (the PostToolUse edit-hook's own highest-frequency call, across every concurrent agent session) as a variable; (4) 25 REAL typed-edge re-ingests (not just cache rebuilds) of a synthetic 41-package Go service — closer to a real service's package count than any prior driver. Two more ran in-process (no HTTP, no daemon) at a scale no HTTP driver could reach in a reasonable time, against 10k-node graphs (comparable to this repo's own ~10.6k symbols): 300 sequential misses across 15 services, and a 50-round CONCURRENT "thundering herd" of 12 simultaneous misses on the same (service, watermark) key — the daemon's own real condition (many parallel agent sessions sharing one daemon) that every sequential driver missed; `Graph()`'s `build()` call runs outside the lock by design (cache.go's own comment: "a concurrent caller may have inserted the same key while build ran"), so concurrent misses for the same key each run a full redundant build before only one is published. **None of the six reproduced sustained, unbounded growth.** `go_memstats_heap_inuse_bytes` (HTTP drivers) and `runtime.MemStats.HeapInuse` (in-process drivers) consistently plateaued or oscillated within a bounded band (~1.1–1.7x of its post-warmup level, well inside a 2x margin) once the graph memo's 8 entries filled, across every driver including the concurrent one. `fathom_graph_memo_entries` never exceeded 8 in any run. This empirically rules out every named candidate: the watermark-keyed call-graph memo (bounded and correctly evicting — Go's slice-capacity mechanics happen to make the LRU's re-slice-based eviction safe, not the leak the "no eviction" prior assumed), the typed pass (`go/packages` results are function-local and do not survive `EnrichTypedEdges` returning), store handles (`fathom_open_stores` held at 1 throughout every driver), the call logger (bounded per-surface maps, no leak). Goroutines stayed flat (5) across all drivers. **What every driver reproduced without exception**, and the one signature this incident's own numbers already named: `go_memstats_heap_sys_bytes` (what the Go runtime has reserved from the OS) is a high-water mark that never decreases, even while `heap_inuse` (live reachable bytes) correctly drops after each churn burst — exactly "12 GB (11 GB compressed) MEM, 623 MB RSS, the rest paged out": memory that was touched once (a large graph build, a burst of concurrent misses) and is no longer live, but that Go's own lazy, low-priority background scavenger had not yet returned to the OS, so macOS held it inactive and compressed rather than free. A 24/7 daemon accumulates these peaks all day; none of this round's drivers ran anywhere near 9.4 hours, so whether heap_sys's climb over a REAL day's many peaks alone reaches 12 GB, or some other mechanism outside this round's candidate list contributes too, is still open.
  - **2026-09-23 production confirmation (W-L, W-N):** the deployed daemon, with W-L's scavenger live, gave the first direct evidence THIS round's synthetic drivers could not: after one typed-edge pass it sat at `heap_inuse` 10 MB but `heap_sys` 753 MB and RSS 765 MB — a single pass, not sustained operation, already produces the gap the incident's own 12 GB/623 MB numbers showed. The scavenger closed it from the OS's own point of view: `top -pid <pid> -stats mem,cmprs` read 44 MB with nothing compressed after the next scavenge pass, while `ps` RSS kept reporting 765 MB the whole time — `ps` counts `MADV_FREE`'d pages as resident until the kernel actually reclaims them, so `ps rss` is not the right instrument to judge the scavenger by; `top -stats mem,cmprs` (or `/metrics` `go_memstats_heap_sys_bytes`) is. Separately, a same-day session issued 19 `ingest` calls against `app-kit` (≈390 packages) in 20 minutes while the daemon's footprint grew 1.4 → 5.2 GB — concurrent/rapid-fire typed-edge PASSES over a large service, not steady-state serving, is the growth driver. This reframes root cause from "unattributed growth, six candidates ruled out" to: the growth is real, idle heap left behind by PEAKS (large/concurrent typed-edge passes) that darwin reclaims lazily (compresses, later swaps) rather than immediately — exactly what the scavenger already targets, and exactly what W-N (`docs/plans/daemon-footprint.md` §D) now also bounds at the source by capping how large and how concurrent a peak can get.
- **Fix:** (1) instrumentation, unconditional (`/metrics` gauges, `/debug/pprof`, `fathom doctor`'s `daemon_footprint` row — DF-1/2/3); (2) `runMemoryScavenger` (`cmd/fathom/memscavenge.go`) calls `debug.FreeOSMemory` on a fixed cadence (default 15 min, `FATHOM_SCAVENGE_INTERVAL` to tune, `FATHOM_SCAVENGE=off` to disable) instead of relying on Go's own lazy scavenger (DF-5) — this is a defensible mitigation DIRECTED BY the one evidence-backed signature (heap_sys never decreasing), not a fix for a located leak; (3) two regression tests (`internal/analysis/callgraph/cache_memory_test.go`, DF-6/DF-7) pin the memo's boundedness under sequential AND concurrent heavy churn at a scale beyond this incident's own rate, closing off the graph-memo hypothesis for good. The 2026-09-22 incident itself was already resolved operationally (daemon restarted the way `task deploy` restarts it; RSS 33 MB at boot, free memory 51% → 71%). **W-N (2026-09-23, `docs/plans/daemon-footprint.md` §D)** bounds the PEAKS the 09-23 evidence named, rather than only cleaning up after them: (4) a process-wide semaphore (`acquireTypedPassSlot`, `internal/fusion/typedpass_serial.go`) serializes the typed-edge pass — two large services (an `app-kit`-sized one included) can never type-check concurrently, which is exactly the condition the 19-calls-in-20-minutes/1.4→5.2 GB reading caught; a waiter is counted (`fathom_typed_pass_waited_total`) and the daemon's current concurrency is visible (`fathom_typed_pass_inflight`) (DF-8/9/10/11); (5) `[daemon] memory_limit` (default `"3GiB"`, `FATHOM_MEMORY_LIMIT` override, `"off"` to disable) applies a SOFT ceiling with `runtime.SetMemoryLimit` at startup — the runtime GCs and scavenges harder as usage approaches it, capping how large any single peak's heap is allowed to grow before the scavenger even gets a scheduled turn (DF-13..18); `go_memory_limit_bytes` on `/metrics` and doctor's `daemon_footprint` row both name the live limit.
- **Status:** mitigated-and-bounded (was: open — mitigated, not closed). The 2026-09-23 production reading confirms the mechanism (peaks + darwin's lazy reclamation, not a live leak) and confirms the scavenger closes the OS-visible gap it targets; W-N adds two bounds on the peaks themselves (serialized typed passes, a soft runtime memory limit) so the SIZE and CONCURRENCY of a peak are both capped, not just its lifetime. Not claimed fully closed: the scavenger's own +9h production confirmation (this observation's prior closure bar — re-reading `top -o cmprs` / `go_memstats_heap_sys_bytes` against a deployed daemon's own value at start, sustained across a real day) is still pending a sampler run; the 2026-09-23 reading above is a single-pass snapshot, not that +9h curve. Close only after the sampler confirms the curve stays flat across a full production day with all three mitigations (scavenger + semaphore + memory limit) live.
- **Correction (2026-09-27, RT-138):** superseded by INC-23 (docs/intent/incidents.md). "Mitigated-and-bounded" rested on `heap_sys`, which never falls by definition (0 of 193 scavenge lines showed it falling by more than 1 MiB), and on no invariant, test or decision. The same root cause, request-path work proportional to repository and fleet size multiplied by concurrency, took the machine four days later at about 29 GB. The footprint instrument is now `phys_footprint` (`fathom resources`), and the bound is ADR-047: the budget and ladder, cost-classed admission, and the cost and stampede tests (I47-I53).
- **Impact:** a machine with the daemon on it lost the ability to run its own gate; every other agent session on the machine paid the swap cost silently for hours.

### O107 — The findings-loop corpus cannot separate the projection from the prompt: its prompts already imply the finding's remedy

- **Symptom:** findings-loop-r4 (5 × 2 × 3, the first valid run): `finding-addressed` held on 9 of 12 cells on BOTH arms — with the projected finding served at the edit (full) and with the outbox removed (control) — and the overall pass rate (2/15 vs 3/15) turned on the tasks' own test oracles, not on the finding.
- **Evidence:** run findings-loop-r4 detector tallies per arm; the corpus's own derivation text ("the prompt is an ordinary small ask whose correct completion also satisfies the finding's own remedy text").
- **Root cause:** a prompt that subsumes the remedy makes the finding redundant information; the treatment has nothing to add that the prompt did not already say.
- **Fix:** not applied this round. The next corpus revision needs prompts that do NOT name the remedy (an ordinary neighbouring change in the same file) so that only the served finding can steer the edit — the vision-30 claim is "the finding reaches the agent at the edit", measurable only when the prompt is silent about it.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** three rounds of instrument fixes bought one valid run that measures nothing about the projection; the ledger row is filed as a null with this note.

### O106 — findings-loop-r3 read all 30 cells as violations for real: the corpus's allow-list contradicted the subject's own doctrine

- **Symptom:** after O105, `no-unrelated-rewrite` fired on every cell of both arms with no baseline note — the sessions had genuinely edited files outside the allow-list: `docs/plans/<slug>.md` (the fathom repo's own plan-first rule for a change task), `cmd/fathom/purge_cmd_test.go` (the repo's real test file; the corpus allowed a `purgecmd_test.go` that does not exist), `configaudit_test.go` beside `doctor_concerns.go`.
- **Evidence:** run findings-loop-r3 cells (`agent_diff_path` file lists; `oracle.detectors[].note` empty); `finding-addressed` passed 9 of 15 on each arm, `change-present` 15 of 15.
- **Root cause:** the corpus author named the one file a finding points at and one guessed test file, and forgot that the subject's CLAUDE.md tells every session to write a plan and a change record first — the detector measured obedience to the subject's doctrine as a violation.
- **Fix:** each task's allow-list admits any `_test.go` in the named source's directory, a plan under `docs/plans/`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `.fathom/observations.md`; everything else still fails the detector. Rerun as findings-loop-r4.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** findings-loop-r3's 30 cells are void (not filed); three rounds of this corpus have now been consumed by the instrument, none by the treatment.

### O105 — findings-loop-r2 read every cell as a violation: the instrument's own files were in the detector's baseline diff

- **Symptom:** findings-loop-r2 (5 × 2 × 3): 0/30 pass on both arms with `no-unrelated-rewrite` "baseline_red: this detector already exited 1 before the session ran" on every cell, while `finding-addressed` and `change-present` passed on most.
- **Evidence:** cell records run findings-loop-r2 (`oracle.detectors[].note`); the detector runs `git diff --name-only $SCENARIO_BASE_COMMIT` and PrepareSubject makes the root commit BEFORE fathom init, RenderArm and the arm's setup write .claude/settings.json, the stanza and .mcp.json.
- **Root cause:** the recorded agent.diff excluded instrument-owned paths (`InstrumentFilesExcluded`) but a corpus detector's own git diff did not; any "no unrelated changes" detector was red at baseline on a wired cell.
- **Fix:** `absorbInstrumentFiles` commits everything the instrument wrote after the root as one commit ABOVE it and points LocalBase there, before the drift step (which stays visible by design); `TestAbsorbInstrumentFiles`. The first cut amended the root, so a shared worktree's reset-to-root carried one cell's arm setup into every later cell and a corpus setup that appends `[findings]` to `.fathom/config.toml` appended it twice — the scratch daemon refused the duplicate table and findings-loop-r3 lost 28 of 30 cells before the commit-above-root form landed.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** findings-loop-r2's 30 cells are void (not filed); the corpus's arms are both wired by design (control = the same surface with the imported findings removed), so the treatment is the projected findings, not the surface.

### O104 — H1-T04's oracle demanded grep's vocabulary ("not found") and failed the wired arm for stating the same fact in verify's own label ("claimed-but-absent")

- **Symptom:** lookups-vs-r1: full 0/2 on H1-T04 while grep 2/2. Both arms named DEMO-H1 as the failing row and said the symbol is absent; the wired arm reached the answer through `find_symbol` + `verify_plan` and quoted the gate's own verdict label, `claimed-but-absent`; the oracle's `answer_all_of` required the literal "not found".
- **Evidence:** cells lookups-vs-r1 H1-T04 full/t1,t2 (final answers), grep/t1,t2; `testdata/scenariobench/lookups-vs.json`.
- **Root cause:** an oracle token that encodes one tool's phrasing of a fact, not the fact — the corpus-parity rule (O53) applied to answers, not only to instructions.
- **Fix:** the oracle keeps `DEMO-H1` as all-of and accepts any of "not found" / "claimed-but-absent" / "does not exist" / "no such symbol" / "absent" / "no match" (a spelling-equivalent group for ONE fact). Rows filed this round stay as measured under the old oracle (the corpus hash is part of the cell key); the next run scores under the fixed one.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** one of the four wired-arm misses on the home lookup corpus this round is a vocabulary mismatch, not a wrong answer; the paired reading in docs/bench/readings names it.

### O103 — findings-loop-r1 lost all 30 cells at arm rendering: the subject's own committed bare `fathom …` hooks

- **Symptom:** every findings-loop cell LOST with "hook command \"fathom edit-hook\" names a bare binary on PATH, not the cell's own fathom binary" — the fathom repo at the corpus's base commit (687ce6a9) commits `.claude/settings.json` rows that call `fathom` through the PATH shim; `fathom init` wrote its own absolute rows beside them and RenderArm refused the bare ones.
- **Evidence:** `$SP/benchroot/logs/findings-loop-r1.log` (30 × LOST, 0 run); `git show 687ce6a9:.claude/settings.json`.
- **Root cause:** the driver assumed every `fathom` hook row in a wired cell came from its own `fathom init`; a subject that is itself a fathom adopter (the dogfooding corpora) ships more.
- **Fix:** `rewriteBareFathomHooks` runs after `fathom init` in wireScratchDaemon: a command whose first token is exactly `fathom` becomes the cell binary + the same arguments; absolute and foreign commands stay byte-identical (`TestRewriteBareFathomHooks`). The subject's hooks now run the cell's own code, which is what the refusal protected.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** any corpus whose subject commits PATH-shim fathom hooks (guards-fathom at a newer vintage would too) was unrunnable; the refusal was right, the remedy was missing.

### O102 — On the LCA bug-localization corpus the wired arm never called `localize`, and half its cells never touched the surface at all

- **Symptom:** lca-r1 (39 tasks × 3 trials, Sonnet, 2026-09-21): fathom 104/115 vs control 105/116 on hit@1 (3 tasks better, 2 worse, 34 tied) at 2.3× the tokens (74k vs 32k median). `localize` was called in 0 of 115 wired cells; 58 of 115 made no fathom call at all; the rest used find_symbol (43), search (30), read_source (24).
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** scenariobench cells, `readings.tool_calls` per cell; `docs/bench/scenarios-ledger.jsonl` run lca-r1
  - **Expected:** an issue-shaped prompt (title + body, no file named) is exactly `localize`'s question — one bounded walk from the error's own terms.
  - **Actual:** nothing in the wired session routed that prompt shape to localize: the session brief and the stanza's opening moves named where / who-calls / what-breaks / what-governs / is-this-still-true, and "debug/trace" began with `blast_radius`. The model fell back to grep and Read, paying the surface's context tax for no answer it used.
- **Root cause:** the routing vocabulary had no entry for the bug-report shape; the verb existed, the route did not.
- **Fix:** the SessionStart brief and the stanza's opening-moves table route "a bug report, stack trace, error string or failing test" to `localize` first (`sessionhook.go` ctxLine, `openingMovesTable`, the committed CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md stanza line). Measured by lca-r2.
- **Status:** open — closes when lca-r2 shows the verb used and hit@1 or tokens move.
- **Impact:** every VB-27 null so far measured the surface's passive cost, not its localization answer.

### O101 — The agent-surface `tools/list` schema tax cost ~10k tokens/turn: `outputSchema` (39% of the payload) rode the wire unconditionally and every description still carried fl-wave-10's 700/1000-byte allowance

- **Symptom:** on the lookups-ts and lookups-py bench scenarios the wired arm's sessions carried a median 15,219 cache-read tokens PER TURN versus 5,165 for the grep arm — a ~10k-token fixed context tax on every turn, the reason fathom's arm cost 2.6× the tokens on 3-turn tasks while answering the same or better.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** the agent surface's live `tools/list` payload (`POST /rpc/agent`, 25 tools), captured to `workspace/tools-list-before.json` and measured byte-for-byte.
  - **Expected:** the schema tax every MCP-connected session pays before its first call should track what a Claude-Code-shaped client actually consumes (no ToolSearch deferral observed on this surface — `toolsearch_turns = 0` in every cell — so bytes served are tokens paid, every turn).
  - **Actual:** 48,341 bytes: descriptions 11,457 B, `inputSchema` 13,589 B, generated `outputSchema` 18,827 B (39%) — `outputSchema` served unconditionally on every `tools/list` call regardless of whether the client validates `structuredContent` against it, and fl-wave-10's per-tool description cap (700 B, 1000 B for four composed tools) was never revisited once the four ADR-035 verbs and `localize` joined the surface.
- **Root cause:** two independent costs stacked with no gate on either: (1) `outputSchema` had no on/off switch — `toolsListEntries` (`internal/mcp/dispatch.go`) attached it to every entry unconditionally; (2) the 700/1000-byte description caps were sized to "fits the RP-9 grammar," never to "fits a measured per-turn token budget."
- **Fix:** `outputSchema` moved behind a request-scoped flag (`internal/mcp/surface.go`'s `WithContracts`/`contractsFrom`), default `false` — `toolsListEntries` only attaches it when the request asked, via the HTTP `?contracts=true` query param (`cmd/fathom/servehttp.go`); the registry, the generated schemas, and `structuredContent` on `tools/call` are unchanged. Every agent-surface `Description` recut to a flat 260-byte cap (no per-tool tiers) and every `inputSchema` property description to 120 bytes (`surface_description_caps_test.go`); alias/history prose dropped from argument descriptions, ported to `docs/mcp-tools.md` where not already there (obligations' claim-feedback args, `verify_plan`'s `package`/`concern`). New budget test `internal/mcp/surface_budget_test.go` (`TestAgentSurfaceBudget`) asserts total/per-tool/per-description bytes together against the served (contracts=false) payload and logs the per-tool table.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-21)
- **Impact:** measured 48,341 → 18,008 bytes (62.7% cut) on the served default. The brief's own ≤16,000 B / ≤1,200 B-per-tool aspiration is not fully reached: annotations (~2,227 B, RP-10, unconditionally served, not in this wave's scope) plus `inputSchema` argument-schema STRUCTURE (~4,900 B, golden-pinned by `TestAgentSurfaceArgSchemaUnchanged`, not in this wave's scope) plus JSON wire punctuation (~3,600 B across 25 tool envelopes) form a ~10.7 kB floor a description/param diet alone cannot cross — the same shape fl-wave-10 documented for its own ≤36,000 B aspiration (O85). Every per-tool cap (1,200 B) and every description/property cap (260/120 B) IS met; only the aggregate total sits at 18,008 rather than ≤16,000.

### O113 — The decision-port A/B (guards-jev-r1): the obligations within-tier re-rank measured net negative once a real provider is configured — default flipped off

- **Symptom:** three trials=2 A/B runs (lca-jev-r1, lookups-vs-jev-r1, guards-jev-r1 — the wired arm vs the same arm plus `[decide] provider=typesafe`, Jev 1.13 live via OpenRouter, DP-13/DP-14): routing showed a real small positive (lca localize gold-source rate 4.6%→23.4%, paired mean +0.047, bootstrap 95% CI [0.000,0.125], 2 better/0 worse; lookups-vs null, mean 0.000, CI [-0.107,0.107]). guards-jev-r1 — the obligations within-tier re-rank site — regressed: paired mean -0.19 (CI [-0.375,-0.062]), 3 tasks worse (`guards-i19-org-agnostic-fixture` 50%→0%, `guards-i28-duckdb-path` 50%→0%, `guards-i31-workspace-artifact` 100%→50%), 0 better, 5 tied, at n=2 trials.
- **Evidence:** `docs/bench/scenarios-ledger.jsonl` (run ids `lca-jev-r1`/`guards-jev-r1`/`lookups-vs-jev-r1`, 109 rows); full analysis and per-task mechanism table `docs/bench/readings/decision-port-ab-2026-09-23.md`. The obligations-site decision log across the 8 guards tasks' full-jev cells: 15,742 decisions, 15,699 applied (43 errors), p50 337ms/p95 1,075ms latency — the re-rank ran and answered on nearly every eligible rule, at real cost.
- **Root cause:** traced in raw transcripts (per-task obligations-block extraction, both arms, both trials) for the 3 regressed tasks. Only `guards-i28-duckdb-path` has a directly traced re-rank mechanism: the governing rule (I28/ADR-025 DuckDB loud refusal) served at position 2 of 6 in the deterministic arm's own FIRST touch of `internal/store` (position 1 there is a different, superseded rule, ADR-020, that only shares the keyword "DuckDB"); the full-jev arm's own FIRST touch never serves the governing rule at all, in either trial — worse than the deterministic arm from the start, not identical to it — and its own position-1 slot is yet another DuckDB-adjacent but unrelated rule (ADR-003). On later touches of the same file within the same session, full-jev's re-rank does surface the rule, but buried at the last visible slot (position 6 of 6), and on a further touch in one trial the rule is absent again — a live, per-edit LLM reorder that never puts the governing rule in a good position, while the deterministic baseline arm never needed a later touch. `guards-i19-org-agnostic-fixture` and `guards-i31-workspace-artifact` show a DIFFERENT, pre-existing pattern: the governing rule (I19/ADR-011, I31 workspace/ paths) was never served in EITHER arm at any touch of the target file — a baseline obligations-coverage gap the re-rank cannot be blamed for on this evidence; whatever else caused those two tasks' extra failed trial under jev is not explained by rule-serving alone.
- **Fix:** `internal/config.DefaultDecide.RankWithinTier` default flipped `true`→`false` (ADR-042 amended 2026-09-23, ADR.md and `docs/intent/decisions/README.md`; `docs/vision/32-decision-port.md` §4 and status line; `docs/configuration.md`'s `[decide]` table; `cmd/fathom init`'s commented `[decide]` block; `CHANGELOG.md` [Unreleased]) — a decision provider configured for routing/compile no longer reranks obligations within a tier unless `rank_within_tier = true` is set explicitly. Routing and the compile pre-filter are unchanged by this fix.
- **Status:** closed (2026-09-23) — default changed and proven (`internal/config` tests green). Open follow-up (named in the ADR's revisit trigger): a guards-shaped rerun at n ≥ 3 trials, and a corpus with two governing rules genuinely competing within one scope tier — this A/B's 8 guards tasks never had that contest to arbitrate, so a positive case for the re-rank was never actually tested.
- **Impact:** anyone who sets `[decide] provider = llm | typesafe` for routing or the compile pre-filter no longer silently inherits an unreviewed obligations re-rank on upgrade; the mechanism (a live per-edit LLM reorder of the served rules) is confirmed real and, on this sample, more likely to displace a governing rule later in a session than to help.

### O112 — The 2026-09-23 diagnostics sweep: no daemon logging had timestamps, one stale-service line was 97% of the error log, and typed-edge recall silently dropped selector/interface-dispatch calls

- **Symptom:** The 2026-09-23 diagnostics sweep: no daemon logging had timestamps, one stale-service line was 97% of the error log, and typed-edge recall silently dropped selector/interface-dispatch calls
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** four parallel investigator passes (`logs`, `fleet`, `logging`, `backlog`) against a live daemon and its call-log, synthesized in `diag-plan.md` against the pushclone checkout at `959bddff`
  - **Expected:** daemon diagnostic output is timestamped and attributable; a service whose recorded corpus root has vanished is reported once, structurally, not re-discovered as a bare error on every read; `references`/`spec_refs` resolve against the SAME verified identity `obligations` and `verify_plan` use; a selector-method call (value or pointer receiver) and an interface-dispatch call are typed-confirmed, not dropped
  - **Actual:** zero hits for `log.New(`/`log.SetFlags`/`log/slog` anywhere in `cmd/fathom`/`internal/fusion` outside `third_party/agentic-kit`'s unrelated telemetry bridge — every diagnostic line was a bare, unattributable `fmt.Fprintf(stderr/stdout, ...)` (DG-1..DG-7's shared root cause); `fleetAdapter.UnsupportedLanguagesFor` re-walked a service's filesystem (`client.Sources` → `connector.Discover`) on every single call with no cache, confirmed by three independent investigators as 97%+ of the entire daemon error log, and independently measured as `list_services`'s (the fleet's most-called MCP tool, ~31k calls) live cost: p95 4.6s, max 182s (DG-3/DG-18); SQLite CANTOPEN bursts (errno 14, a transient TCC-verdict wedge or a genuinely deleted path) were logged identically to a permanent failure with no distinguishing tag (DG-17); `renderPostEdit`'s candidate loop let a later, unverified basename/git-toplevel guess overwrite an earlier daemon-verified identity once `list_services`'s own latency exceeded the hook's 2s round-trip budget — the O15 failure shape, DG-11/DG-12 — and the sweep's fleet/backlog investigators additionally reported `spec_refs` resolving through that unverified fallback identity on a majority (reported ~55%) of sampled calls before this fix (investigator-reported per diag-plan.md's own live-numbers provenance note; not independently re-measured against the live fleet in this integration pass — DG-11/DG-12's regression tests prove the mechanism is closed, not that historical percentage)
  - **Repro:** `diag-plan.md` §§0-7 (root-cause citations file:line against the pushclone checkout); this plan's own `docs/plans/diagnostics-sweep.md` rows for the closing regression tests
- **Root cause:** six independent gaps, all present before this sweep: (1) no leveled/timestamped logger existed anywhere in the daemon, so every stderr line was both unattributable to wall-clock time and undeduped, letting one condition dominate the log; (2) `fleetAdapter.UnsupportedLanguagesFor`/`pkg/client.Client.Sources` re-walked a service's filesystem on every `list_services` call with no cache and no distinction between "root recorded and reachable" and "root recorded but gone"; (3) a post-edit identity resolution raced `list_services`'s own uncached latency against a hardcoded 2s budget, and on timeout let an unverified basename guess silently overwrite an already-daemon-verified identity (O15's shape recurring); (4) a typed-edge loading failure or discovery gap was visible only as an unstructured stderr line, never carried on `TypedEdgeStatus` for `/statusz`/`doctor` to read, and did not survive a daemon restart; (5) `doctor`'s `binary_fresh` and `unproven_claims` rows discarded information they already computed (which side of HEAD is behind; the evidence timestamp); (6) `internal/analysis/callgraph.MentionConfidence`'s one-hop, byFile-keyed confidence lookup was suspected (O39/O40) to drop selector-method and interface-dispatch calls — DG-21's fixture-driven repro against the real `fusion.Pipeline.Ingest` → `EnrichTypedEdges` → `MentionConfidence` path proved the typed pass and confidence matching are themselves sound for every tested shape (value/pointer receiver via selector, interface dispatch), narrowing O39's remaining open lead to `references_tool.go`'s exact-mode filter comparing a byFile map built at ingest time against a possibly-differently-normalized service root resolved fresh per query (same DG-3/DG-18 root-cause family, different symptom) — recorded as still-open in O39, not closed by this sweep.
- **Fix:** DG-1/DG-2 (`38d8477d`) — `internal/dlog`, a leveled/timestamped `log/slog` wrapper with dedupe-and-count. DG-3 (`7b730925`) — `fleetAdapter` routes through the deduped logger and stops re-walking a missing corpus root. DG-4/DG-5/DG-6/DG-16/DG-19 (`8138a32e`, `9f2153aa`) — remaining call sites threaded through `dlog`; HTTP access log with `X-Fathom-Request-Id`; log rotation; corrected live resident-memory gauges (never `getrusage` peak Maxrss mislabeled as current — darwin `proc_pid_rusage`/`RUSAGE_INFO_V2`, linux `/proc/self/status` VmRSS, honest peak named `process_max_resident_memory_bytes`, omitted where no live reading exists); startup bind-retry with backoff on `EADDRINUSE`. DG-7/DG-11 (`84b87bba`) — `fetchObligations` names its failure branch; `renderPostEdit` never lets an unverified guess overwrite a verified identity. DG-12 (`2b0f610f`) — the identity cache serves a stale positive resolution (capped 24h) on a genuine round-trip failure instead of falling all the way back to basename matching. DG-8/DG-9/DG-10/DG-17 (`234dbc0d`) — typed-loading failures name the failing package; discovery gaps and out-of-scope drops carry on `TypedEdgeStatus`; status persists across a daemon restart (SQLite-backed); CANTOPEN is tagged distinctly. DG-18 (folded into the dg-a/dg-c integration merge, this pass) — `list_services`'s per-service unsupported-language/root-missing lookup is memoized per (service, store watermark), never re-walked on a read; a vanished root reports `root_missing: true` plus a `remove_service` remedy instead of an error line. DG-21 (`d47d84d8`) — the owner-specified selector/pointer/interface-dispatch fixture reproduced against the real pipeline; all five call shapes resolve `EdgeConfidenceResolved`; O39/O40 Status fields updated with the fix and every regression test's name. DG-13 (`bdaa440a`) — `binary_fresh`'s remedy names which side of HEAD is behind. DG-14 (`b309e245`) — `fleetWiringNotes` checks user-scope hooks before reporting an absence (closes O15). DG-15 (`cc3165ea`) — `unproven_claims`'s reason carries its own computed evidence timestamp. OWN-1/OWN-2/OWN-4 recorded and deliberately deferred to the owner (`docs/plans/diagnostics-sweep.md`'s Owner-decision section) — not actioned by this sweep.
- **Status:** fixed — DG-1 through DG-21 (P0/P1/P2) landed and merged across four parallel integration branches into `work`; proven by `fathom verify --service fathom --plan docs/plans/diagnostics-sweep.md` (see that plan for the per-row contract and which rows verify). O39 stays open (DG-21 narrowed but did not close it); O40, O15 closed by DG-21 and DG-14 respectively. Owner items, 2026-09-24 on the owner's go-ahead: OWN-1 executed — `demo`, `fathom-dp-wave-a` and `esp-thumper` removed through the daemon's `remove_service` (none had compiled layers; `esp-thumper` has never had a commit and its tree is backed up outside the repo), list_services now counts 18; OWN-2 answered — ADR-016 amended to a 320-token whole-payload ceiling (Q21); OWN-4 diagnosed benign (the project's own committed hook edits never re-recorded in a gitignored per-checkout ledger) and the reconcile remedy fixed so it can clear hook drift.
- **Impact:** the 97%-of-log-volume stale-service spam and the live `list_services` re-walk cost (p95 4.6s/max 182s on ~31k calls) are eliminated at the mechanism level (DG-3/DG-18's cache; regression-proven at most one `Discover` per service+watermark); every daemon diagnostic line is now timestamped, leveled, and deduped instead of an unattributable, undeduped stream; a post-edit identity resolution can no longer silently regress to an unverified basename guess once a verified one has answered (DG-11/DG-12, closing the O15 failure class a second time); typed-edge diagnosability (failing package names, discovery gaps, restart-durable status) moved from "grep the stderr stream" to structured, queryable state; three selector/interface-dispatch call shapes are now regression-locked as correctly resolved, narrowing rather than closing O39.
- **Correction (2026-09-27, RT-138):** superseded by INC-23 (docs/intent/incidents.md) for its resource claim. "Eliminated at the mechanism level" rested on a sequential test counting `Discover` calls, not bytes under concurrency. On 2026-09-26, 49 cold `list_services` calls at once and obligations' two whole-tree reads per edit took the machine. The logging and diagnosability fixes above stand. The request-path claim is now held by I48 and I51, with `list_services` answered from `service_summary` (RT-79) and the cold-stampede test counting bytes (RT-95).

### O114 — A certificate stays `live` through any code change: liveness is not bound to the commit its run exercised

- **Symptom:** found by the 2026-09-24 refactoring assessment's adversarial critique and confirmed in code. After a refactor changes the symbols a certificate's test reaches, the certificate still reads `live`, on the strength of a run that predates the change.
- **Evidence:** `internal/certify/state.go` `CertificateState(reached, runs)` returns `live` when the test reaches the target and the newest ingested run passed; it takes no commit. `store.TestRun` records the ingest `Watermark`, never the commit the tests ran against, and test-kit's `#EnvInfo` has no commit field. `internal/certify/sweep.go` marks a certificate `stale` only when the test's own FILE moved, while `docs/design/test-certifications.md` §3 and plan row TC-8 promise "stale when the test or a reached symbol moved since the last run".
- **Root cause:** the sweep implemented the test-file half of the staleness rule and no run carries a commit to compare against. `fathom verify` proved TC-8 because its symbol exists and is reached by its test; the verify oracle checks reach, not what the function decides, so a row can be proven while its promise is only half kept.
- **Fix:** implemented as vision 34 / ADR-044 stage 1 (`docs/plans/refactor-conservation.md` rows CV-1..CV-6, CV-43). test-kit's `#EnvInfo` gains optional `commit`/`dirty`/`race` (platform-engineering ADR-0106, CV-43); `surveyor ingest --format go-test-json` takes them as `--commit`/`--dirty`/`--race` flags, the caller (a CI step, fathom's own `task tests:attest`) states them rather than test-kit reading git itself. fathom mirrors the schema (`schema/testkit/result.cue`, `.vendor-commit` bumped) and reads them into the run (`internal/testevidence.runCommitFromEnvelope`, CV-1: env.commit/dirty/race, or an in-toto subject named `gitCommit`, accepted the same). `store.TestRun`/`store.TestCaseRun` carry `Commit`/`Dirty`/`Race` as additive columns (CV-2). `internal/certify.CertificateStateAt` (CV-3) replaces the commit-blind `CertificateState` in both `CompileCertifications` and `SweepCertifications`: a certificate is `live` only for a passing run bound to a commit (`env.commit` set, tree clean) after which nothing the certificate reaches — its test file plus the files of the symbols it reaches — changed, checked by `reachUnchangedSince` (CV-4, `internal/certify/git.go`, an injected `GitRunner`: `git diff <commit> -- <files>`, a single-ref diff against the current working tree, so a committed AND an uncommitted edit are both caught in one read). An unbound run leaves the certificate `unproven` ("run not bound to a commit"); a changed reach since the newest passing bound run also leaves it `unproven`, and marks the compiled claim's own `Status` `stale` — both `CompileCertifications` and `SweepCertifications` share the one code path (CV-5), so a sweep now catches a reached symbol changing after the attested run exactly like it already caught the test file moving. `task tests:attest` (CV-6) re-attests this repository's own suite: `go test -json ./...`, converted by test-kit's go-test-json adapter (`TESTKIT_GO_JSON`, skipped with a clear message when unset), bound to `git rev-parse HEAD` / `git status --porcelain`, written to `[tests] results_dir` for a plain `fathom ingest` to pick up.
- **Status:** fixed — CV-1 (`TestLoadEnvelopes_CommitDirtyRace`), CV-2 (`TestTestRuns_CommitRoundTrip`), CV-3 (`TestCertificateStateAt_EveryRule`), CV-4 (`TestReachUnchangedSince_CommittedAndWorktreeEdits`), CV-5 (`TestSweepCertifications_ReachChangedSinceRunGoesStale`), CV-6 (`task tests:attest`), CV-43 (platform-engineering ADR-0106, `TestRunIngest_EnvCommitDirtyRace`).
- **Impact:** a certificate's `live` state now provably reflects a run exercised against the exact code at HEAD (or a still-unchanged earlier commit), never a run that predates a refactor; existing certificates in a pre-CV-1 store read `unproven` until re-attested with a commit-bound run (`task tests:attest`), the honest consequence design doc §1.1 states, not a regression.
