### O115 — `fathom ingest -scip` against the real, currently-published scip-typescript/scip-python keys every imported edge by the raw SCIP symbol string, never fathom's own key space — SU-9's scip import is silently non-functional against these producers today

- **Symptom:** found while running CV-M7's L0 oracle reading against real calibration clones (zod, TypeScript; flask, Python). `fathom ingest -scip <index.scip>` completed and reported edges written (zod: 123,012; flask: 13,034), but every `FromSymbol`/`ToSymbol` it stored was the full raw SCIP symbol string (e.g. `` scip-typescript npm zod 4.4.3 src/v4/core/`registries.ts`/$output# `` ) instead of a clean fathom `SymbolKey` (e.g. `zod-scip::packages/zod/src/v4/core/registries.$output`) — a namespace that never joins the SAME service's own extracted `SymbolNode.Key()` values (used by native/syntax/file-resolved edges, `find_callers`, `references`, `blast_radius`).
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `@sourcegraph/scip-typescript@0.4.0` and `@sourcegraph/scip-python@0.6.6` (both the current, only published versions — `npm view @sourcegraph/scip-typescript version` / `@sourcegraph/scip-python version`), run against real calibration clones (`zod@v4.4.3` with `pnpm install` completed first — no dependency-install issue) and `flask@3.1.3`.
  - **Expected:** `internal/scip.ImportIndex`'s own doc comment (`qualifiedName`, `scip_import.go`): a definition's fathom-facing name is `SymbolInformation.DisplayName`, "prefixed by its enclosing definition's own qualified name" — `internal/scip/testdata/tsfixture/index.scip`'s committed fixture produces exactly that (`TestScipOracle_TSFixture` passes, 2/3 direct truth edges join a real fathom syntax edge by key).
  - **Actual:** a raw-protobuf scan of both real indexes found **zero** `SymbolInformation` entries with a non-empty `DisplayName` — 0 of 23,583 (zod) and 0 of 5,551 (flask) — so `displayNameFor`'s documented fallback ("the raw SCIP symbol string when a producer omitted it") fires for every single definition, and `qualifiedName` inherits that raw string for the WHOLE qualified name, not just the leaf. This makes the import's `FromSymbol`/`ToSymbol` namespace entirely disjoint from fathom's own `core.SymbolKey` namespace for every edge these two producers emit, live, today.
  - **Repro:** `npm install --prefix <scratch> @sourcegraph/scip-typescript@0.4.0` (or `@sourcegraph/scip-python@0.6.6`), index any real TypeScript/Python project, `fathom ingest -scip <index.scip>`, then compare a stored `typed_edges` row's `from_symbol` against the same service's `symbols` table — no row's `from_symbol` matches a `SymbolNode.Key()`.
- **Root cause:** `internal/scip.ImportIndex` assumes a SCIP producer populates `Document.Symbols[].DisplayName` for local (non-external) definitions; the fixture it was tested against does, but the actual scip-typescript/scip-python releases available today do not (confirmed on their only published versions, not a version-pinning or missing-dependency artifact — installing zod's full pnpm workspace made no difference).
- **Fix:** done (refactor-conservation wave 2, same day). `internal/scip.ImportIndex` gained a required `nodes []core.SymbolNode` parameter (the service's own already-extracted symbol table) and never reads `DisplayName` at all any more: each definition occurrence is mapped by **position** — its document path + its own `Range` start line, matched against a node's `FilePath`+`Line` (`resolveDefinition`/`indexNodesByLocation`) — falling back to parsing the SCIP symbol string's own descriptor suffix (`descriptorLeafName`, per SCIP's published grammar) only when that position match is ambiguous. A definition neither path places is counted in the new `Report.Unmapped` (surfaced as `scip_imports[].unmapped` in `fathom ingest --json` plus a stderr warning) and contributes no edge in either direction — never guessed, never conflated with a genuinely external reference. Re-running the L0 oracle against the SAME real clones this observation's own evidence used (zod, flask) surfaced two FURTHER mapping bugs, both hand-verified against real source and fixed with regression tests reproducing the exact shape: (a) a function's own PARAMETER sits on the same source line as the function's own definition in real Python/TypeScript signatures, and fathom mints no `SymbolNode` for a parameter, so an ungated position match silently collapsed the parameter onto its enclosing function — `flask`'s `Flask.raise_routing_exception` (which never calls itself) produced a self-loop for every reference to its own `self`/`request` parameters until `descriptorIsParameterLike` refused Parameter/TypeParameter descriptors before position matching; (b) a SCIP `local <id>` symbol (document-scoped by SCIP's own spec, freely reused by unrelated documents) was being looked up in one flat, index-wide table — a `zod` method referencing only its own local variable ended up wired to an unrelated type in a different test file that happened to reuse the same local id, until `scopedSymbolKey` scoped `local` lookups by document path.
- **Status:** closed — proven by `internal/scip/scip_import_test.go`'s `TestImportIndex_O115_ResolvesWithoutDisplayName`, `TestImportIndex_O115_UnmappedEndpointNeverEmitsEdge`, `TestImportIndex_O115_DescriptorFallback_AmbiguousPosition`, `TestImportIndex_O115_ParameterNeverCollapsesOntoEnclosingFunction`, `TestImportIndex_O115_LocalSymbolsAreDocumentScoped`, `TestDescriptorLeafName`, `TestDescriptorIsParameterLike`, and `TestImportIndex_FindCallers_ResolvesThroughImportedEdges` (an end-to-end check that a caller resolves through an imported edge via the SAME `internal/analysis/callgraph` code `find_callers` uses) — plus a fresh, hermetic re-import of the real zod/flask clones this observation's own evidence table was built from, hand-verified 9/9 correct on a stride-sampled `direct`-edge spot check (flask) and confirmed real recursion (not a residual bug) on a sample of the remaining same-symbol edges. Full numbers, methodology and the two further bugs: `docs/bench/readings/l0-ts-py-2026-09-24.md`'s "O115 fixed — CV-M7 re-run" section.
- **Impact:** `fathom ingest --scip`/SU-9 (ADR-041 decision 5) was silently non-functional for real callers against the two producers `docs/languages.md` names as the delegate indexer for TypeScript/JavaScript/Python — the daemon accepted the import, reported edges written, and `find_callers`/`references`/`blast_radius` for a scip-imported TS/Python service resolved NONE of them (the file-resolved tier, SU-11, was unaffected — it never uses `internal/scip` and correctly hand-verified at 26/26 in the same reading). Now fixed: on the same real clones, zod resolves 82.0% of definitions unmapped (SCIP's much finer granularity — parameters, type parameters, object-literal properties fathom never tracks — not a defect) and flask 65.5%, with the remainder correctly keyed into fathom's own symbol space; a genuine (not self-referential) precision/recall read of the file-resolved tier against this now-independent truth reads 0.87/0.03 (TypeScript) and 0.95/0.12 (Python) — high precision, low recall, as expected of a heuristic resolver. A related, DISTINCT gap the re-run surfaced (filed separately as O116): `PutTypedEdges`'s per-package replace leaves a package's stale old edges in place forever once a corrected re-import produces zero edges for that package.

### O116 — `PutTypedEdges`' per-package replace never clears a package whose corrected re-import now produces ZERO edges, so a stale, previously-wrong row can survive an otherwise-correct fix indefinitely

- **Symptom:** found while re-verifying O115's fix against a store from a PRIOR (broken) `fathom ingest -scip` run: after re-importing the SAME SCIP index with the fixed `internal/scip.ImportIndex` into the SAME store, a handful of sampled `typed_edges` rows still showed the OLD, raw-SCIP-string-keyed garbage (e.g. `` zod-scip::packages/zod/src/v4/classic/schemas.scip-typescript npm zod 4.4.3 src/v4/classic/`schemas.ts`/.… `` as a `from_symbol`) — bytes the fixed importer can no longer produce, from a re-import that reported success and 0 failures.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `pkg/client.Client.ImportSCIP` / `internal/store.PutTypedEdges` (both SQLite and in-memory backends share the contract).
  - **Expected:** re-importing a SCIP index for a service replaces that service's scip-derived typed edges with exactly what the current import computed — the same "re-importing one changed file's worth of SCIP output never disturbs another package's rows" contract the package doc comment already documents, just for the FULL set of packages the import ever touched, not only the ones it touches THIS time.
  - **Actual:** `PutTypedEdges` is called once per entry in `imp.Packages` (`ImportSCIP`'s loop), and `imp.Packages` only lists packages the CURRENT import produced at least one edge for. A package whose OLD import wrote edges but whose NEW (corrected, stricter) import produces zero edges for — because every one of its former "direct" edges turns out, correctly, to be unmapped or external — is simply never named in `imp.Packages`, so `PutTypedEdges` is never called for it, and its old rows are never replaced or cleared.
  - **Repro:** `fathom ingest --service S --path P --store s.sqlite -scip old.scip` (any importer that produces edges for package `pkg`); then fix/change the importer so `pkg` now produces zero edges for the SAME source; `fathom ingest --service S --path P --store s.sqlite -scip old.scip` again; query `typed_edges` for `pkg` — the FIRST run's rows are still there.
- **Root cause:** the package-granular replace contract (`store.PutTypedEdges(ctx, service, pkg, edges)`) is a replace-BY-CALL, not a replace-BY-SERVICE: it has no way to express "this service's scip-derived edges are now exactly these packages, and no others" — only "replace `pkg`'s rows with `edges`" for each package a caller happens to name. Nothing in `ImportSCIP` ever asks the store which packages it PREVIOUSLY held scip-derived edges for, so a package that drops to zero edges is indistinguishable, from the caller's side, from a package the import never touched at all.
- **Fix:** done (refactor-conservation wave 3). The root cause was entirely on the `internal/scip.ImportIndex` side: `Import.Packages` was built solely from `edgesByPkg`'s own keys — the packages that happened to accumulate at least one edge — so a package with a mapped definition but zero edges this run (nothing calls FROM it, or every occurrence now correctly resolves to unmapped/external) was never named in `Import.Packages` at all, and `ImportSCIP`'s loop over `imp.Packages` therefore never even calls `store.PutTypedEdges` for it. `store.PutTypedEdges` itself already clears a package's rows correctly the INSTANT it is called with an empty slice, on both backends (`internal/store/contract_test.go`'s pre-existing `typed_edges_roundtrip_and_replace` subtest already proved this) — the gap was entirely about which packages ever got called at all. Fixed in `internal/scip/scip_import.go`'s `fromIndex`: `Import.Packages` now lists the union of every package with at least one MAPPED definition (`bySym`'s own package set) and `edgesByPkg`'s keys, so a package "in the import's package set" with zero new edges is still listed, with an empty `Edges` slice, and the existing `PutTypedEdges` call clears its stale rows exactly as it already does for any other empty-edges call. No `store.Store` interface or backend change was needed — `store.PutTypedEdges`'s existing behavior on both backends was already correct; only the caller-visible `Import.Packages` set needed to include zero-edge packages. Scope: this fixes the exact repro above (a package whose SOURCE is still present/scanned but now correctly computes to zero edges) — a package that disappears from a NEW SCIP index ENTIRELY (no document mentions it at all, e.g. its source file was deleted or excluded) was a different, then still-open case: `ImportIndex` has no way to know about a package it never saw anything about, so that shape needed the ORIGINAL proposal above (tag scip-imported rows with their producer/import kind, or have the caller diff against the prior run's package list).
- **Fix (residual — package vanishes entirely):** done. Provenance did not need a new store method or schema migration: `internal/fusion/typedcue.go` already tags every CUE-produced typed-edge row with a `"cue:"` pkg_path prefix so its own reconcile (`store.ListTypedEdgePackages` + `store.DeleteTypedEdgePackage`) never touches the Go typed pass's bare-keyed rows — the same convention now covers SCIP. `pkg/client.Client.ImportSCIP` writes every package under a `"scip:<index path>::"` namespace (`scipStoreKeyPrefix`, `pkg/client/client.go`) instead of the bare package name, then lists every stored package in that SAME namespace and deletes (`store.DeleteTypedEdgePackage`) any this call's `imp.Packages` did not just (re)write — clearing a package that is absent from the new decode entirely, not merely zero-edges-but-listed (O116's original fix). Scoped by the SCIP index's OWN path, not a bare `"scip:"`, because one `fathom ingest` can import several SCIP files (`--scip` is repeatable) covering disjoint packages of the same service — a whole-service reconcile would make one file's transient failure, or simply not re-passing it this run, look identical to "this package vanished," and delete another file's still-current rows. `store.PutTypedEdges`'s per-package replace contract is unchanged for every other caller (Go pass, CUE pass).
- **Status:** fixed — proven by `internal/scip/scip_import_test.go`'s `TestImportIndex_O116_ZeroEdgePackageStillListed` (a package with a mapped definition and zero edges is listed) and `TestImportIndex_O116_ReimportClearsPackageThatDropsToZeroEdges` (end-to-end: a package that drops from 2 edges to 0 across a re-import has its stale rows cleared, proven against BOTH `store.NewMemoryStore()` and `store.NewSQLiteStore("")`), plus `TestImportIndex_MapsOccurrencesToTypedEdges`'s own fixture assertion for package "math" flipped from "must be absent" (the pinned-wrong behavior) to "must be present with 0 edges." The residual (package vanishes entirely) is proven by `pkg/client/scip_o116_test.go`'s `TestImportSCIP_O116_VanishedPackageClearsStaleEdges` (imports a SCIP index with packages "greeter"/"math"/"main", re-imports the SAME path with greeter.ts's document dropped entirely, asserts every edge FROM a greeter symbol is gone — red before the fix, green after, on BOTH `store.NewMemoryStore()` and `store.NewSQLiteStore("")`) and `TestImportSCIP_O116_GoTypedPassPackageSurvivesSCIPReimport` (a bare-keyed Go-typed-pass package for the same service survives a SCIP re-import that never names it, on both backends). Follow-up (2026-09-25): the namespace is keyed by the index file's stable identity (`pkg/client.scipIndexKey`: root-relative path inside the service root, else base name), not the path as given, so re-ingesting from another checkout or a fresh `--scip` temp path reconciles the same namespace instead of leaking a new one each run (`TestSCIPIndexKey_StableAcrossRootsAndTempPaths`, `TestImportSCIP_O116_SameIndexFromAnotherPathReconciles`, red on the path-keyed version). Known limit: rows a pre-fix import wrote under bare package keys cannot be told apart from Go typed-pass rows and are not migrated; a store that imported SCIP before this fix clears them by re-ingesting into a fresh store.
- **Impact:** was scoped but real: a language whose SCIP-import quality genuinely IMPROVES over time (a producer upgrade, or a fathom-side mapping fix like O115's) could leave STALE, WRONG edges standing for any package that goes from "some edges" to "no edges" under the correction — the opposite of what re-running the fix is supposed to prove. Every fresh (never-before-imported) store was unaffected; this reading's own CV-M7 numbers avoided it by re-importing into fresh stores once the pattern was caught (`docs/bench/readings/l0-ts-py-2026-09-24.md`). Now fixed for both shapes: "package still known, zero edges" (original fix) and "package vanished entirely — deleted, renamed, or the producer stopped emitting it" (residual fix above); `find_callers`/`references`/`blast_radius` no longer serve edges for a package a SCIP re-import no longer knows about at all.

### O117 — `fathom conserve`'s default plan discovery silently sees ZERO `retires:`/`surface_change:` declarations, at both base and head, because `discoverPlanPaths` hands `NewInput` absolute paths where it expects repo-relative ones

- **Symptom:** found while re-running CV-M5's Blindness replay (`docs/bench/readings/cv-m5-blindness-replay-2026-09-24.md`) with the resurrection fix: a plan row declaring `retires: "api:...DuckStore.*"` at the declared base, added specifically so the retirement gate would fire on the seeded copy, was silently ignored — the seeded copy came back `Kind: "moved"` (informational, no retirement claimed) instead of `Kind: "body_resurrected"` (a loss) — even though the SAME row, read directly through `verify.ParseMarkdownPlan`, parses its `retires:` field correctly.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `cmd/fathom/conserve.go`'s `cmdConserve` (default plan discovery, no `--plan` flag — the ONLY way `lefthook.yml`'s `refactor-conserve` step ever invokes it: `bin/fathom conserve --service fathom --since "$since" --store workspace/db/fleet.sqlite`, no `--plan`) and `internal/conserve.NewInput`.
  - **Expected:** every `docs/plans/*.md` file `discoverPlanPaths` finds is read at base (`git show <base>:<path>`) and at head (the worktree file), exactly as it is when a caller passes an explicit, repo-relative `--plan docs/plans/x.md`.
  - **Actual:** instrumenting `NewInput`'s plan-loading loop directly (temporary `stderr` logging, reverted before this fix landed) showed `baseOK=false` AND `headOK=false` for EVERY plan file under default discovery, including one that unquestionably exists at both base and head. Passing the identical file via an explicit, repo-relative `--plan docs/plans/x.md` instead reads correctly at both base and head, and the retirement gate fires as designed.
  - **Repro:** `cd <any git repo root>` (an absolute `$PWD`, as `os.Getwd()` always returns) `&& bin/fathom conserve --service x --since <base> --json` with no `--plan` — every `retires:`/`surface_change:`/`gone:` declaration from `docs/plans/*.md` is silently invisible to both the resurrection plane's retirement gate and the surface/certificates planes' I45 prior/self split, at base AND at head.
- **Root cause:** `discoverPlanPaths(root)` (`cmd/fathom/conserve.go`) globs `filepath.Join(root, "docs", "plans", "*.md")`, and `root` (`verifyRepoRoot()`) is always an ABSOLUTE path (`os.Getwd()` plus the git toplevel) — so every match is an absolute path. `NewInput`'s loop passes these straight through as `rel` to `gitShowFile`/`worktreeFile`, both of which treat their `path` argument as repo-RELATIVE: `gitShowFile` builds `<ref>:<path>` (git's own `rev:path` syntax refuses a leading `/` — confirmed directly: `git show HEAD:/abs/path/CHANGELOG.md` → `fatal: path '...' exists on disk, but not in 'HEAD'`, even though the file is tracked at the equivalent relative path) and `worktreeFile` does `filepath.Join(root, path)`, which — because `path` is ALSO already absolute — produces a doubled, nonexistent path (`filepath.Join("/a/b", "/a/b/c") == "/a/b/a/b/c"`) that `os.ReadFile` reports as not-existing. Both failure modes are swallowed as the ordinary "this plan doesn't exist here" case (`ok=false, err=nil` — by design, since a plan being absent at one side of a diff is not an error), so nothing ever surfaces the silent loss.
- **Fix:** `internal/conserve.NewInput` now relativizes each `planPaths` entry against `root` itself (`planPathRelativeTo`, `internal/conserve/input.go`) before handing it to `gitShowFile`/`worktreeFile` — an absolute path already inside `root` is rewritten relative to it; an already-relative path (the ordinary explicit `--plan docs/plans/x.md` shape) or an absolute path outside `root` is left as-is. This fixes the bug at its one true source rather than at each of `discoverPlanPaths`'s two call sites (`cmd/fathom/conserve.go`, `internal/mcp/review_tool.go`'s `discoverConservePlanPaths`), so any future caller of `NewInput` inherits the fix for free. Regression test: `TestCmdConserve_NoPlanFlagStillReadsRetiresDeclarations` (`cmd/fathom/conserve_test.go`) runs `fathom conserve --since <base> --service svc --store <db>` with NO `--plan` flag — lefthook.yml's exact invocation shape — against a fixture repo with a genuine `retires: api:widgets.Old` row at base and a resurrected body at head, and asserts the resurrection plane reports `body_resurrected`, never `moved`. Confirmed failing before the fix (both findings read `[moved]`/`declared: none` even with the retirement declared) and passing after.
- **Status:** fixed.
- **Impact:** significant if confirmed on the real repository, not just this reading's scratch clones: `fathom conserve`'s ONLY real invocation (the pre-push `refactor-conserve` step, CV-47) never passes `--plan`, so every `retires:`/`surface_change:` declaration made anywhere in `docs/plans/*.md` may be silently excusing NOTHING today — the resurrection plane's retirement gate (§3.3) would never see a claimed retirement (degrading every genuine Blindness case to "moved," never "body_resurrected"), and the surface/certificates planes' base-side I45 "declared before the range" excusal would never apply either, though CV-M5/CV-M6's own conserve runs happened not to expose this: CV-M6 retired only `internal/` packages (inventory.Extract never tracks those regardless), and this reading's OWN first "genuine" runs (before this observation's fix was found) got 0 findings on the DuckDB/vector ranges for the unrelated reason that the resurrection fix itself removes the false positives independent of whether retirement is recognized. Needs confirmation against the real repository and a dedicated fix; this reading's own measurements below route around it with an explicit `--plan <relative path>`.

### O118 — CV-M4's Go `api:` surface delta silently dropped any change made via an interface's embedded fields (e.g. adding `io.Closer`), a recall regression against the OLD plain-text comparison

- **Symptom:** found by a review of the wave-3 gap-closing diff, not by CV-M4's own 50-pair calibration corpus (none of those 50 real release pairs happen to add or remove an embedded interface, so the gap went unmeasured by that reading's reported 1.000 recall).
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `internal/analysis/inventory/apicompat.go`'s `interfaceMethodShapes` (populates `TypeDetail.Interface`) and `itemChanged` (chooses the type-identity comparison over the old whole-text `Detail` comparison whenever both sides carry a `TypeDetail`).
  - **Expected:** adding a method requirement to an already-fully-exported interface — whether written directly or via embedding another interface (`io.Closer`) — is a real, `apidiff`-classified incompatible change (`api:widgets.Reader` reported `Changed`), matching `TestDiff_InterfaceMethodAdditionIncompatibleUnlessSealed`'s own direct-method case.
  - **Actual:** `interfaceMethodShapes` skipped every `ast.Field` with `len(Names) != 1` outright, so an embedded interface was never added to `TypeDetail.Interface` under any key, not even an opaque marker. `itemChanged` takes the `TypeDetail` branch exclusively whenever both sides carry one, never falling back to the old whole-text `Detail` comparison for a type declaration. A throwaway fixture — base `type Reader interface { Read() error }`, head `type Reader interface { Read() error; io.Closer }` — produced an empty `Diff{}` under the pre-fix code, while the PRE-CV-M4 code on the identical fixture correctly reported `api:widgets.Reader` as `Changed`. The reverse direction (removing the embed) also returned an empty delta.
- **Root cause:** `interfaceMethodShapes` treated "not a single named method" (`len(m.Names) != 1`) as uniformly uninteresting, conflating two different shapes: an embedded interface (a real, AST-visible fact about THIS interface's own declaration, needing no cross-package resolution to notice its identity changed) and a type-set element (`~int | ~string`, a generics constraint, genuinely a different animal). Treating both as "skip" meant an embed's own addition/removal/retargeting — visible from the same two AST parses `Extract` already does — was thrown away instead of at least registering as "this interface's shape changed."
- **Fix:** `interfaceMethodShapes` now adds one `"embed <base-identifier>"` entry to `TypeDetail.Interface` per embedded interface field (keyed via `embeddedFieldName`, the same helper `structFieldShapes` already uses for an embedded struct field; a space can never appear in a Go identifier, so this key space never collides with a real method name), valued by the embed's own rendered type text. `embeddedFieldName` returns `""` for a type-set element (a `*ast.BinaryExpr` union or `*ast.UnaryExpr` tilde matches none of its cases), so that shape is still correctly left untracked. `interfaceCompatible`'s existing asymmetric rule (removing/changing an existing key is always incompatible; adding a new key is incompatible unless the base interface already carried an unexported method) now applies to embeds for free — this does not resolve the embedded interface's OWN method set (still a documented, out-of-scope gap; no cross-package resolution), it only makes the embed's own identity part of the interface's tracked shape. Regression test: `TestDiff_EmbeddedInterfaceAdditionIsChanged` (`internal/analysis/inventory/inventory_test.go`), mirroring `TestDiff_InterfaceMethodAdditionIncompatibleUnlessSealed`'s own fixture shape — an open interface gaining/losing `io.Closer` is `Changed`; a sealed interface (an existing unexported method) gaining the same embed is not. Confirmed failing before the fix (`Changed = []` both directions) and passing after.
- **Status:** fixed.
- **Impact:** was real, not merely a documented gap: `fathom conserve`'s surface plane (and `fathom refactor status`'s ratchets, `fathom review --since`'s `conservation` dimension) would report `conserved`/no finding on a genuine ABI-breaking interface change made via embedding, exactly the shape ADR-044 exists to catch. `docs/bench/readings/cv-m4-apidiff-full50-2026-09-24.md`'s reported precision/recall stand unchanged (the 50-pair corpus never exercised this class either way), so CV-M4's `(op)` row is not amended.

### O119 — `modelprice.go`'s doc comment claimed compile.go's and servehttp.go's real-run `WithLLM` calls read its named rate constants; neither call site was ever changed

- **Symptom:** found by a review of the wave-3 gap-closing diff: `cmd/fathom/modelprice.go`'s own doc comment on `knownModelInRatePerKTok`/`knownModelOutRatePerKTok` says they exist "so `rateForModel` and those call sites read the identical number under one name instead of a second, silently driftable literal," naming `compile.go`'s and `servehttp.go`'s real-run `WithLLM` calls as "those call sites" — but neither call site was ever edited to reference the constants.
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `cmd/fathom/compile.go:350` (the spending-compile path) and `cmd/fathom/servehttp.go:131` (the daemon-startup path used by every HTTP/MCP `compile_layer` call).
  - **Expected:** both read `knownModelInRatePerKTok`/`knownModelOutRatePerKTok`, exactly as `rateForModel` (the dry-run path) already does, so editing the constants in one place changes the price everywhere the doc comment claims it does.
  - **Actual:** both still hardcoded their own `0.001, 0.005` literal, independently of `modelprice.go` and of each other. `cmd/fathom/smoke.go` carries a FOURTH independent copy (`smokeInRatePerKTok`/`smokeOutRatePerKTok`), further confirming nothing was actually shared.
- **Root cause:** the constants were added (wave 3, gap-closing item 2) to give the dry-run path a real rate, and the doc comment was written describing the INTENDED end state — one name, three readers — but only `rateForModel` was ever wired to them; updating the two real-run call sites was never done, and nothing tested that they agreed with the constants.
- **Fix:** `compile.go:350` and `servehttp.go:131` now pass `knownModelInRatePerKTok, knownModelOutRatePerKTok` directly instead of the literal. Regression test: `TestRealRunWithLLMCallSitesShareNamedRateConstants` (`cmd/fathom/modelprice_test.go`) — a source-lexical check (neither call site builds without a live LLM provider, which no test in this suite exercises) asserting both files contain a `WithLLM(..., knownModelInRatePerKTok, knownModelOutRatePerKTok)` call and neither contains a literal `0.001`/`0.005` anywhere. Confirmed failing before the fix (both files: constant call missing, literal present) and passing after. `smoke.go`'s own separate copy is unchanged — out of this fix's named scope (the doc comment never claimed to include it).
- **Status:** fixed.
- **Impact:** was a drift risk, not a wrong number today (all three sites happened to agree on 0.001/0.005). Had a maintainer later repriced `defaultCompileModel` by editing only `modelprice.go`'s constants — reasonably trusting the doc comment — a dry-run estimate would have silently diverged from what an actual `--dry-run=false` compile or an MCP `compile_layer` call of the identical model actually billed, with no test to catch it.

### O120 - The rule-outcome events violated and complied do not measure compliance for a prose rule: violated means the same claim topped the file's served set on the file's previous resolved turn, and complied means the top claim changed, so every governance-learning reading built on them measures scope persistence and churn

- **Symptom:** The rule-outcome events violated and complied do not measure compliance for a prose rule: violated means the same claim topped the file's served set on the file's previous resolved turn, and complied means the top claim changed, so every governance-learning reading built on them measures scope persistence and churn
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** cmd/fathom/ruleoutcome_track.go (SR-61, GL-10)
  - **Expected:** violated fires when the edit fails a rule's checkable condition and complied when it meets it, so GL-10's per-rule counts and GL-11's classes (ignored, effective) say something about compliance
  - **Actual:** Rules are served by glob scope, so a prose rule with no checkable condition reads violated on every repeat edit of the same file whether or not the edit complied, and complied fires on a recompile or scope churn; found by code reading during the 2026-09-29 judgment research (ADR-056), not yet measured on a ledger
  - **Repro:** Read trackRuleOutcomeAt in cmd/fathom/ruleoutcome_track.go and TestObligationsFor_ZeroOutputOnClean in internal/semantic/serve_test.go; edit the same file twice under a served prose rule and read the recorded events. Fix planned as JF-1 in docs/plans/judgment-foundations.md: rename to still_served and top_changed, keep violated only for a checkable condition
- **Root cause:** the tracker keys both events on claim-id equality between consecutive resolved turns of one file, not on a check of the rule's own condition; rules are served by glob scope, so equality measures scope persistence and churn. No rule with a checkable condition exists to give `violated` a real meaning.
- **Fix:** planned, not yet landed: `docs/plans/judgment-foundations.md` JF-1 renames the events `still_served` and `top_changed` and keeps `violated` only for a rule with a checkable condition, JF-2 makes every reader read the new names and map old ledger rows, and the GL-10 readings carry a correction note (`docs/design/governance-learning.md` section 5, done 2026-09-29). ADR-056 decision 8.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** every governance-learning reading built on these events (GL-10 counts, GL-11's ignored and effective classes) is mislabeled until the rename lands; whether GL-13 has already promoted or retired a rule from them was not checked. Not measured on a ledger: `~/.fathom/rule-outcomes.jsonl` did not exist on the reference machine at the time of the reading.
- **Addendum (2026-09-29, ADR-057):** JF-1 lands alone and drops the clause that keeps `violated` for a rule with a checkable condition. The tracker evaluates no check: a checkable rule denies and records `blocked` (PR-10 in `docs/plans/served-not-kept.md`), pin losses have their own `pin` beat, and a `violated` name with no writer would invite this defect back. The design notes (`docs/design/judgment-foundations.md` section 5, `docs/design/governance-learning.md` section 5) carry the same amendment.

### O121 - Scratch-clone runs of fathom verify and fathom intent audit append rows keyed by scratch paths to the user-level verify and intent ledgers, so 13,514 of 19,639 verify-ledger rows (and 91 of 180 intent-ledger rows) on 2026-09-28 describe throwaway clones and distort proof history and economics

- **Symptom:** Scratch-clone runs of fathom verify and fathom intent audit append rows keyed by scratch paths to the user-level verify and intent ledgers, so 13,514 of 19,639 verify-ledger rows (and 91 of 180 intent-ledger rows) on 2026-09-28 describe throwaway clones and distort proof history and economics
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** cmd/fathom/verifyledger.go (verifyLedgerPath, appendVerifyLedgerFrom), internal/intent/ledger.go (LedgerPath, AppendLedger), cmd/fathom/gc.go (the `ledgers` class only reports)
  - **Expected:** the ledgers hold the fleet's registered repositories only, so burn-up, proof regressions, cost per proven row, the session join's unjoined share and the verify-gate figure describe real work
  - **Actual:** every run appends a row under its own working root, registered or not; on the reference machine 7,729 verify rows came from one scratch clone and 241 distinct roots appear in the verify ledger; found by the 2026-09-29 judgment research agent and confirmed by counting `repo_root` values under `/private/tmp` in `~/.fathom/verify-ledger.jsonl` and `intent-ledger.jsonl` (read only)
  - **Repro:** run `fathom verify --plan <plan>` or `fathom intent audit` inside a clone outside the registered service roots without setting FATHOM_VERIFY_LEDGER or FATHOM_INTENT_LEDGER, then count rows whose repo_root is the clone. The per-file variables already exist but are undocumented in docs/usage.md and nothing sets them for a scratch run
  - **Fix:** planned, not yet landed: `docs/plans/progress-and-economics.md` PE-23 to PE-25 (readers filter to registered service roots and name the ignored count), PE-26 to PE-28 (a documented FATHOM_HOME and --ledger override that test and scratch runs use), PE-29 (a `fathom gc` class `ledger-unregistered` that archives, never deletes, on the owner's command) and the PE-M5 reading; design `docs/design/progress-and-economics.md` section 11
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** every reading that joins or counts ledger rows without a root filter is inflated or skewed until PE-24 lands, among them proof history and burn-up (PE-2, PE-6), cost per proven row (PE-16), the unjoined share of the session join (PE-15), the verify-gate figure, and the corpus that decision E4 (WS-12) would fit on. Not measured: how far each figure moves once the scratch rows are excluded, which PE-M5 reads.

### O122 — the Bash-write hook treats a quoted `>` (echo "a -> b") or a quoted writer name as a file write and injects six unrelated root rules (__bash_write__.go nearest guide)

- **Symptom:** the Bash-write hook treats a quoted `>` (echo "a -> b") or a quoted writer name as a file write and injects six unrelated root rules (__bash_write__.go nearest guide)
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** PostToolUse Bash obligations hook
  - **Expected:** no injection for a command that writes nothing; a Bash write is a redirect, tee, sed -i, mv/cp, or a known writer
  - **Actual:** ~350 tokens of unrelated ADR rules per read-only Bash call in orchestration sessions
  - **Repro:** `for id in a b; do echo "$id -> x"; done` via the Bash tool with the hook installed (also: an argument string that merely mentions `sed -i` or `tee`)
- **Root cause:** `bashWriteTargets` (cmd/fathom/bashwrite.go) matches `redirectRe`, `sedInPlaceRe` and tee against raw command text without respecting quoting, so a `>` or a writer name inside a quoted argument is write-shaped; the target is then unresolvable, which takes the doubt branch in cmd/fathom/edithook_bashwrite.go and serves the cwd nearest guide (at the repo root: six unrelated root rules plus the displaced count)
- **Fix:** blank single- and double-quoted spans and heredoc bodies before the write-shape scan (a quoted `>` is text, not a redirect); serve the doubt payload at most once per session per directory; efficiency wave row; addendum 2026-09-29: a heredoc body is data, so a `>` or a writer name inside it is never a target, and a write whose every target resolves outside all indexed roots (`printf ... >> ~/.claude/.../MEMORY.md`) is silent, because `len(files)==0` in cmd/fathom/edithook_bashwrite.go reads it as unresolvable today: doubt only when a target is unresolvable, never when it is resolved and foreign
- **Status:** fixed 9cf53014 (EW-1) — proven by `TestBashWriteTargets` (a quoted `>` or writer name is text; heredoc bodies are data, including an apostrophe, `${1:?usage}` and a `bin/fathom` line; a quoted target is still read), `TestEditHookBashWriteQuotedArrowIsSilent`, `TestEditHookBashWriteForeignTargetIsSilent` (`printf >>` an absolute or `~` path outside the repo, `../x`, and a heredoc writing `/tmp` are silent with zero obligations calls) and `TestEditHookBashWriteDoubtServesAtMostOneLine` (one line, once per session and directory, a `post-bash` payload-cost beat per emission); `fathom verify --strict` proves EW-1 wired from `main`
- **Impact:** about 350 tokens injected per affected Bash call; an orchestration session hit it on 3 of its first 8 calls, none of which wrote a repo file through the flagged text

### O123 - SR-45 is marked [x] but the half that says the obligations answer marks a served rule checkable exists only as a library: guardFromCheckableRule and LayerRuleIsCheckable have test callers only, so no served answer carries the mark

- **Symptom:** SR-45 is marked [x] but the half that says the obligations answer marks a served rule checkable exists only as a library: guardFromCheckableRule and LayerRuleIsCheckable have test callers only, so no served answer carries the mark
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** internal/guardspec/checkablerule.go (SR-45, F-26), the obligations answer path
  - **Expected:** a served rule that compiles to an `enforce: true` guard is marked checkable in the obligations answer the agent reads, so the agent and every reading can tell a rule that denies from one that only advises (the SR-45 row says the answer marks it checkable)
  - **Actual:** `LayerRuleIsCheckable` is reached only through `guardFromCheckableRule`, and `guardFromCheckableRule` is called only from `checkablerule_test.go`; no obligations output path calls either. Found independently by the promises critic (2026-09-29) while reading the guard and rule-outcome paths; the row was flipped `[x]` on the library half
  - **Repro:** `grep -rn "LayerRuleIsCheckable\|guardFromCheckableRule" --include='*.go' .` and read the callers (non-test: only the package's own chain); call `obligations` for a file governed by an enforced guard and look for a checkable mark
- **Root cause:** the row proved the library (`TestCheckableRuleCompilesToGuard`) and stopped: the wiring half (mark the served rule in the obligations answer) has no consumer, so the row is proven by tests and reached by no product entry point ("done means wired"). Nothing else reads the mark, so the checkable/not-checkable split is invisible to agents and to `docs/design/served-not-kept.md`'s ladder.
- **Fix:** planned, not yet landed: a separate wiring row (the integration line tracks it as SR-45w) that marks a served rule checkable in the obligations answer through `LayerRuleIsCheckable` and proves it from the answer, not the library; until it lands SR-45 reads library-only.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** the ladder of ADR-057 (a checkable rule denies, a prose rule advises) is stated but not visible in what the agent is served; a reading that splits served rules by checkable would find every rule unmarked. No wrong verdict is served (the deny path itself works, ADR-038); the mark is missing, not false.

### O124 - The shared build-cache prune race failed pre-push gates: the prune lock in scripts/lib/build-cache.sh serializes pruners but not builders, so a build that starts during go clean -cache reads a cache being deleted

- **Symptom:** Pre-push gates failed while another process pruned the shared Go build cache; the gate scripts were moved to a private `FATHOM_CACHE_ROOT` to stop it
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** scripts/lib/build-cache.sh (I39, ADR-039), the pre-push gate
  - **Expected:** a prune above `FATHOM_CACHE_MAX_MIB` never fails a concurrent build; a gate run is unaffected by another run's cache trim
  - **Actual:** the prune branch takes its lock with `mkdir` and the lock only stops a second pruner (it prints "prune already in progress; continuing" and builds on), so builders that continue race the `go clean -cache`; found in the 2026-09-28 gate runs when parallel worker waves shared one cache root
  - **Repro:** read the size guard around `go clean -cache` in `scripts/lib/build-cache.sh`; run two gates against one `FATHOM_CACHE_ROOT` whose Go build cache is above `FATHOM_CACHE_MAX_MIB`. The mechanism is inferred from the code path; it was not reproduced from a saved log
- **Root cause:** suspected: the lock excludes pruners from each other, not builders from a pruner, so nothing keeps a live build away from a cache being deleted
- **Fix:** EW-4: the guard no longer runs `go clean -cache` at all. `fathom_cache_trim` (scripts/lib/build-cache.sh) deletes entries by age in three passes (unused for 7, 3, then 1 days; Go stamps an entry's mtime when a build uses it), stops at the first pass under `FATHOM_CACHE_MAX_MIB`, never removes an entry used within the last day, and reports a cache that stays over the limit. The prune lock is removed (an age trim is idempotent). fathom's own Go code has no cache prune; this script was the only site. ADR-039 carries the dated amendment; I39 and docs/usage.md say the new rule
- **Status:** fixed — proven by `scripts/build-cache_test.sh` (`task scripts-check`): the over-limit guard never invokes go, stale entries are trimmed and a fresh one kept, a builder reading an entry through three trims never sees it missing, a cache under the limit is untouched, and the script never spells the whole-cache clean (EW-4's lexical oracle). The mechanism stays inferred from the code path; the race itself was never reproduced from a saved log.
- **Impact:** a failed gate costs a full run (about 40 minutes) and reads as a code failure until someone notices the cache error; the private-root workaround leaves the shared-root path unfixed for any operator who runs two builds at once

### O125 - The intent living docs are over the project-intent size budget and the 2026-09-27..29 program grew them without the prune that should ride with new records: futures.md 692 lines, scope.md 375 and domain.md 198 against a budget of 150 each

- **Symptom:** The program added goals G27 to G33, futures F34 to F55, questions Q31 to Q34 and principle P24 to `docs/intent/` without running `intent-prune`; the living docs that were already over budget on 2026-09-27 (futures.md 399 and scope.md 326 lines on origin main) grew further
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `docs/intent/` (ADR-026), the `intent-doctor` budget row and the `intent-prune` skill
  - **Expected:** the router at most 80 lines and each living doc at most 150 (agentic-kit `docs/project-intent/` adoption budgets), with met goals, promoted or dropped futures and answered questions deleted in the change that adds new records (git keeps history)
  - **Actual:** on branch durability-sweep `wc -l docs/intent/*.md` reads futures.md 692, scope.md 375, domain.md 198; the router (79), principles (134), questions (138), incidents (117), invariants (93) and autonomy (65) are within budget
  - **Repro:** `wc -l docs/intent/*.md`; run `intent-doctor`
- **Root cause:** suspected: the durability sweep's write plan scheduled the prune after G33 and it was not run; a prune on one unmerged branch also deletes text that other open branches still edit, so it is safer in the merge change
- **Fix:** planned, not yet landed: run `intent-prune` then `intent-doctor` in the change that merges the program's branches to main, never before the merge, so no record is lost in flight; every deleted goal or future must already be met, promoted or dropped by its own condition, never trimmed for length alone
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** an agent routed to futures.md or scope.md loads two and a half (scope.md) to four and a half (futures.md) times the budgeted context before designing, and a long futures file hides which options are live; until the prune lands the budget row of `intent-doctor` fails

### O126 — the prompt hook fires on a cross-agent relay message (a sub-agent's message, not typed by the user) and serves rules for path-like tokens that are not repo files

- **Symptom:** the prompt hook fires on a cross-agent relay message (a sub-agent's message, not typed by the user) and serves rules for path-like tokens that are not repo files
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** UserPromptSubmit prompt obligations hook
  - **Expected:** no rules for a token that names no indexed or working-tree path; a relayed agent message is not a user prompt to govern
  - **Actual:** a relay naming bin/fathom and a scratchpad script extracted bin/fathom as a directory (bin/fathom/__prompt__.go), the scratchpad path and an unrelated schema path, then served six rules and 178 more eligible
  - **Repro:** relay a sub-agent message that mentions bin/fathom and a scratchpad path into a session with the prompt hook installed
- **Root cause:** `resolvePromptCandidates` (cmd/fathom/prompthook.go) adds path and package tokens without checking that they exist (it assumes a prompt names repo-relative text), and a package token becomes `<dir>/__prompt__.go` even when `<dir>` is a file (`bin/fathom`) or absent; `isSystemNotification` screens harness notifications (SU-7) but not an agent relay message
- **Fix:** (1) keep a path or package candidate only when it exists in the index or the working tree at that path, else drop it; (2) skip, or cap, the hook when the prompt is an agent relay the harness marks as one; (3) serve an identical rule set at most once per session, the same dedupe as O122
- **Status:** fixed e2839dfa (EW-21) for items (1) and (2) — proven by `TestPromptCandidatesDropNonexistentPaths` (a file, a directory, `bin/fathom` as a package, a scratch path, `~/`, `../` and ghosts crowding the five slots), `TestIsAgentRelay`, `TestPromptHook_AgentRelay_SilentNoDaemonCall` and `TestPromptHook_NamesNothingReal_NoObligationsCall`; `fathom verify --strict` proves EW-21 wired from `main`. Item (3), serving an identical rule set at most once per session, is not built: an existing path in a real prompt still serves its rules on every prompt naming it
- **Impact:** six rules plus a displaced count injected into an orchestration turn from text no one typed; filed from the 2026-09-29 O122 addendum, where it was drafted as "the O123 candidate" before the sweep's records took O123 to O125

### O127 — a worker re-rooted the shared daemon's own fathom service onto its scratch clone, and every fathom answer in the orchestrating session then resolved against a deleted tree

- **Symptom:** a worker re-rooted the shared daemon's own fathom service onto its scratch clone, and every fathom answer in the orchestrating session then resolved against a deleted tree
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** ingest (MCP, remap)
  - **Expected:** a worker's scratch ingest never re-points a service whose current root is a live checkout; doctor names a service whose root no longer exists
  - **Actual:** after the push 78 deploy the fathom service root was a deleted roadmap-worker clone (<scratch>/rm/w-gl-w3); the SessionStart hook reported index state unknown
  - **Repro:** from a clone of a registered repository, call the ingest tool with path=<clone> and remap=true against the owner's daemon, then delete the clone
- **Root cause:** A worker called the `ingest` tool with its clone path and `remap: true` against the owner's daemon; nothing isolates workers from the shared daemon, and remap accepts any path.
- **Fix:** EW-8 worker isolation (FATHOM_HOME, no shared-daemon ingest from a worker); EW-19 remap guard; EW-20 doctor names a missing root. Repaired 2026-09-29: owner checkout fast-forwarded, then ingest {service: fathom, path: <owner checkout>, remap: true}: 27,589 symbols, 78,554 typed edges, 259 packages re-resolved (TypedEdgeEpoch 3), daemon RSS peak 1.25 GB against the 3 GiB shed mark.
- **Status:** fixed — EW-19 (`TestIngestRemapRefusesScratchCloneOfLiveRoot`: `remap:true` alone is refused while the recorded root is a live checkout of the same repository; `remap_from` names the root being replaced), EW-20 (`TestDoctorNamesServiceWithMissingRoot`: the `service_roots` doctor row names a deleted root with the re-point call) and PE-26 (`TestFathomHomeRelocatesLedgersAndPerFileVarsWin`: a worker's scratch run relocates `~/.fathom`). The worker-brief half (no shared-daemon ingest from a worker) is EW-8.
- **Impact:** Every fathom answer in the orchestrating session resolved against a deleted tree until repaired.

### O128 — a long ingest over /rpc answers with an empty body although the ingest succeeds

- **Symptom:** a long ingest over /rpc answers with an empty body although the ingest succeeds
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** ingest (/rpc tools/call)
  - **Expected:** a JSON-RPC result, or the queue state as /refresh-request answers
  - **Actual:** a remap ingest of a 27,589-symbol repository took 3m30s and curl received no JSON; list_services then showed the ingest had completed
  - **Repro:** curl -m 900 -X POST <daemon>/rpc with tools/call ingest {service, path, remap: true} on a cold typed pass
- **Root cause:** Probably a server write timeout shorter than a cold ingest: the answer is dropped after the work completes.
- **Fix:** EW-18: past the write budget answer with the refresh queue's state, as /refresh-request does.
- **Status:** fixed — EW-18 (`TestIngestLongRunAnswersQueueState`): past `mcp.DefaultIngestWaitBudget` (120 s, under the 150 s route write deadline; `TestIngestWaitBudgetLeavesRoomUnderTheRPCWriteTimeout`) the ingest tool answers `{service, completed:false, state, waited, note}` and the run finishes on the queue.
- **Impact:** The caller cannot tell success from failure; a retry repeats a multi-minute ingest.

### O129 — task deploy exits 201 at its healthz wait while the deploy succeeds

- **Symptom:** task deploy exits 201 at its healthz wait while the deploy succeeds
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** task deploy
  - **Expected:** the health wait covers launchd's respawn throttle and names launchd's last exit reason on a failed spawn
  - **Actual:** launchd showed spawn failed with OS_REASON_CODESIGNING and ThrottleInterval 60; the new daemon came up 61s after the old one drained; the wait is 30 tries at 1s (push 78 and push 79 deploys)
  - **Repro:** task deploy on macOS with the launchd agent installed; read launchctl print for last exit reason
- **Root cause:** launchd's ThrottleInterval 60 delays the respawn after a spawn that failed with OS_REASON_CODESIGNING; the Taskfile waits 30 tries at 1s.
- **Fix:** EW-17: `task deploy` ends on `scripts/deploy-wait.sh`, which waits throttle + exit timeout + a 30 s margin, read from `launchctl print` ("minimum runtime" is ThrottleInterval: 60 + 20 + 30 = 110 polls for the installed agent), and prints launchd's last exit reason and job state when the wait runs out or a bring-up needed more than 5 polls. It only ever runs `launchctl print`. Why the first spawn after the signed mv fails codesigning is not answered here: O132.
- **Status:** fixed — the symptom (a deploy reporting failure while the daemon came up 61 s later) is gone and proven by `scripts/deploy-wait_test.sh` against a fake curl and a fake launchctl (a daemon healthy on poll 62 passes; one that never comes up fails after 110 polls naming `last exit reason = OS_REASON_CODESIGNING` and the job state; the script runs no launchctl verb but print); `task deploy` itself was not run. The cause of the failed first spawn stays open as O132.
- **Impact:** Both deploys of 2026-09-29 reported failure although the daemon served HEAD; the last two asserts were run by hand.

### O130 — the first verify after a deploy or ingest is abandoned at the index-class deadline; the same call seconds later answers in 0.36s

- **Symptom:** the first verify after a deploy or ingest is abandoned at the index-class deadline; the same call seconds later answers in 0.36s
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** verify (daemon verify_plan)
  - **Expected:** the first call waits for, or is served from, the product-reach walk the wired check needs, or answers with a named partial
  - **Actual:** RPC error: abandoned: the call's deadline passed ... verify_plan did not finish and no partial answer is served; the retry answered 34 tasks, 1 claimed, 1 verified
  - **Repro:** task deploy (or an ingest of a large Go service), then fathom verify --service fathom --plan docs/plans/efficiency-wave.md
- **Root cause:** Probably the wired check's product-reach walk (memo group product_reach, one walk per watermark) runs cold inside the first verify_plan after the watermark moves and exceeds the index-class deadline; the walk completes and caches, so the retry is fast.
- **Fix:** warm the product-reach memo after an ingest or daemon start (it is a pure function of the watermark), or let verify_plan wait for an in-flight walk up to its own deadline; efficiency-wave row EW-22.
- **Status:** fixed (2026-09-29) — EW-22: verify_plan waits for its evidence stage no longer than its deadline less a reserve and otherwise answers with every row elided and a named partial; the builds are kept flights and the next call is served from them. Measured on a scratch store of this repository (28.5k symbols), the cold stage is the graph builds (syntax 2.7s, both 2.2s), not the walk (16ms); verify_plan no longer builds the two graphs it never read.
- **Impact:** every first verify after a deploy or commit fails; agents read it as a verify failure or retry blind.

### O132 — the first spawn of the daemon after `task deploy`'s signed mv fails with OS_REASON_CODESIGNING and launchd throttles the respawn for 60 s

- **Symptom:** the first spawn of the launchd daemon after `task deploy` replaces the installed binary fails with OS_REASON_CODESIGNING; launchd holds the respawn back by ThrottleInterval (60 s), so the daemon serves the new binary about a minute later than the deploy expects
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** task deploy, launchd (the agent's plist has ThrottleInterval 60), `/usr/bin/log show` on launchd's own messages, read-only `launchctl print`, `codesign -dvvv`
  - **Expected:** the first spawn of the freshly signed binary succeeds and the daemon is healthy within the drain time
  - **Actual:** five of five spawns after a deploy in the last three days (2026-09-28 09:50, 19:15, 21:02, 2026-09-29 01:01, 05:47, local time) failed the same way and came up 60.1 to 60.2 s later; each deploy carried a different binary hash in `~/.fathom/daemon/<label>/lifecycle.jsonl` (19a7a29339b6, 6b38f2c1c915, dac171da1212, 090f2a0c186b for the four the ledger holds). launchd's line each time: `xpcproxy exited due to OS_REASON_CODESIGNING | Launch Constraint Violation, error info: c[5]p[1]m[1]e[0], (Constraint not matched) launch type 0, failure proc [vc: 10]: ~/.local/libexec/fathom/fathom`, then `removing service since it exited with consistent failure`, then 60 s later `Successfully spawned fathom[pid] because semaphore`. The failed spawn ran 206 ms. `launchctl print gui/501/<label>` on the running job shows `properties = ... managed LWCR | has LWCR`, `LWCR = { reqs = { cdhash = { $in = [ <one entry> ] } } }` (the entry's bytes print as nothing, so the pinned hash cannot be read from launchd), `BTM uuid`, `last exit reason = OS_REASON_CODESIGNING`, `runs = 15`
  - **Repro:** task deploy on macOS with the launchd agent installed; then `/usr/bin/log show --last 10m --predicate 'process == "launchd" AND eventMessage CONTAINS "<label>"' --info --debug` (zsh: the bare `log` is a builtin)
- **Root cause:** established to the mechanism, not to the remedy. The job carries a launch constraint (a light-weight code requirement) that requires the spawned binary's cdhash to be in a list launchd holds; a re-signed binary has a new cdhash, so `kickstart -k` right after the mv is refused with "Constraint not matched", launchd removes and re-adds the service, and the re-added job (constraint re-read) spawns the same file 60 s later. Ruled out, with the reason: (1) a kernel-cached signature for the vnode: the same file, the same vnode and the same xattrs spawned fine 60 s later, and the refusal is launchd's xpcproxy applying a launch constraint, not the kernel killing a running process for an invalid page; (2) a stale `com.apple.quarantine` or `com.apple.provenance` attribute the mv carried over: the file has only `com.apple.provenance`, and AppleSystemPolicy allowed direct execs of the installed file within three seconds of each refusal (the hooks run it); (3) the signature itself: `codesign --verify --strict` reads valid, the designated requirement is satisfied, Identifier `fathom` (not `fathom.new`), Authority `fathom-dev`; amfid's "adhoc signed or signed by an unknown certificate chain" line appears on the failed and the later spawn alike, so it is not what separates them; (4) temp path then rename: the deploy already does that, and a constraint on a cdhash does not depend on how the file arrived. Not shown: that the constraint's list holds exactly the previous binary's cdhash (launchd prints the entry blank), and what re-reads it
- **Fix:** none, deliberately. Any deploy change that avoids the refusal has to re-register the job around the swap, and `launchctl bootout`/`bootstrap` are the owner's to run (a job bounced from an agent's shell has wedged before), so this is a proposal, not a step: (a) the negative control first: `task deploy` twice at one HEAD; the second binary is the same bytes, so if the cdhash is what launchd pins the second spawn succeeds at once; (b) then `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/<label>` before the mv and `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/<label>.plist` after it, replacing the kickstart, and read the log for `Successfully spawned` in seconds rather than 60; (c) lowering ThrottleInterval only shortens the penalty and weakens the crash-loop breaker's premise (the plist is the owner's config). EW-17 already removes the false failure: the deploy waits out the throttle and names launchd's reason
- **Status:** open (cause narrowed to launchd's cdhash launch constraint; remedy unproven, and unprovable without touching the owner's job)
- **Impact:** every deploy of a rebuilt daemon costs about a minute in which the fathom MCP tools and hooks fail open or error

### O133 — the wired check counts a function called only inside a func literal held in a package variable nothing invokes

- **Symptom:** the wired check counts a function called only inside a func literal held in a package variable nothing invokes
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** verify --strict (the wired criterion)
  - **Expected:** `var swapForTest = func() int { return lib.DeadInVarClosure() }`, a variable main never calls, leaves DeadInVarClosure library-only
  - **Actual:** the row verifies wired, entry `cmd/app/main.init`: the typed pass attributes every reference in a package variable's initializer, a func literal's body included, to the package's init, which runs whenever the package is linked
  - **Repro:** the EW-15 adversarial corpus's case A3 (`var swapForTest` in cmd/app/main.go): ingest it and `fathom verify --strict` a row anchored on DeadInVarClosure
- **Root cause:** a func literal in a package variable's initializer is created at init but runs only when the variable is called; the typed pass has no vertex for the variable (the Go extractor indexes exported package variables only) and resolves a call through a func-typed variable as unresolved, so there is nothing to attribute the literal's calls to but the init.
- **Fix:** give every package-level func-typed variable a vertex, attribute its initializer's func literal to it, and resolve a call through the variable to it; the product walk then reaches the literal only when reached code calls the variable. EW-15 left this case open; a row (EW-25 is reserved) should carry it.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** one of the six rows the re-audit corpus's base reading got wrong still reads wired after EW-15 (the other five read library-only): a helper kept in a package variable "so tests can swap it" reads wired whether or not the product calls the variable.

### O135 — internal/ownership has no production caller, so the CODEOWNERS matcher fixed by IM-F1 is library-only and the design's "feeds reviewers.go today" is wrong on this base

- **Symptom:** `fathom verify --strict` reads IM-F1 library-only: `matchPattern` is reached only through `Owners.For`, and nothing outside the package's own tests imports `internal/ownership`
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** fathom verify --strict --plan docs/plans/incident-maps.md --row IM-F1 on a fresh scratch ingest of the committed fix
  - **Expected:** a product entry point reaches the matcher (docs/design/incident-maps.md §7 says it "also feeds internal/review/reviewers.go today")
  - **Actual:** wired criterion unknown, "no product entry point reaches it (library-only)"; `review.Input.CodeownersMatches` and `FrequentCommitterMatches` have no producer outside tests, so `suggestReviewers` always merges two empty lists in production
  - **Repro:** grep -rn '"github.com/geoffmilleraz/fathom/internal/ownership"' --include='*.go' . finds only ownership_test.go
- **Root cause:** CX-4 built the merge and the parser as libraries and no row wired a caller; the incident-maps design read the package's doc comment as a live feed.
- **Fix:** the matcher is fixed (IM-F1 code); the row stays open until a caller lands: IM-10's owners facet, or a `review_diff` feed that reads CODEOWNERS for the changed files (which also gives CX-4's suggested reviewers real input).
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** CODEOWNERS ownership is not served anywhere today, so the matcher defect misrouted nobody in production; the receipt's suggested reviewers section is always empty.

### O137 — `search kind: docs` answers a question-shaped query with no hits because FTS5 requires every word, function words included, to appear in one section

- **Symptom:** `search kind: docs` answers a question-shaped query with no hits because FTS5 requires every word, function words included, to appear in one section
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** search (kind: docs), docindex.RankDocs over the ingested index of this repository (KR-8 wired 2026-09-29)
  - **Expected:** a question about a decision returns the sections that discuss it, as KR-M1's doc-question slice asks
  - **Actual:** "embeddings" returns 3 units; "what did we decide about embeddings" returns 0 (implicit AND over every token); "decide embeddings" returns 3
  - **Repro:** ingest this repository, then `search` with `kind: docs` and each query above
- **Root cause:** QueryDocUnits passes the query to FTS5 as written, whose implicit conjunction requires "what", "did", "we" and "about" in the same section as the topic word; a plain-text query that FTS5 rejects as syntax is now re-run as its words (also a conjunction), so the rewrite does not widen it.
- **Fix:** none yet, deliberately: whether to drop function words, or rank an OR of the words by bm25, is what KR-M1's doc-question slice measures (docs/plans/knowledge-retrieval.md); widening without that reading returns loosely related sections an agent would trust.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** An agent that asks a docs question in a sentence is told there is nothing, and falls back to grep or opens the document whole, which is the cost KR-8 was meant to remove.

### O138 — a section whose last compile kept no claim goes to the model on every compile, because the digest gate remembers a compile only through its claims

- **Symptom:** the AG-26 dry runs still send unchanged sections to the model: on fathom, 6 sections (about 2,975 estimated input tokens) whose subjects hold only superseded claims; on agentic-kit, 3 sections (about 1,217) that have never held a claim
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** fathom compile --dry-run --layer obligations, on a fresh scratch ingest of HEAD plus a read-only copy of the shared store's obligations claims and links (fathom at 0f350063; agentic-kit at 53024ff)
  - **Expected:** an unchanged section whose compile accepted no claim is gated like one whose compile accepted some
  - **Actual:** the gate sends it on every run. Examples: `ADR.md#adr-001-three-layer-architecture` (6 superseded, 0 live), `ADR.md#architecture-decision-records-fathom` (never a claim), and the kit's `CLAUDE.md#fathom-fleet-index-code-intelligence-use-it-first`, a stanza section whose drafts were rejected as over-broad (`**/*`)
  - **Repro:** compile a section whose model answer yields no accepted claim, then compile again with no change: `sections_compiled` counts it both times
- **Root cause:** the gate's only record that a subject was compiled from a digest under a prompt is the claims. A compile that accepts none (the section states no rule, or every draft is rejected) supersedes the old claims and leaves nothing to gate on, so the next run pays again. AG-26's carry has the same blind spot, because a zero-claim section has nothing to carry.
- **Fix:** record (subject, digest, prompt identity) for every compiled section, including one with no accepted claim, and gate on that record. Layer coverage should name the sections whose drafts were all rejected, so they do not read as silently uncompiled. Not done in AG-26, because it changes the store schema.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** at the dry run's haiku rates, about $0.03-0.12 per compile on fathom (under 4% of its $0.90-3.35 estimate) and $0.01-0.05 on the kit, the kit's whole remaining estimate, paid on every compile

### O139 — doctor's `stanza_loaded` passes on InstructionsLoaded beats that name a root CLAUDE.md the checkout no longer has

- **Symptom:** `fathom doctor` on the main checkout reports `stanza_loaded` PASS ("CLAUDE.md loaded by a session … 7 beat(s)") although the checkout has no CLAUDE.md, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `CLAUDE.local.md` and `stanza_present` in the same run says "no CLAUDE file"
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** fathom doctor --service fathom --path <main checkout> (read-only, 2026-09-29, after push 81 removed the root CLAUDE files under ADR-059)
  - **Expected:** a repository with no root CLAUDE file and no beat for its AGENTS.md reads as the named agents-md SKIP (AG-12): the harness has reported nothing about this stanza
  - **Actual:** PASS on 7 `instructions-loaded` beats for the root CLAUDE.md (latest 2026-09-29T00:57:48Z, before the conversion); `checkStanzaLoaded` matches a beat's `file_path` against `stanzaLoadedFiles` and never asks whether that file still exists
  - **Repro:** `fathom doctor --service fathom` in a repository whose root CLAUDE.md was deleted after a session loaded it; compare `stanza_present` (no CLAUDE file) with `stanza_loaded` (PASS)
- **Root cause:** `checkStanzaLoaded` (cmd/fathom/doctor_stanza_loaded.go) counts any beat for a listed file under the repository root, whether or not the file is still there, so a deleted CLAUDE.md's old beats outlive it in the log.
- **Fix:** none yet: count a beat only when its file exists in the checkout, or only when it is newer than the file's last change; either one turns this repository's row back into the named SKIP until a session loads AGENTS.md (which writes no beat, so the SKIP is the expected steady state).
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** the row says the harness reported loading the stanza when the only reports are for a file that is gone, so a reader takes a stale PASS for evidence; the stop-1 reading (docs/bench/readings/efficiency-wave/stops.md) says so instead of citing it.

### O140 — doctor's hook_budget reads stop-hook p99 8.6 s against its 8.0 s deadline: the hook spawned a git process per plan file and asked the daemon before it knew a plan had a claim

- **Symptom:** `fathom doctor` hook_budget WARN: stop-hook p99 over its deadline (8.6 s in `~/.fathom/hook-latency.jsonl` against 8.0 s; the brief's 8.9 s is the same reading a window earlier or later)
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `~/.fathom/hook-latency.jsonl` (read only, no per-stage field), then a scratch run of the stop hook: real `~/.fathom` beat log (62 MB), session ledger and this session's 98 MB transcript copied into a private FATHOM_HOME, a scratch daemon on a scratch ingest, a fake Stop payload, a build with stage marks
  - **Expected:** a Stop that claims nothing is cheap and its cost does not grow with the repository
  - **Actual:** 55 stop-hook rows in 24 h, p50 2.3 s, p90 4.5 s, max 8.6 s (n < 100, so the p99 is the maximum). Stages on the 89-plan repository, machine idle-ish: 89 `git show` spawns 630-820 ms (25 ms parsing), the retro 650-900 ms (whole beat-log read 440-500 ms, join 180-220 ms), the ledger row 32-125 ms, the daemon probes 1-35 ms; total 1.3-1.7 s. Load is the multiplier: the same work (app-kit, 4 plans, nothing claimed) took 677 ms at 12:17 and 3048 ms at 23:33 (4.5x), the 89-plan repository 2-4 s and 7.1 s at worst; the 8.6 s row is a codex Stop in a 4-plan repository at 09:08 (no per-plan cost to speak of): its identity probe waited the whole 5 s client timeout on a stalled daemon (ident-cache `miss` at :21.2, `miss-error` at :26.2), the indexed probe then waited until the hook guard cut it at the 8 s deadline, and 0.6 s of local ledger and retro work ran after that
  - **Repro:** `fathom stop-hook` with a Stop payload in a repository with 80+ plan files and a daemon running, timed per stage; or `git show HEAD:<plan>` in a loop against one `git cat-file --batch`
- **Root cause:** both. Work: the hook spawned one `git show` per plan file (O(plans) processes, 736 ms for 89 at load 10 against 16 ms for one `cat-file --batch`) and ran two daemon round trips (identity, indexed) before it knew any plan had gained a claim, which is almost never; and the retro ran after the deadline was spent. Load: identical work varies 4.5x with the machine's load, and a slow daemon costs the probe's full client timeout
- **Fix:** `plansWithNewClaims` scans locally first (a plan with no `[x]` is not compared; the HEAD text of the rest comes from one `git cat-file --batch` through gitro, bound to the hook's deadline) and the daemon is asked only when a plan gained a claim; `runStopHookRetro` returns at once when the hook's deadline is spent (RT-7). Pinned by counts, not a clock, in `cmd/fathom/stophook_budget_test.go`: one git spawn for 42 plans, zero daemon requests when nothing was claimed, one verify_plan for one claimed plan, no retro past a spent deadline (each red against the old flow). Before/after on the scratch harness at machine load 7.5 (10 CPUs), 7 runs each: 1587-2138 ms (median 1690) to 993-1198 ms (median 1162)
- **Status:** fixed for the two unbounded parts; the retro's whole-log read remains (O141). The session-hook channel is also over its budget in the same window (p99 4.4 s against 4.0 s, n=225): not this row
- **Impact:** a Stop hook that outlives its deadline is killed by the harness; on a loaded machine (the wave's normal state) every Stop in a repository with many plans paid 0.6-3 s for git processes that could not have found anything.

### O141 — the Stop hook's retro reads the whole beat log every turn: 440-500 ms of a ~1.1 s hook at the rotation cap

- **Symptom:** after O140 the retro is about three quarters of the Stop hook's remaining cost; `fathom retro --project . --session <id>` takes 0.83-1.2 s at load 10
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** the scratch stop-hook run of O140, per-stage marks; `fathom retro` timed alone
  - **Expected:** a Stop's local work is bounded by what changed since the last Stop, as the transcript fold is (RT-132)
  - **Actual:** `readRetroBeats` reads the whole live beat log (`beatLogRotateBytes` is 64 MB; the live file is 62 MB), splits it into strings and unmarshals every line, then `buildRetroModel` filters to the project and session (over 100 thousand of the rows are this repository's), runs two git processes (`log --since`, `status --porcelain`) and a custodian audit; the retro artifact is rewritten each turn although only the last write of a session is read
  - **Repro:** as O140
- **Root cause:** the retro was written as an on-demand verb (IR-2) and IT-13 called it from every Stop unchanged
- **Fix:** none yet. Bounded, so not urgent: the read cannot exceed the rotation cap. Options that keep the join exact: an offset-stamped fold of the session's beats like the transcript's (`transcript_offset.go`), or writing the artifact at SessionEnd/PreCompact and skipping it on a Stop. A tail read would shorten a long session's window, so it is not equivalent
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** about 0.5-0.9 s of every Stop's wall time, more under load; below the deadline by a wide margin (8 s) once O140's fix is in

### O143 — a named exploration map still ends with the session that filed it, so naming does not make it durable and the next session never hears of it

- **Symptom:** `fathom map name ID --purpose "…"` on a map filed with the default session-end condition leaves it `ended` once that session's end note exists: `map list` hides it, and the hooks that deliver maps (XM-8, XM-11 to XM-13) do not name it
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** a probe over `loadMapSet` and `explmap.Lifetimes` at the hooks branch (594cb173): `thread note --map auth …`, `map name auth --purpose …`, then a session-end note for the filing session
  - **Expected:** docs/usage.md says `name` makes a map durable, and `mapLifetime` says an unreferenced named map is permanent
  - **Actual:** the lifetime is `ended`, `completes_when` is still `session_ended` for the filing session, and `Named()` is true
  - **Repro:** file an entry with the session's id set, name the map, add a `session-end` thread row for that session, then read `loadMapSet` and `Lifetimes`
- **Root cause:** `map name` restates the header through `writeMapHeader`, which copies the first header's `completes_when` forward (`mapHeaderRow` takes `base.CompletesWhen`), and `mapLifetime` tests `Ended` before it tests `Named`
- **Fix:** none in the hooks slice, which only reads the lifetime: XM-6 and XM-7 own it. Either `map name` drops the condition (a named map is durable), or the lifetime rule tests `Named` first; design section 2 has to say which
- **Status:** fixed by XM-32 (2026-09-29): a named map is never ended (`Map.Ended`), so the lifetime rule reads it permanent and alive whether the name came before or after its condition held, and a named header is not closed. `map name` could not drop the condition: the threads fold carries a restated header's missing `completes_when` forward. Design section 2 has the dated note
- **Impact:** a map the planner names at the end of a session is gone from the next session's brief, from the pre-compact resume and from the location pointer; only a map still inside its own session is served, which is the handoff case and not the resume case

### O144 — `TestIntentTab_RefreshAfterAnUpdateReaudits` races the fixture's handler on `f.now`, so `go test -race ./cmd/fathom` fails on about one run in six

- **Symptom:** `TestIntentTab_RefreshAfterAnUpdateReaudits` races the fixture's handler on `f.now`, so `go test -race ./cmd/fathom` fails on about one run in six
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** go test -race (the gate's `task test`), cmd/fathom console tests
  - **Expected:** a test that passes on every run, as the gate's `-race` run assumes
  - **Actual:** `go test -race -count=6 -run TestIntentTab_RefreshAfterAnUpdateReaudits ./cmd/fathom/` printed one `WARNING: DATA RACE` and `--- FAIL ... race detected during execution of test`, on `wb-core` and on `wb-cli` alike (2026-09-29); the selected 579-test run failed on it twice out of two and passed when the test was run alone
  - **Repro:** the `-count=6` command above, on any checkout of this base
- **Root cause:** the test writes `f.now = f.now.Add(time.Minute)` (console_project_tabs_test.go:572) while the served handler reads `f.now` through `f.deps.now` (console_fixture_test.go:218) on a request goroutine, with no synchronisation.
- **Fix:** none yet: many console tests assign `f.now` directly, so the change is an atomic or locked clock on the fixture with a setter those tests call.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** the pre-push gate runs `go test -race`, so an unrelated push can fail on this test at random and be retried until it does not.

### O145 — the first import_resolution runs: relative-path leaves ESM `./x.js` and directory specs unresolved, namespace-dir an aliased `using` of a type

- **Symptom:** `indexbench lang-oracle -scip` with LB-F40's `import_resolution` reads zod 0 of 504 in-repo imports, express .34 (53 of 155), Polly 0 of 15 and Newtonsoft.Json 0 of 313, where axios reads 232 of 232 and nest 3,271 of 3,271
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `indexbench lang-oracle -scip` on the SCIP indexes and clones kept from round 2 (scip-typescript for zod, nest, axios, express; scip-dotnet for Polly and Newtonsoft.Json), into scratch readings (not committed)
  - **Expected:** an import whose statement the index resolves to a file of the subject resolves to that file under the dialect's strategy
  - **Actual:** zod: every spec is an ESM `./benchUtil.js` naming `benchUtil.ts`; express: `require('..')` and `require('../../')` naming the root `index.js`; C#: `using Assert = Newtonsoft.Json.Tests.XUnitAssert;` naming a type defined in `TestFixtureBase.cs`, and Polly's aliased `using Scenario = ...PolicyExtensionsAsync.ExceptionAndOrCancellationScenario;` (a nested type)
  - **Repro:** `go run ./cmd/indexbench lang-oracle -dialect typescript -subject <zod clone> -scip <typescript-zod.scip> -ctags none -out <scratch>/ts.json` and read the `import_resolution:` note
- **Root cause:** `resolveRelativePath` (internal/fusion/resolvestrategy.go) tries a spec as itself and with each suffix, so `x.js` is never tried as `x.ts`; `..` without a slash is a bare specifier (nil unless `relative`); `../../` cleans to `.`, whose index candidate `./index.js` is not a universe path (`index.js` is). namespace-dir resolves an aliased `using` only by path mirroring; the D7 declared-namespace lookup serves wildcards alone
