- **Fix:** 928ec49a (LB-F43, LB-F44): relative-path's TS/JS rule maps a JS or TS extension to its source as TypeScript's node16/bundler resolution does (`./x.js` tries x.ts, x.tsx, x.d.ts, then x.js; `.mjs` .mts; `.cjs` .cts), before the file as written for a TypeScript importer and after it for a JavaScript one, and resolves `.`, `..` and a spec ending in `/` as a directory (the repository root among them) through package.json `main` (a TypeScript importer: `types`, `typings`, `main`) before its index file, as Node does. 9de28530 (LB-F45): namespace-dir indexes the symbols by package, separator and name, so `using A = N.T;` and `using static N.T;` (a nested N.Outer.Inner too) resolve to the files declaring the type, `A.M()` binds T's member, and with the new manifest parameter `type_wildcard: "members"` (C#) `using static` brings T's members into scope; ExtractorEpoch 31
- **Status:** fixed (2026-09-29): zod 0 to 1 (504 of 504), express .3419 to 1 (155 of 155), Polly 0 to 1 (15 of 15), axios and nest unchanged at 1, Newtonsoft.Json 0 to .0415 (13 of 313; the other 300 name one type the dialect extracts without its namespace, O148), each `indexbench lang-oracle -scip` on round 2's indexes and recorded in the typescript, javascript and csharp readings. The wave-D audit corrected two rules before the push: TypeScript reads a directory's package.json `typings` before `types` (readPackageJsonTypesFields), and a `using static` member no longer binds a bare call that a type enclosing the caller declares. It also spliced zod's file-edge counts from the same run, which the fixes moved (0 to 67 of 592): TypeScript's file_edge_recall reads .037, not .0224
- **Impact:** TypeScript and JavaScript subjects written as ESM, or requiring a package root by directory, and C# code aliasing types cannot reach L3's `import_resolution` bar whatever their other metrics; the file-resolved call edges built on these imports are missing in ingest too

### O146 — clang-includes cannot see an include the compiler does not enter: a guarded re-include or an `#ifdef`-skipped one reads as resolved where the oracle names no file

- **Symptom:** the first cpp `import_resolution` run (2026-09-29, tier B) reads 218 of 218 in-repo imports, but 79 of 777 imports (fmt 15 of 422, nlohmann/json 64 of 355) are resolved by fathom where clang-includes names no file, and import edge precision reads .734 (218 of 297)
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `calibration/oracles/clang-includes` (`-I include -I test/gtest` on fmtlib/fmt 12.2.0, `-I include` on nlohmann/json v3.12.0) into `indexbench lang-oracle -dialect cpp`, scratch readings (not committed); a temporary print of each unattested statement
  - **Expected:** every `#include` a measured file writes has the file the compiler's search finds for it
  - **Actual:** `include/fmt/core.h:13` (`#include "format.h"`) sits inside `#ifdef FMT_DEPRECATED_HEAVY_CORE`, which this run does not define, so the compiler never reads it; `include/nlohmann/detail/conversions/from_json.hpp:27` (`#include <nlohmann/detail/macro_scope.hpp>`) follows an include that already entered that header, and an include guard makes `clang -E` write no line marker for a re-include
  - **Repro:** `cd calibration/oracles/clang-includes && go run . -subject <json clone> -I include -files <list> > json-imports.json`, then `indexbench lang-oracle -dialect cpp -subject <json clone> -tool-oracle json-imports.json -out <scratch>/cpp.json` and read the `import_resolution:` note's unattested count
- **Root cause:** the oracle reads the line markers `clang -E` writes when it enters a file; a directive that opens no file (a guarded re-include, a skipped conditional) leaves none. The unattested count is where fathom reads more of the source than the run's preprocessor state does, not where fathom is wrong
- **Fix:** none yet. `clang -E -dI` prints every include directive it processes, a guard-skipped re-include too (verified on from_json.hpp, where it repeats a directive whose header was already entered); pairing each printed directive with the includer's directory and the `-I` roots would resolve the guarded ones. A conditional skipped under this run's macros needs the build's own flags (a `compile_commands.json` run, the tier-A evaluation the oracle's README names as not implemented)
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** a tier-B cpp reading understates import edge precision and lists these includes as unattested; `import_resolution` is a rate over the includes the compiler entered under this run's flags, so an include the oracle cannot see is neither a hit nor a miss in it (218 of 218 is not a claim about the 79)

### O147 — `fathom map append --anchor` resolves only package-qualified symbol keys, and blames the anchor when the service or the index is what it could not reach

- **Symptom:** the wave-D worker's `map append --anchor` failed with "does not resolve in the index" for the bare name `ResolveImportSpecs` and the Go-style `fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` although `find_symbol` finds both, and right after a deploy a valid anchor failed the same way; only `internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` and `fathom::internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` worked, and both landed on the map as two entries for one symbol (O149)
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `fathom map append --anchor` (`appendMapEntry`, `ctxSymbolResolver`); reproduced on a scratch ingest of this checkout with `--store`
  - **Expected:** an anchor resolves the way `find_symbol` does (exact, then package.Name, then case-insensitive); an ambiguous name lists its candidates and files nothing; an index that is unreachable or still warming is named as such, with the retry
  - **Actual:** with `--store <scratch store of fathom>` every spelling above, and `resolveimportspecs`, read `anchor "X" does not resolve in the index`; `map append` has no `--service`, so the service is `threadScopeFor(root).service` (a 1.5 s daemon lookup, empty when it times out or the repo is not the daemon's), and the store is asked for the symbols of service "" (none). Against the daemon the same empty service ends at "names a symbol, and no index is reachable" with the cause dropped. The wave's report of the post-deploy failure was not reproduced here (no deploy from a worker)
  - **Repro:** `fathom map new r1 --purpose q; fathom map append r1 --store <scratch store of fathom> --anchor ResolveImportSpecs --why w`, then `fusion.ResolveImportSpecs`, `internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs`, `fathom::internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs`, `resolveimportspecs`
- **Root cause:** three things. The service is never named by the caller (no flag) and an unanswered lookup leaves it empty; the resolver asks `symbolnav.SymbolContext` by the bare name after `splitPointer` cuts the anchor at its last dot (so a `Type.Method` anchor is cut through its method, and an ambiguous bare name is "not found", not a list); and `cmdThreadNoteMap` drops the index-open error, so "unreachable" and "not found" read alike
- **Fix:** XM-31: `--service`, a bounded wait for a warming daemon, one resolver with `find_symbol`'s tiers (`explmap.ResolveAnchorSymbol`) that stores the canonical `dir.Name` key, an ambiguity error that lists candidates and files nothing, and an unreachable-index error that names the cause and the retry
- **Status:** fixed by XM-31 (2026-09-29): `map append` and `thread note --map` take `--service`, wait up to 8 s for a daemon that does not answer, resolve through `explmap.ResolveAnchorSymbol` (find_symbol's tiers, canonical `dir.Name` key, candidates listed on ambiguity, nothing filed) and name an unreachable index with the cause and the retry. `map show` and `map compose` take `--service` too, so the read-time re-resolution of a worker's clone needs no daemon lookup either
- **Impact:** an orchestrator's or a worker's first appends to a wave map fail or file the wrong spelling, and the error sends the caller to check an anchor that is fine

### O148 — the csharp dialect loses a file's namespace when two `#if`/`#else` method headers share one body, so a type declared there resolves by no `using` of it

- **Symptom:** after O145's fix, Newtonsoft.Json's `import_resolution` reads 13 of 313: the other 300 are `using Assert = Newtonsoft.Json.Tests.XUnitAssert;`, which stays unresolved though XUnitAssert is declared in the repository
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `indexbench lang-oracle -dialect csharp -scip <csharp-Newtonsoft.Json.scip>` at 9de28530 (scratch reading, spliced into docs/bench/readings/lang/csharp.json); a scratch test extracting each namespaced `.cs` file of both subjects; `indexbench lang-probe -dialect csharp`
  - **Expected:** XUnitAssert (Src/Newtonsoft.Json.Tests/TestFixtureBase.cs:108) carries package `Newtonsoft.Json.Tests`, the namespace declared at line 60
  - **Actual:** the file yields no CSharpNamespace symbol and every symbol in it carries the directory fallback package; it is the only one of Newtonsoft.Json's 939 namespaced files (and none of Polly's 536) to lose its namespace. The same file with one extra `}` appended parses with its namespace
  - **Repro:** extract Src/Newtonsoft.Json.Tests/TestFixtureBase.cs (Newtonsoft.Json 4e13299d) with `extractor.NewTSExtractor("csharp")`: no CSharpNamespace symbol, and XUnitAssert's Package is not `Newtonsoft.Json.Tests`; lines 279-284 are `#if DNXCORE50 / protected TestFixtureBase() / #else / [SetUp] protected void TestSetup() / #endif` followed by one body
- **Root cause:** tree-sitter-c-sharp reads both branches of a `#if`/`#else` as code, so two method headers meet one body; the braces then no longer balance for the parser (one extra `}` restores the namespace) and the `namespace_declaration` does not parse, so tags.scm's `@scope.package` never fires for the file. Not bisected further than that
- **Fix:** none yet. Options: take the file's package from a `namespace` declaration found in an ERROR-recovered tree (a query over the first `namespace` keyword and its qualified name), or read only one branch of each `#if` group before parsing, as the C# compiler does for the symbols it defines
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** every symbol of such a file is outside its namespace for namespace-dir's declared lookups (a `using` wildcard, alias or `using static` naming it resolves to nothing) and for `Package`-keyed queries; C#'s `import_resolution` reads .0854 (28 of 328), and the 300 statements naming XUnitAssert are 91% of its in-repo imports

### O149 — `fathom map append` of an anchor the map already carries files a duplicate entry, and one symbol under two spellings lands as two entries

- **Symptom:** `fathom map show wave-d --budget 1200` printed the same range twice: `internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs` and `fathom::internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs`, one span, one why, two lines
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** `fathom map show wave-d --budget 1200` (2026-09-29, the wave-D map: 4 entries from 3 anchors); `fathom map append` twice with the same anchor on a scratch map
  - **Expected:** one entry per anchor; a second why for it an annotation with its provenance (XM-4), and a repeated why nothing at all
  - **Actual:** every append filed a new entry row. Resolution merged only entries whose dedup keys were equal (a span, or the symbol string as typed, plus the content hash), so one symbol under two spellings stayed two items and two lines; `Map.Entries`, and the "N entries" every hook quotes, counted every row
  - **Repro:** `fathom map append m --anchor internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs --why w` and again with `--anchor fathom::internal/fusion.ResolveImportSpecs`, then `fathom map show m`
- **Root cause:** `appendMapEntry` never looked at what the map already carried, and `Entry.Key` used the symbol string as typed
- **Fix:** XM-33: the append reads the map's own entries by dedup key (`Map.OwnEntry`, the key ignoring a `svc::` prefix); the same why files nothing, another is a row with an `annotates` pointer that the read folds into the first entry's `Notes`, served as another annotation; the first entry's row is never touched. XM-31's canonical `dir.Name` key makes one symbol one key whatever spelling was typed
- **Status:** fixed by XM-33 (2026-09-29). Rows filed before it are not rewritten: two rows of one symbol read as one item, but `Entries` still counts both
- **Impact:** a wave map grew by every worker's re-append, the entry counts in the brief, the pointer line and the sub-agent primer overstated the map, and `map show` spent its 1,200 bytes on one range twice

### O160 — CV-M1's "pilot 8" cells are the 2026-09-21 round's guards-fathom-r2; pilots 6 and 8 have no cells on disk

- **Symptom:** CV-M1 names the raw directory guards-fathom-r2 as guards-pilot 8's cells, but the cells on disk are the 2026-09-21 round's guards-fathom-r1/r2; ADR-057, the served-not-kept design and PR-M0's brief all cite 'pilot 8' for them
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** docs/bench/readings/cv-m1-guards-pilot-8-replay-2026-09-24.md (CV-M1's cell attribution)
  - **Expected:** a replay reading names the run its cells came from, checked against the ledger
  - **Actual:** all 58 transcripts under benchroot-raw-2026-09-23/guards-fathom-r{1,2} carry claude_code_version 2.1.278 and 2026-09-21 timestamps; the ledger records guards-pilot-6 on 2.1.273 and guards-pilot-8 on 2.1.274 (2026-09-17) and has rows whose run_id is literally guards-fathom-r1 (cbfbfeb7) and guards-fathom-r2 (05448187) matching the directories cell for cell; guards-fathom-r1's i28/control/t1 is graded violated where pilot 7's borrowed cell passed, so r1 cannot be pilot 7's either
  - **Repro:** grep -o 'claude_code_version":"[^"]*' <raw>/guards-fathom-r2/*/attempt-1/session-0-single.stream.jsonl | sort -u; jq -r 'select(.run_id|test("guards-(pilot-[68]|fathom-r[12])"))|[.run_id,.harness_version,.generated_at]|@tsv' docs/bench/scenarios-ledger.jsonl | sort -u
- **Root cause:** CV-M1 inferred the run from a reading's borrowed-cell note, not from the ledger's run ids or the transcripts' harness version; pilot 6 ran at a023dc7, before 7150215e (O77) persisted cell diffs under raw, and pilot 8's raw directory was not kept in this root.
- **Fix:** PR-M0 (`docs/bench/readings/served-not-kept/pr-m0-replay.md`) replays every guards cell on disk under its own run id and grades it against that run's ledger rows; CV-M1's measurements stand, its run label does not. Not yet corrected: the "pilot 8" wording in CV-M1, ADR-057's context and `docs/design/served-not-kept.md` section 2.
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** the i28 evidence behind ADR-057 is real but belongs to guards-fathom-r2; a reader checking it against pilot 8's ledger rows or reading finds a different i28/control grade and cannot reconcile them.

### O161 — PR-9's retired-pin exemption passes 3 of the 5 i28 violations: the agent rewrote I28's row to name its new test, once with no decision record

- **Symptom:** in the PR-M0 replay, 3 of the 5 oracle-violating i28 cells also rewrote invariant I28's row to name the new test, so PR-9's rule reads them as a retired pin (printed, exit 0) and blocks only 2 of 5; one of the 3 (guards-fathom-r1 i28 control t1) and the unscored r1 i28 control t2 did it with no decision record in the range
- **Evidence:**
  - **Tool:** PR-9's accepted rule (fathom conserve --strict-plane integrity, ADR-057 PR-8 decision)
  - **Expected:** a pre-push block on a lost pin that the governing rule still requires; a retirement exempted only when a decision says so (K4)
  - **Actual:** r1 i28 control t1: invariants.md I28 row rewritten, no ADR.md change; r2 i28 control t2 and r2 i28 full t1: I28 row rewritten plus an ADR-025 amendment in the same diff; r1 i28 full t2 and r2 i28 full t2: source untouched, still named at head, so PR-9 blocks them
  - **Repro:** docs/bench/readings/served-not-kept/pr-m0-method: ./replay.sh then ./summarize.py; read the pin view column of part-a.tsv for the i28 rows
- **Root cause:** by design (ADR-057 PR-8 decision): a loss whose naming source was edited in the range and no longer names the test at head is a retirement, printed for review. These agents predate any pin line and were not gaming; they rewrote the rule to match the behavior they had just changed, which is the case K4 and ADR-057's third revisit clause name.
- **Fix:** none in PR-9 (built as accepted). The owner's call, per K4: whether the pre-push exemption should require a decision cite in the range (an ADR.md change would have exempted 2 of the 3; the plan files the agents also wrote would not count).
- **Status:** open
- **Impact:** on this evidence the pre-push block stops 2 of 5 i28 violations; the pin line (PR-3) still names all 5, adding "its source was edited in this commit" for the 3.
